# Changelog

The versions of LibreELEC Hades. Before 1.0 RC1 there were only private test builds.

Patch numbers are those of the version in whose section they appear. In 1.0 the patches of Hades were numbered again in the order in which they apply, so a number in an older section may name another patch today.

## 1.2.1

A short version after 1.2: a 1D LUT for the calibration of a display, the frame of the video saved alone, HDMI 3D frame packing on NVIDIA graphics, the remote waking the PC from sleep, Dolby Vision in IPT with much less work for the graphics, two fixes for HDR and Dolby Vision in 3D, a check of the Dolby Vision output that says more on older Radeon cards, and fixes for AV receivers and for audio outputs that come and go.

### What's new for you

**Picture**

- **A 1D LUT of the display.** Next to 3D LUTs, the display calibration takes a 1D LUT: the `.cal` of ArgyllCMS and DisplayCAL (the curves that a calibration loads into the video card) and a `.cube` of `LUT_1D_SIZE`, alone or with a 3D LUT as Resolve's shaper. It is applied at its own resolution, in the bridge and in Kodi's own shaders, and fits an SDR output of any gamut. `kodi.log` says what a LUT holds.
- **The pictures of the display's LUT dithered.** Where Kodi draws a video with the LUT into an 8-bit window, the picture is now dithered, as Hades already dithers the pictures it maps, so a slow gradient keeps no bands from the LUT. Pictures without a LUT, and windows of more than 8 bits, are drawn as before.
- **Dolby Vision sent in IPT takes much less work** (the expert transport *As licensed players send (IPT)*). Each pixel is now converted once, where the left pixel of each pair was converted twice, and the PQ curves are read from tables made in double precision instead of being worked out with exponentials and logarithms for every pixel. On the software renderer the conversion takes half the time, and the picture comes closer to the exact conversion than before: one step of 4096 off in under 0.01 % of the samples, against up to 0.04 % before. After a report by MrMagic.
- **The frame of the video alone** (issue #30). Shift+Print Screen, or *Save the frame of the video* in the video settings of the player, saves the frame of the film without menus, the player information or subtitles, at the video's own size wherever it can be drawn so, as `videoNNNNN.png` in the screenshot folder, with an SDR copy beside it when it is HDR.

**3D**

- **HDMI 3D frame packing on NVIDIA** (issue #33). Where NVIDIA's driver refuses the picture buffer of a frame-packed mode, Hades now draws the two eyes into buffers of its own that the display engine can show, and the 3D mode goes out in frame packing instead of falling back to top and bottom or side by side; `kodi.log` says which buffer was used.
- **3D subtitles no longer leave old menus and subtitles in the right eye** (issue #34). With an HDR video in frame packing, top and bottom or side by side, the right eye showed the menus from before the video and earlier subtitles over the picture whenever a subtitle was on screen. The layer of the menus and subtitles is now cleared for both eyes, so each eye shows only the current subtitle, at its depth. Found by cinemaONE.
- **A Dolby Vision 3D film keeps its colours after going back to 2D** (issue #34). Where the processor cannot keep up with both views and the video goes on in 2D, a profile 20 film turned green and purple from that point; it is now rebuilt exactly as when it plays in 2D from the start. Found by cinemaONE.

**Graphics and connections**

- **A DisplayPort link that the kernel reports as failed is set up again, also behind the HDMI port of a PC that feeds it from DisplayPort,** with the same picture, as the kernel asks of the program that drives the display. Found by CroqueMr's build.
- **AV receivers that lose the HDMI link again while they settle** (issue #28). When a receiver such as the Anthem AVM 90 loses the setup of the link once more a few seconds after Hades set it up again, the repair now also keeps the port off for `i915.hdmi_relink_off_ms` first, as switching the output off and on does, and the link is checked once a second for 5 s after each such set up. Found and analysed by corgeone.

**Sound**

- **The audio outputs listed once for a burst of device changes** (a Bluetooth output that comes or goes), instead of once per event, and the events that the audio engine takes out of its queue no longer leak (Kodi pull request 29331, by sopparus).

**Sleep**

- **The remote wakes the PC from sleep** (issue #32). Linux lets only some keyboards wake a PC, so the receiver of a remote control could not, and a PC that does not wake looks hung. The receivers of remote controls (a flirc, a Media Center receiver, the USB receiver of a remote with media keys) may now wake it by default, and *Settings > LibreELEC > System > Sleep > Wake from sleep* chooses remotes, remotes and keyboards, or only the power button. Mice and game controllers never wake it, a change applies at once, and the kernel log names each device that may wake the PC, and a receiver that was not made to wake one.

**The diagnostics**

- **Check the Dolby Vision output says more on Radeon RX 400, RX 500 and Vega cards** (issue #20). On these cards the card's own check is taken inside the display pipe, before the stage that brings each colour back to 8 bits. The result now also says whether a change it finds fits only a widening of the colours inside the pipe, with the 8 bits kept, or a change of the colours themselves. An output whose check is not exact still plays Dolby Vision as HDR10.
- **The diagnostic report and the kernel log say, around each sleep, whether the firmware offers suspend to RAM or a low-power idle, how long the PC slept, what woke it, and which keyboards or remotes could wake it but are not allowed to** (issue #32).

**Up to date**

- Kodi's Piers branch up to `e1f5bbb647`: its one change since 1.2, `cf719c653e`, is of Kodi's continuous integration and changes nothing in the image. LibreELEC master up to `c301806e95fc`: add-ons, other devices, and NVIDIA's driver 580.196.11, which Hades already has for the GeForce GTX 900 and 10 series.

### Details

- The Kodi changes of this version are `kodi-9350` to `kodi-9353` (the 1D LUT in the bridge, in Kodi's shaders and in the settings, and the dither of the LUT's pictures), `kodi-9370` to `kodi-9372` (buffers of Kodi's own of 8 bits and linear, a test commit that asks nvidia-drm whether it takes a buffer in a frame-packed mode, and frame packing through those buffers where it refuses the window's), `kodi-9400` and `kodi-9401` (the frame of the video at its own size, and Shift+Print Screen, `TakeScreenshot(video)` and the button of the video settings that save it), `kodi-9410` and `kodi-9411` (the GUI's layer of the HDR composite cleared for both eyes, and a 3D video gone back to 2D rebuilt by the Dolby Vision bridge as a 2D one), `kodi-9420` to `kodi-9422` (a link that the kernel marks Bad), `kodi-9430` and `kodi-9431` (Kodi pull request 29331, taken before it reaches Piers), `kodi-9440` (Piers), and `kodi-9450` and `kodi-9451` (the IPT transport converted in one compute pass, each pixel once, and PQ read from tables made in double precision).
- `x86/linux-9887`: a repair of the link within 5 s of a set up with the port kept off keeps the port off as well, and the SCDC looks go on for those 5 s (`i915.hdmi_relink_off_ms`).
- `hades-wake`, a new package: `wake.sh` sets the `power/wakeup` of each USB input device by the setting *Wake from sleep* (`LibreELEC-settings-9093`), from udev (`70-hades-wake.rules`), after each sleep and when the setting changes, and writes `hades-wake:` lines to the kernel log.
- `hades-diag` 29: `hades-output-check` shows two more sets of test pictures, whose values (multiples of 0x11 and of 0x55) every widening keeps linear, and writes "what the change fits" to its report and to the kernel log; `sleep.sh`, which systemd-sleep runs before and after each sleep (`/usr/lib/systemd/system-sleep/hades-sleep`), writes `hades-sleep:` lines to the kernel log, with the setting of *Wake from sleep* and why each input device may wake the PC or not.
- The update channel: 1.2.1 is 1020199.
- `tools/regress` gained the tests of each change, each proven by a mutant that must make it fail.

### Credits

- Thank you to SunnySnacks for the new report of issue #20, to NTM-3D and Khazar321 for the logs of issue #33, to cinemaONE for issue #34, to corgeone for issue #28, to Mibix for issue #32, to MrMagic, whose report made the IPT transport lighter, and to MrPhenix007, who asked for the frame of the video in issue #30.

## 1.2

More for the picture and for 3D, and fixes for what was reported after 1.1. 3D videos in MV-HEVC, such as the spatial video of an iPhone, play in 3D, and subtitles and menus over a 3D video sit in front of the picture. The player information names the settings of yours that change the picture from the film, and *Check this setup* compares Hades with licensed players on your own TV. Kodi draws the colours of its videos more truly, subtitles can go into the black bars, and HDR mapped to SDR can go out in BT.2020 or DCI-P3. A TV with an automatic Filmmaker Mode switches to it for films by itself, a home-cinema projector can be controlled over the network (its lens memory by the shape of the picture, its picture mode by the signal), and a 3D LUT can calibrate each display. The settings of the display are laid out by what goes out, and a few questions at the first start set the picture for your display. Hades now finds its own new versions, signed, and installs them after your yes. It also brings Linux 7.4-rc4, FFmpeg 9.1 and the latest NVIDIA drivers. Every change was tested before release, with the code that the image runs on the host or in a virtual machine, and on my PC where it has the hardware; [What has been tested](docs/tested.md) says what has been seen working, and on which hardware.

### What's new for you

**The player information**

- **It says when your settings change the picture from the film.** While an HDR video plays, the first page names the settings that make the picture differ from what the film carries (trims left out, content mapping v4.0 added, a peak set by hand, another transport, tone mapping by Hades and the like), with a button that puts exactly those back to their defaults after a question. `kodi.log` says them for each video, and a diagnostic report shows them at its top.
- **Low-Latency is no longer shown as TV-led** (issue #25). In native Dolby Vision Low-Latency, the Output and Signal rows said "Native, TV-led" and "Dolby Vision (TV-led)"; they now say "Native, Low-Latency" and "DV Low-Latency". The rows "Sent to the TV" and "Blocks sent" stay hidden in Low-Latency, which carries no metadata levels in the picture.
- **Frame packing is named, with its real picture size** (issue #33). In frame packing the mode reads "1920x2205 @ 23.976 Hz, 3D frame packing"; a half 3D layout says so; and where the driver did not take frame packing and the eyes go out one above the other in an ordinary mode, it says "3D not told" and, on the output page, to set the display to 3D by hand. Not tried on a 3D display.
- **Hold Menu to open it.** While a video plays, a long press of Menu opens the player information, as O and the red key do, for remotes without colour keys such as a Fire TV remote over Bluetooth, and for TV remotes over HDMI-CEC whose menu key is held long enough. A short press of Menu still opens the on-screen controls.
- **The player information costs less GPU time while it is open.** It is processed at most four times a second while nothing is asked of it and nothing of it moves, so where its picture is composited from the frame before (native Dolby Vision, HDR10 and HLG) the interface is no longer drawn again in almost every frame. It shows the same values, each at most a quarter of a second later. While a control of it animates (a skin's own panel that fades a row in or out, a focus), it is processed in every frame, so that the animation runs as before.
- **The GPU time of each pass, on any graphics, without a setting.** One frame in eight is timed, and the System page and `kodi.log`, so a diagnostic report too, say what each pass costs: those of native Dolby Vision, and of the pictures converted, adapted to the display or upscaled. `<video><dvpasstimes>` in `advancedsettings.xml` now times every frame.
- **It says when the display's data come through a sound bar or AV receiver.** The System page shows the connection ("Direct" or "Via sound bar/receiver") and the sound formats of the EDID; ALLM, QMS and FreeSync share one row. Through such a device the Dolby Vision block, the sound formats and the HDMI 2.1 link are that device's (an LG C5 behind a sound bar: Dolby Vision version 1 instead of 2, DTS, FRL at 40 Gb/s). `kodi.log` and the diagnostic report say it too.
- **The overview says when frames are lost, and why.** It no longer says that everything is as intended while Kodi drops or skips frames all along. Where frames are lost steadily (at least 6 in the last 30 seconds, in two of its parts of 10 seconds), the overview says how many, names the graphics where they are busy, or says that the video was buffering because the network or the source cannot keep up. A start, a seek, a pause or a change of refresh rate loses a few frames by design and never changes it, and the rows of dropped and skipped frames stay as they were. *Check this setup* says it too.
- **The content type that the TV is told** (Video page, Video output): Cinema, Game, or what the graphics driver says.
- **The use of an AMD GPU is the same measure on every AMD GPU:** the time its graphics engine spent on the work of Kodi, from the driver's accounting of Kodi's own files, instead of a firmware figure that Ryzen processors of different generations compute differently (a Ryzen 5 PRO 3400GE showed 100 % where a 4650GE showed about 50 %).

**Updates**

- **Hades tells you about a new version, and installs it after your yes.** Every six hours it looks on GitHub for a newer version of LibreELEC Hades and tells you ("New version: Settings > LibreELEC > Updates"); *Available Versions* shows it, asks, downloads it and restarts to install it. "Manual" stays the default; "auto" downloads by itself and installs at the next start, as in LibreELEC. A version is taken only when its manifest is signed by one of the two keys inside the image, is current (each manifest lasts 365 days), names a version newer than yours for this kind of PC, and its file arrives over HTTPS from GitHub, whole and with the signed SHA-256; anything else is refused and nothing of it is kept ([how](docs/how-it-works.md#updates-from-the-system-itself)). Looking sends nothing about the system: no identifier, no hardware, no version. With "manual" and the notifications off, it does not look until you ask. Before it keeps a downloaded version, Hades removes any other update left in the update folder, so that the next start installs exactly the signed version, and it reads the file back to check that it was written as signed. 1.0 and 1.1 have no channel, so the update to 1.2 is made with the installer or the `.tar` file. Tested in a virtual machine from one version to the next.

**Picture**

- **Films are said to be films: Filmmaker Mode by itself.** While a video plays, LibreELEC Hades tells the TV the HDMI content type Cinema, and Game for games, where the TV declares it, so a TV with Filmmaker Mode Auto Start (LG) switches to Filmmaker Mode by itself and back for the menus. NVIDIA and AMD graphics used to say "computer graphics" for everything. It changes only with a display mode change that happens anyway, never with a black screen of its own, and not a byte of the picture changes. *System > Display > Content type (Filmmaker Mode)*, Expert: Automatic (the default) or As LibreELEC.
- **A 3D LUT of the display** (`.cube`, `.3dl` or madVR's `.3dlut`), one for SDR and one for HDR for each display, applied as the last step of the colour of the video: the LUT for SDR on SDR videos (drawn by Kodi or by Hades) and on HDR and Dolby Vision converted to SDR; the LUT for HDR on HDR10 adapted to the display, Dolby Vision as HDR10 mapped to it, and Dolby Vision Low-Latency. Never on native Dolby Vision that the TV maps, nor on HDR that the display maps itself; the menus and subtitles are not corrected. A LUT is read in the background, so that even a large one (madVR's, of 256 points a side) never holds a frame up: until it is ready the picture goes without it, and the player information says "loading". Off by default (*System > Display > 3D LUT (display calibration)*, Expert); the player information says the LUT in use, or why it is not applied. Not tried on a calibrated display.
- **Projector control over the network** (*System > Display*, off by default). Hades selects the lens memory of a home-cinema projector by the shape of the picture, for a wide screen with constant image height (up to three memories with your thresholds; the shape from the Dolby Vision level 5 of the video, or measured on the picture as subtitles in the black bars do), and its picture mode by the kind of signal (SDR, HDR10, HLG, Dolby Vision, 3D). JVC D-ILA, Sony (ADCP and SDCP) and Epson (ESC/VP.net). Hades connects only to the IP address you give and never listens; the password is kept like the web server's and never written to the log. *Test the connection* shows the model or the answer, with a progress that can be cancelled while a projector that is moving its lens has not answered yet, and the player information shows what was selected. While the control is off it costs nothing: it waits until it is turned on. Tested against a simulator of each protocol; not tried on a projector yet.
- **Truer colour edges in every video that Kodi draws itself** (SDR, HDR10 and HLG drawn by Kodi, with software decoding, VAAPI or NVIDIA). The colour samples are now taken where the video places them (Kodi took them a quarter of a sample off on almost every video) and with Lanczos 3 instead of bilinear. Measured against 4:4:4 masters, the colour error goes down by 13 % (animation) to 26 % (film), on average and at its worst. On by default; where the graphics cannot keep up, it steps down by itself to a lighter filter and says so in the log, by the GPU time of the pictures, or by the refreshes they miss on graphics that have no GPU timer.
- **Upscalers: AiUpscale for film and for animation**, two more choices of the upscaler settings (Expert, off by default). Measured closer to the original than FSRCNNX 16 on animation and on SDR film, at about two thirds of its cost, and a little less close on HDR10.
- **Smoother gradients in 8-bit output.** Where Hades maps or upscales a picture into an 8-bit picture (an SDR screen, a laptop panel), it now dithers with blue noise instead of rounding: the steps of skies and fades go away (the measured error of the steps goes down by 45 % on film and 92 % on gradients) for a fine noise of less than half a level. Never on native Dolby Vision, nor on a 10-bit output.
- **Subtitles in the black bars** (*Settings > Player > Subtitles*, off). For films wider than the screen, such as 2.39:1 films whose bars are inside the 16:9 picture (most UHD remuxes), subtitles go into the bar under the picture, so that they never cover it. Hades finds the bar from the Dolby Vision active area where a video has one, and otherwise by measuring the picture twice a second on the graphics: only a bright scene can widen it, so dark scenes and fades never move the subtitles, and an IMAX scene that opens up brings them back at once. Two lines must fit at your subtitle size (38 or less for a 2.39:1 film on a 16:9 screen). Text and Blu-ray picture subtitles alike.
- **Colour range** (*System > Display*): Kodi's setting with three values, Auto, Full (0-255) and Limited (16-235), replaces "Use limited colour range". Hades asks the graphics driver for the range, so that the picture is compressed once; native Dolby Vision is always full range. A saved "Use limited colour range" becomes Limited, once. The menus composited over HDR follow a change of the range at once.
- **HDR shown as SDR in BT.2020 or DCI-P3** (*HDR to SDR: gamut of the output*, *System > Display*, Expert; BT.709 by default, which changes nothing). For a projector or a TV that takes SDR in a wider gamut, Dolby Vision, HDR10, HDR10+ and HLG mapped to SDR go out in BT.2020 or DCI-P3 (D65): the video is mapped into that gamut, not mapped to BT.709 and then put in it, the display is told so, and the menus and subtitles are converted to match. Only where the display's EDID declares that gamut and the graphics driver can signal it (AMD's driver offers no DCI-P3); elsewhere BT.709 (and in 3D where the eyes are put into one picture: anaglyph, interlaced, checkerboard), and `kodi.log` says why. Asked for by JonnyVee. Checked on the software bridge.
- **The spline tone curve** (the expert choice "Spline" of *Tone mapping curve*, from libplacebo) darkens the shadows and middle tones less and keeps highlights brighter, for a little less gradation at the very top: measured on 100 cases (HDR10 ramps, film pictures and a chart, to SDR and HDR displays), it comes closer to the master and to BT.2390 in 97. BT.2390, the default, and every Dolby Vision path draw the same bytes as before.
- **An SDR copy of every HDR screenshot** (issue #30). A screenshot taken while the output is HDR is still saved as the display gets it (PQ or HLG, tagged), and next to it, as `screenshotNNNNN-sdr.png`, a copy tone mapped to SDR, which any picture viewer shows right. It is mapped from the peak of the picture (its MaxCLL or its mastering display, 1000 nits where neither is known), which also makes the thumbnails of bookmarks taken in HDR less dark. A screenshot of HDR over the tunnel of an HLG video is now tagged HLG, not PQ.
- **SDR in BT.2020 after an HDR10 video tells the display SDR.** With *Keep the wide colours of SDR videos (BT.2020)* on, an SDR video after an HDR10 one kept the gamut, and a display could stay in PQ; it now gets an HDR InfoFrame that says SDR while the picture goes out in BT.2020 (Intel and AMD; NVIDIA's driver does it itself). Found by CroqueMr's build.
- **AMD: HDR turned on or off at the same mode reaches the display.** When HDR10 or HLG started or ended without a change of the timing, the depth or the format, the display kept the HDR signalling of the video before, as it did where a new stream kept the pipe of the old one. Found by CroqueMr's build.
- **The last pictures of a video are shown.** At the end of a file without sound, or whose picture outlasts its sound, the last fraction of a second was dropped. Found by CroqueMr's build.
- **A video paused near its end keeps its last pictures.** Where the picture had to wait for the display (paused in the last half second of a file, or a change of stream while paused), the pictures the decoder still held were thrown away unseen, and the player did not answer for up to half a second for each. A fault of Kodi itself. Found by CroqueMr's build.
- **Blu-ray Java menus get the screen configuration they ask for.** A menu that asked for 1920x1080 as a preference could be drawn at 960x540: libbluray took the last configuration it could accept instead of the best one, and ignored the preferences of the disc. A fault of libbluray itself; the 3D menus keep their mode. Found by CroqueMr's build.

**Dolby Vision**

- **Compare Hades with licensed players on your TV** (*Check this setup > Dolby Vision: Hades or licensed players (A/B)*). A guided comparison of native Dolby Vision as Hades sends it and as licensed players send it, with a test clip made for it (a dark scene with a lift of the shadows, near-black squares and black bars), which it can also copy to a USB drive to play with the TV's own player first. Your answer is kept with the name of your TV for the diagnostic report; nothing changes unless you say so. After measurements by fandangos.
- **The form of the metadata as licensed set-top boxes send it, as expert choices** (*System > Display > Dolby Vision engine*). Hades keeps sending what the TV's own player gives the TV. To compare on a display, each thing that a licensed set-top box does differently can be chosen: the active area (Hades' own, Hades' own without one made where the video has none, as in the video, or none), the metadata blocks in the order of their level, level 254 with version 0, a neutral level 3 for content mapping v2.9, and BT.2020 in the AVI InfoFrame (Intel, AMD and NVIDIA). Nothing changes by default, and `kodi.log` says the blocks sent with the first picture of each video. Read on an Amlogic player with Dolby's own core, and after the findings of fandangos.
- **"As licensed players send (IPT)" sends exactly what a licensed core sends.** Its matrices are those of a licensed core byte for byte (they were those of the profile 5 videos, a code apart in four coefficients), and profile 5 goes out in them too.
- **Dolby Vision shown as HDR10 or SDR keeps up on small graphics.** The measured fidelity step-down of native Dolby Vision now also applies where Dolby Vision is rebuilt for a display without it: only where the graphics are measured not to keep up (their GPU time and missed refreshes both say so), one level at a time, with the enhancement layer always drawn. Graphics that keep up show the exact picture, byte for byte as before. On the software renderer, the last level of a profile 7 FEL picture takes 46 % less time rebuilt as HDR10 and 29 % less mapped to SDR. Found with the report of JonnyVee on a Ryzen 5 PRO 3400GE. Not tried on such graphics yet.
- **Dolby Vision as SDR is easier to find.** The help of *Play HDR videos as SDR*, *Show Dolby Vision as HDR10 when the display cannot* and *Native Dolby Vision output*, and the overview of the player information when Dolby Vision goes out as HDR10 because of the display, say where to choose "As SDR (without HDR)" for a video. Asked about by JonnyVee.
- **HLG as Dolby Vision also takes 8-bit HLG videos** (from cameras and some broadcasts): each picture is given to the conversion as the same picture in 10 bits, so the result is exactly that of 10-bit HLG. After an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- **`kodi.log` says why an HLG video stays HLG** where *HLG as Dolby Vision* would send it but cannot take it: 4:2:2 chroma, a depth other than 8 or 10 bits, full range or interlaced.
- **After an update, the first Dolby Vision video no longer stutters on AMD integrated graphics.** It starts from the way of drawing and the levels of the fidelity step-down that the build before found with the same graphics, and measures again from there. From the update after this version on; a new installation still measures first.
- **The scene refreshed in every frame, for a comparison** (expert, off): `<video><dvscenerefreshall>true</dvscenerefreshall></video>` in `advancedsettings.xml` sends every frame of native Dolby Vision as the first picture of a scene, whatever the RPU says. Off, the flag of the RPU goes out, as a licensed player sends it. Asked for by a comparison of brightness on a Sony A80K (lvasan).

**3D**

- **How 3D video goes out, a choice for 3D TVs and projectors** (*System > Display > Stereoscopic 3D > How 3D video goes out*, Expert): Automatic (the default: as the same video in 2D, HDR where the display takes it, SDR mapped as in 2D elsewhere), HDR, SDR in BT.2020 or SDR in BT.709, for every HDR 3D video, to try what a display takes. What is chosen goes out only where the display declares it and the driver can send it; the player information and `kodi.log` say what went out and why. Not tried on a 3D display.
- **Dolby Vision 3D films (profile 20) in 3D:** both views are decoded and each is rebuilt as HDR10 or mapped to SDR, with the same picture as in 2D; before, they played one view in 2D. Checked with a profile 20 file made from an ITU-T conformance stream; not tried on a 3D display.
- **The Dolby Vision bridge draws 3D a view at a time:** each eye of a 3D picture (side by side, top and bottom, frame packing) is rebuilt or mapped exactly as the same picture in 2D, value for value on the software renderer. So an HDR10 or HLG 3D video mapped to SDR is now mapped as in 2D, by the reference method (BT.2390) by default, where Kodi's own tone mapping drew it before. Not tried on a 3D display.
- **HDR shown as SDR in BT.2020 or DCI-P3 in 3D too:** *HDR to SDR: gamut of the output* (and *Keep the wide colours of SDR videos*) now apply in side by side, top and bottom and frame packing; only anaglyph, interlaced and checkerboard stay BT.709. Not tried on a 3D display.
- **3D in MV-HEVC:** both views of a 3D video in MV-HEVC (spatial video of an iPhone or a Vision Pro, 3D films made for the Vision Pro, in MP4, MOV or Matroska) are decoded and go out in 3D as 3D Blu-ray video does: by the card's video decoder (NVDEC) on NVIDIA graphics with driver 620 (Turing and later), and by the processor otherwise. A Matroska file whose codec data lost the second view's parameter sets still plays in 3D where its packets carry them. `kodi.log` says each step. Checked with the ITU-T MV-HEVC conformance streams and files made from them; not seen on a 3D display.
- **Apple's spatial video at its intended depth:** its disparity adjustment is applied, half to each eye, so that far things sit behind the screen as on the Vision Pro. Not tried with a real spatial video.
- **3D back to 2D where the processor cannot keep up** (MVC and MV-HEVC): after two windows of four seconds in a row with late pictures, the video goes on in 2D, with hardware decoding where there is, and the player information says so.
- **Faster 3D pictures:** the picture of both eyes is made in reused memory, 3.8 ms instead of 28.6 ms at 3840x2160 per eye on a Ryzen 9 7950X3D.
- **Dolby Vision profile 20 with correct colours:** its base layer (IPT-PQ-c2) is rebuilt and sent as HDR10 or mapped to SDR; before, it was shown as it is, in green and purple. It never goes out natively; a 3D film in profile 20 plays in 3D (below). Not tried with a real profile 20 file. Thanks to ht2tweak's Full SBS 3D Converter.
- **Subtitles in front of the picture in 3D without depth data.** For MV-HEVC, side by side and top and bottom 3D, and 3D Blu-ray video without plane offsets, Hades measures the depth of the nearest thing in the picture where the subtitles are, on the graphics, and places each subtitle just in front of it: it never jumps while shown, only glides forward, and never comes nearer than a comfortable limit. The menus and the player information over a 3D video open in front of the picture and stay put while open. *Player > Subtitles > Depth of subtitles and menus over 3D video* (Automatic, or Fixed as in Kodi). A disc's own offsets always come first, and 3D Blu-ray subtitles that follow a sequence the title does not carry are now placed by the measured depth. Not seen on a 3D display.
- **Subtitles over a 3D video in HDR10 and HLG are placed in front of the picture too;** they took the fixed depth there. On pictures of known depth in PQ and HLG, at full and at a quarter of the light, they are placed where their SDR copies are. Not seen on a 3D display.
- **HDR video in 3D in both eyes.** An HDR10 or HLG video in frame packing, side by side or top and bottom was drawn whole into the left eye, with the menus and subtitles squeezed into it too. Each eye now shows its own half or view, with menus and subtitles over the whole frame. SDR 3D and 2D video are drawn exactly as before. Not tried on a 3D display.
- **3D Blu-ray in MKV: the second view is found in every form a file declares it** (issue #33). A Matroska file whose video declares its second view by a block addition mapping (the form the Matroska specifications prefer) or only in its stream (after a remux) played in 2D. `kodi.log` says which form a file uses.
- **Check this setup has a frame packing card for 3D TVs and projectors.** Where the display says it takes 3D, a 15-second 3D clip at 1080p and 23.976 Hz goes out in frame packing, and the card says whether the driver took it, or what it went out as and why (issue #33). It is skipped on displays without 3D.
- **Files named `.3D.FSBS.` or `.3D.FTAB.` play in 3D,** and a full side by side or top and bottom frame sized as a whole is shown at the shape of one eye (Portisch).
- **NVIDIA: frame packing refused.** Every reason why the driver refuses HDMI frame packing goes to the kernel log, and `kodi.log` names the buffer it was given (issue #33); a frame-packed mode draws into an 8-bit window, and a mode with a 3D layout takes away a vendor InfoFrame that another output left. Where frame packing is refused, the display goes straight to side by side, instead of top and bottom for a few seconds first.

**Sound**

- **Multichannel PCM with heights behind** (5.1.2 and up, decoded by FFmpeg) keeps its back and top back channels apart: the back ones were counted twice and the heights lost. Found by SamuriHL.
- **Passthrough:** a damaged AC-3, E-AC-3 or TrueHD frame too large for its burst is dropped instead of overrunning the buffer, and a false DTS header with no channel arrangement is ignored. Found by SamuriHL.
- **Passthrough sound that starts late after a seek is brought back in sync by the audio engine,** as in Kodi's `Piers` branch, also when such a stream resumes from a pause.

**HDMI-CEC**

- **The TV remote works as soon as CEC is connected** (issue #24). Kodi ignored the keys of the TV remote until the TV answered that it was on, which took 39 to 130 seconds after a start or a wake on some TVs. They are now taken as soon as the connection to the CEC adapter is open. The TV's standby command, its menu language and the receiver's audio state still wait for the TV, so a TV that is off while the PC starts does not put it to sleep.
- **The CEC settings say when the TV remote cannot reach Kodi** (issue #24). Where the CEC adapter is on another output than the picture (an AV receiver on the board's HDMI while the picture comes from a graphics card), its settings begin with "TV remote: not through this adapter", which says which output each one is on and how to get the remote working.
- **An adapter whose line answers nothing no longer holds Kodi up** (issue #24). On a DisplayPort output whose converter passes no CEC, libCEC waited for minutes, and sleep and shutdown waited for it. Kodi now asks the line first, twice in a row before libCEC is ever asked to open it, leaves such a line alone, says so in `kodi.log`, and asks again every 30 seconds.
- **`kodi.log` says why the PC went off, or stayed on.** A standby command of the TV is logged with the setting *When the TV is switched off* and what it made Kodi do (Ignore too), and every power off, suspend, reboot or quit says where it came from: HDMI-CEC, a power menu or a key, an add-on, JSON-RPC or the idle timer. A power off held back for a recording says so too.

**Settings**

- **Your display, at the first start and in Check this setup.** Hades sets the picture from what the display declares; *Your display* asks only what no display declares: whether it is a projector and how bright (then it sets the mapping for it, by whether it takes Dolby Vision, HDR10 or SDR only), and whether HDR videos go out as HDR, HLG as SDR, or all as SDR. It also says what Hades found: the display, what it takes, whether a sound bar or receiver is in between, and whether the TV remote reaches the PC over HDMI-CEC. Going on without an answer changes nothing, and a later answer puts back what an earlier one changed. Not tried on a projector.
- **Share my results.** One line with the PC, its graphics and driver, the display and the results of the last *Check this setup*, to paste in the forum or in an issue, shown and copied to a USB drive (`hades-results.txt`); no serial number, address, network or name of the PC. A diagnostic report has it too.
- **The display settings laid out by what goes out.** *System > Display* goes from the general to the fine: Kodi's *General* and *Whitelist*, then *HDR* (whether the display switches to HDR, which HDR videos go out as SDR and the SDR they are made for, the wide colours of SDR videos, the brightness of the menus in HDR), *Dolby Vision* (how it goes out, then what the TV gets with it), *Other videos as Dolby Vision*, *HDR on displays without Dolby Vision, and projectors* (Dolby Vision as HDR10, with its mapping to the display right under it), *HDR colour depth and format*, and the engine. A setting that depends on another sits right under it, and every setting keeps its value. Asked for by JonnyVee.
- **"Adjust display HDR mode" next to "Play HDR videos as SDR".** Kodi's setting moves from *Player > Videos* to the top of *System > Display > HDR*, with a help that says what Off does in LibreELEC Hades (Dolby Vision still goes out as Dolby Vision while native output is on) and how to have Dolby Vision as SDR; *Play HDR videos as SDR* sits under it, at the Advanced level. Asked for by JonnyVee.
- **The settings of the player information in *Player > Videos*:** *Show the playback mode when a video starts* and *Kodi's original player information* have a group of their own there, and the depth of subtitles over 3D video is in *Player > Subtitles > Stereoscopic 3D*.
- ***Check the Dolby Vision output* is in *Check this setup*** (*Settings > LibreELEC > System*), and *Submit Log* starts with the diagnostic report, the diagnostic switch and the copy to a USB drive.
- *Custom: gamma of the display* showed "?0?" instead of its value, and *Black of the display* showed "0.0" for blacks under 0.05 cd/m2; both are sliders with their value now.
- **The file cache is 2 GB on PCs with 28 GiB of memory or more,** so a Dolby Vision FEL remux at 130 Mbit/s keeps about 50 s ahead; and the `<cache>` of `advancedsettings.xml`, which Kodi has ignored since version 21, is taken as the default of *Settings > Services > Caching* (a value set there still wins, and `kodi.log` says which).

**Graphics and connections**

- **Intel graphics and AV receivers** (issue #28). After an AV receiver drops HPD to switch inputs or leave standby, the HDMI port now stays off 300 ms before its link is set up again, as the Anthem AVM 90 needs (`i915.hdmi_relink_off_ms`; 0 turns it off). The kernel log also says how each HDMI mode set goes out. Thanks to corgeone for the measurements. Not tried with such a receiver.
- **A display that gives no EDID through a passive DisplayPort adaptor** (issue #31, AMD graphics): the kernel log says that no EDID came through the adaptor, so the driver takes the display for a DVI one and lists 640x480 only, with no sound, no HDMI 3D and no Dolby Vision. It said "a DVI display" before.
- **AMD: variable refresh on TVs kept with Linux 7.4,** which asks a TV for a FreeSync code that TVs do not have and would turn off VRR, HDMI 2.1 VRR too; Hades keeps it as in Linux 7.3, and `amdgpu.freesync_hdmi_vcp=1` gives the new check. After an idea of YBLOD.
- **AMD: Dolby Vision and 3D keep their InfoFrame with Linux 7.4,** whose new ALLM signalling would have replaced it at every frame.
- **NVIDIA: the driver offers DRM's "content type"** (drivers 620 and 580), for Filmmaker Mode above, and native Dolby Vision says no content type in the AVI InfoFrame (ITC 0), as Intel, AMD and licensed Dolby Vision players send it.

**Streams, discs and files**

- **The settings of each film are kept by the film.** Kodi keeps the settings of a video by its file; for a film or an episode that an add-on plays, LibreELEC Hades also keeps them by its IMDb, TMDb or TVDb id (with the season and number of an episode), so they come back when the same film plays from another source: the audio and subtitle tracks, found again by what they are (language and its variety, forced, commentary, name, codec, channels), never by their number; subtitles turned off; the zoom, for a picture of the same shape; the picture settings, for a video of the same format; the volume, for the same cut; the audio and subtitle delays, only for the same release; and the Dolby Vision output chosen for it. Two films are never mixed, and a short notification says what was restored. Files of this device and of the local network, and the library, keep their settings by their file, as in Kodi. *Player > Videos > Remember the settings of films from add-ons*, on by default.
- **FTP sources with a user and a password** no longer stop at the first seek with curl 8.23 (Rudi Heitbaum).
- **External subtitles:** a `.sup` file seeks back to its start, a chapter jump moves the subtitle file too, a subtitle file no longer delays the start of a seek, a closed or replaced subtitle file is no longer read, and dense PGS and VobSub files no longer fall behind (matthane).
- **An HLS stream loads only the variant it plays,** within the bandwidth set in the network settings, instead of every variant (Daniil Gentili; its FFmpeg part written again for FFmpeg 9.1).
- **A font collection with one broken face keeps the other faces** (Portisch), and **network shares are not opened again while the system goes to sleep** (Portisch).
- **Smaller fixes:** no default refresh rate read from an empty list, and a thread that ends on an exception is logged as an error (SamuriHL); the counter of demuxer ids is atomic (matthane).

**The start, the system and the diagnostics**

- **A start no longer stays on the picture of the start with Kodi running behind it** (issue #14). Where Kodi started while the picture of the start was being drawn on displays of different sizes, the picture held the displays and Kodi ran with no picture. Kodi's start now waits for a picture that is being drawn, at most as long as that picture takes, and takes the displays once it is shown.
- **The driver of the ASUS Xonar sound cards starts after the NVIDIA driver** (issue #14). On a PC with an RTX 5090 and a Xonar Essence STX II, some starts stopped while both drivers started at once; the Xonar now waits for the NVIDIA card to be up (about a second where the NVIDIA driver starts; 5 seconds where it has not begun to start, 30 seconds at most), and the kernel log says so.
- **The diagnostics name the drivers that a start that hung was starting** (issue #14). With the diagnostic switch on, `previous-boot/not-returned.txt` lists every driver that the journal of the start before shows starting and not finished by its last line, with the last line each one wrote, and the kernel log says it in one line.
- **NVIDIA's driver 580 keeps all of its video memory across a sleep** (GeForce 900 and 10 series, TITAN V), with NVIDIA's power management interface. Driver 620 does it by itself, as before.
- **The diagnostic report says what the firmware offers for sleep,** how the last sleeps went and what may wake the PC (issue #32).
- **Check this setup no longer cuts a test picture that is slow to come up.** A picture counts from when it fills the screen, which waits for the change of mode and of HDR, up to 30 seconds; the result says when a picture never came up, and the report says why each clip stopped.
- **With Secure Boot, only an update that the update channel signed is applied inside Hades.** The running system checks it against its signed manifest before anything of the update is read, and signs the new kernel and boot loader with its own signer. Any other update, such as a `.tar` copied by hand, is not applied: use the installer for Windows, which signs it for the PC. Secure Boot does not protect against software running as root in Hades, which today includes every add-on.

**Up to date**

- **Linux 7.4-rc4,** the newest release candidate (7.3.6, the newest stable, has nothing that it lacks). The patches of Hades that mainline now has are gone (40 NTFS fixes, the mt7921 and mt7925 join fixes, a DCN 4.2B fix); the others are carried over, and the Realtek 8723BS SDIO Wi-Fi keeps a driver (rtw88, since 7.4 drops the staging one). A release candidate is not a stable kernel.
- **FFmpeg 9.1.** 278 of the 301 patches of 1.1 are part of it now; the 23 that stay (the Dolby Vision RPU and metadata, H.264 MVC 3D, Matroska editions and seek, PGS, and seven fixes from upstream that 9.1 lacks) apply unchanged or refreshed, two more fixes come from its development branch (the frame rate of streams with corrupt packets, H.264 field pairs), and the H.264 MVC decoder follows the H.264 decoder of 9.1. 9.1 brings, among much else, AC-3 decoded without truncating its coefficients and MV-HEVC decoding.
- **Kodi 22:** the `Piers` branch up to `c1d4d42d1a74`, among it the delay of the sound output with passthrough packets that carry no frames, a GUI held to 1080p (kept out of the 3D modes of Hades) and the abort of the probe of a demuxer.
- **Mesa 26.3.1** (radeonsi and iris fixes), **NVIDIA 620.73.04**, and **580.196.11** for the GeForce GTX 900 and 10 cards and the TITAN V, built by Hades for Linux 7.4.
- **libplacebo** up to `bfdb8dec6ef6`; the Dolby Vision bridge draws the same bytes as in 1.1 (measured on the software bridge).
- **LibreELEC `master` up to `c1800c3c4228`:** LLVM 23.1.6, Intel's media driver 26.4.3 and new root certificates.
- **Firmware:** linux-firmware 20261124 (AMD display and graphics, Intel GuC, Wi-Fi and Bluetooth).
- **Security and other fixes:** OpenSSL 4.1 and 3.5 with the fixes of their stable branches up to 24 November (among them an endless loop in SSHKDF and safe integer arithmetic); curl with 19 fixes from its development branch (TLS session and host name checks, malformed HTTP header endings, alt-svc and IDN host names, proxy exceptions, memory-safety fixes); FFmpeg with 33 fixes from its development branch (the HEVC, H.264, AV1, VVC, AAC and MP3 decoders, the mov, MPEG-TS, HLS, DASH and MP3 readers, HTTP streaming); libass with six memory-safety fixes for subtitle files and embedded fonts; BlueZ with eleven fixes for data that Bluetooth devices send; Python 3.14 with 20 fixes of its branch (among them NUL bytes in getaddrinfo() and proxy credentials sent to the wrong host); HarfBuzz with two overflow fixes for fonts embedded in videos; SQLite with fixes of its 3.54 branch for damaged databases and the sqlite3 shell; BusyBox with 13 fixes from its development branch (among them stricter checks of NTFS and HFS+ volumes plugged in); glibc, libbluray (4 fixes) and avahi (2 fixes) from their own repositories; OpenJDK 27.0.1.1 with the October update (among it CVE-2026-114938); expat 2.11.0, GnuTLS 3.8.14 with libtasn1 4.21.0 (now a library of its own), libseccomp 2.6.2 (the filters of the sandbox of Blu-ray Java), libmicrohttpd 1.0.11, fontconfig 2.18.4, HarfBuzz 15.2.0, NSS 3.134 with NSPR 4.41, exfatprogs 1.4.5; zstd 1.6.0 (fixes in decompression), libnfs 8.1.0 (replies checked, TLS that fails closed), pycryptodome 4.6.0, libffi 3.8.1, flex 2.7.8, kmod 34.4, nfs-utils 3.2.1, utfcpp 5.0.0, soxr (crash fixes of the resampler), packaging 26.4 and PyYAML 6.0.4.

### Details

- The log says "all automatic" when the Dolby Vision engine is at its defaults.
- The read back of the Dolby Vision tunnel on the Radeon RX 400, RX 500 and Vega cards names two more fields of the line buffer, for the check of issue #20.
- BT.2020 in the AVI InfoFrame of the Dolby Vision tunnel: `linux-9897` (the DRM helper, with KUnit tests), `linux-9898` (i915), `linux-9899` (amdgpu), the property `DVBRIDGE_DV_AVI_BT2020_LAB`.
- Intel: `linux-9882` keeps the port off before a sink's link is set up again (`i915.hdmi_relink_off_ms`, 300 by default), `linux-9886` writes each HDMI mode set to the kernel log.
- AMD: the static HDR packet of a stream that replaces one at the same timing or on a pipe that DC keeps (`linux-9883`); a display without an EDID behind a passive adaptor said by `linux-9863` alone.
- NVIDIA: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9084` and `nvidia-legacy-open-9081` say why HDMI 3D frame packing is refused; `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9085` and `nvidia-legacy-open-9082` offer the "content type" property of DRM on HDMI heads and put it in the AVI InfoFrame; `nvidia-legacy-open-9080` and `nvidia-legacy-9001` build nvidia-drm of 580.196.11 on Linux 7.4; the driver of the Xonar cards waits for nvidia.
- Linux 7.4: `linux-9864` (`amdgpu.freesync_hdmi_vcp`), `linux-9865` (the vendor InfoFrames of Dolby Vision and 3D with the ALLM update), `linux-9866` (KUnit tests of the amdgpu connector init helper with the defaults of Hades).
- FFmpeg: `ffmpeg-9953` (the second view of H.264 MVC in Matroska by a block addition mapping or by the stream), `ffmpeg-9971` (fixes from FFmpeg's development branch), `ffmpeg-9972` (the HLS demuxer loads only the variant that `max_bandwidth` selects).
- `not-returned.awk` of `hades-diag`; `LibreELEC-settings-9072` (the wait for a test picture); `LibreELEC-settings-9073` (the A/B comparison); the clip of the A/B comparison is made in the build by `hades-check` (`abclip.py`).
- The Kodi changes of this version are `kodi-9157` to `kodi-9291`. The settings kept for films are in a table of their own, `hadesfilmsettings`, in Kodi's video database, whose version does not change: an older build opens the same database and leaves the table alone.
- `libplacebo-9947` (`pl_opengl_pause_timers()`), so that seven frames in eight open no timer query; `LibreELEC-settings-9074` (the frame packing card), `9080` (the layout of the settings), `9085` (*Your display*, *Share my results*), `9086` (a setting that the A/B comparison or the frame packing card could not put back is said as one of the check, which the next start puts back as it was) and `9087` (the frames lost in steady playback, said by *Check this setup*); `hades-diag` 26; a sleep hook for driver 580 (`/usr/lib/systemd/system-sleep/hades-nvidia`, with `NVreg_PreserveVideoMemoryAllocations=1` for 580 only).
- The state that says that the GPU lost the time of its timer queries (`GL_GPU_DISJOINT_EXT`) is read once a frame and told to every part that times its pictures (Kodi's renderer, HDR10 adapted to the display, Dolby Vision rebuilt and native, the passes of the bridges), so that none takes a time of a lost period as true; and a GPU whose timestamps have no bits (`GL_QUERY_COUNTER_BITS_EXT` 0) is taken as one without a timer.
- Taken from upstream: `curl-9921` to `9939`, `libass-9902` to `9907`, `bluez-9900` to `9910`, `Python3-9910` to `9921`, `harfbuzz-9900` and `9901`, `openssl-9991` to `9999`, `openssl3-9964` to `9975`, `ffmpeg-9973`, `busybox-9917` and `9918`, `sqlite-9900` to `9913`; and, for add-ons, ntfs-3g 2026.9.28 and libyaml 0.2.7.
- `hades-picture`: a picture that is being drawn writes `/run/hades-picture.busy` before it opens a device, and "release" (Kodi's start) is answered by the service once that picture is shown and the displays are let go of.
- The update channel: the package `hades-update-channel` (the address of the manifest, the two Ed25519 keys with ids `444378a0ca23e565` and `da5e1e82124a210e`, and the number of the version: 1.2 is 1020099), which the build installs only with keys whose proofs hold and, given the publisher's pinned copy, are those; `LibreELEC-settings-9090` puts the client of `9016` in use (strict fields, `min_serial`, a release without a manifest read as nothing newer, a look again in ten minutes when the network or the clock is not ready, notifications with the version) and adds: redirects only to GitHub's hosts of release files, four at most; no proxy from the environment; a time limit for the manifest and a stall limit for the download; room for what the next start unpacks; the leftover of a download cut short removed; a file already downloaded not downloaded again.
- `LibreELEC-settings-9091` (only the verified update is left, read back before it is kept; a manifest gone after one was taken is a warning in the log) and `9092` with the `init` (with Secure Boot, an update is proven by the client and keys of the running system, from its signed manifest kept next to it, before anything of it is read, and signed with the running system's signer); `kodi-9330` to `kodi-9332` and `libbluray-9107`.
- `tools/regress` gained the tests of each change, each proven by a mutant that must make it fail.

### Credits

- Taken with their authors or adapted from their changes, each patch naming its source: Rudi Heitbaum's FTP fix (Kodi pull request 30391), Daniil Gentili's HLS change (Kodi pull request 30046), Portisch's pull requests 30317 and 30662 and his two changes for CoreELEC, matthane's fixes of external subtitles for CoreELEC's Kodi, and fixes after SamuriHL's changes to CoreELEC's Kodi; the commits of Kodi's `Piers` branch; and the fixes of OpenSSL, curl, FFmpeg, libass, BlueZ, HarfBuzz, SQLite, glibc, BusyBox, Python, libbluray, avahi and the Linux kernel.
- AiUpscale is a set of mpv shaders by Alexkral, under GPL-3.0, shipped unchanged.
- The network protocols of the projectors were written from the descriptions in pyjvcprojector (Steve Easley), pySDCP (Galala7) and the Sony projector binding of openHAB, ucr-integration-sonyADCP (kennymc-c) and epson_projector (pszafer), read as protocol documentation; no code of them is in Hades.
- The faults that the items above say CroqueMr's build and SamuriHL found are fixed here in Hades' own way. The A/B comparison and the form of the metadata rest on the measurements of fandangos ([CroqueMr/libreelec-CB1 pull request 212](https://github.com/CroqueMr/libreelec-CB1/pull/212)) and on what the licensed driver of an OPPO player sends ([fandangos/oppo-dv-mkv](https://github.com/fandangos/oppo-dv-mkv)). The colours of profile 20 and the eye of each MV-HEVC view follow ht2tweak's Full SBS 3D Converter, read as data. The kept VRR of AMD on TVs follows an idea of YBLOD. corgeone measured what the Anthem AVM 90 needs.
- [CREDITS.md](CREDITS.md) has every credit of Hades, with the source of each, and where each request came from.
- Requests of users that this version answers: JonnyVee (the gamut of SDR, the layout of the display settings and Dolby Vision as SDR), lvasan (the comparison of brightness on a Sony A80K), NTM-3D (MV-HEVC in 3D) and MrPhenix007 (HDR screenshots).
- Thank you to everyone whose reports and tests shaped this version, among them Khazar321, NTM-3D, JonnyVee, lvasan, theged, Alaric, corgeone, harment, MrPhenix007, SunnySnacks, SeaMonkey82 and MrMagic.

## 1.1

New things to see and to choose, and fixes for what was reported after 1.0. The player information is redesigned, and a remote reaches its button: O, then OK, changes how a Dolby Vision video goes out while it plays. Any HDR video can be watched as SDR, for one film. On a TV with variable refresh rate, a video can start without the screen going black. Upscalers rebuild the detail of videos smaller than the screen, HDR10 can be mapped scene by scene for a projector, and SDR videos made in BT.2020 can keep their wide colours. The start is one picture from the boot menu to the interface. Dolby Vision goes through the USB-C hub of a Chromebook. On a PC with Windows, Hades and Windows restart into each other without the boot menu key, and a PC that keeps Secure Boot on can start Hades with it, with a key made for that PC. On a 2.35:1 screen, Dolby Vision films can be zoomed scene by scene to their picture. It also brings Linux 7.3.5, the latest commits of Kodi 22 and the security fixes of its libraries. Every change was tested on the host, with the code that the image runs, or in a virtual machine; what has been seen on real hardware, and what still waits for hardware I do not have, is in [What has been tested](docs/tested.md).

### What's new for you

**The player information**

- **Redesigned, in one frame.** Press O, or the red key of a TV remote that sends keys over HDMI-CEC. The overview opens with the path of the signal: the film, what Hades does with it, and what reaches your TV. Every page has the same height and the same bar at the bottom: the use and temperature of the processor and of the graphics, the memory, the pictures dropped and skipped, and the button for the output of the video. The System page now also says how the TV is connected (the connector, the link with its speed, the output format and depth, whether variable refresh rate is on) and gives the versions of Hades, of the graphics driver and of the kernel. The mastering display reads least / most ("0.0050 / 1000 nits"), as the source display of Dolby Vision does, the Dolby Vision composer is told by its fields, the sound is given in kHz and kb/s, the table of the Dolby Vision metadata levels shows which are in the file, used and sent, and the speakers are drawn on a plan of the room. It takes the colours of the LibreELEC Hades mark, keeps clear of the title, the clock and the time bar, and writes its values with commas and words. Nothing that it showed is gone.
- **"Output of this video" with a remote, a keyboard and Yatse** (the button was called "Change output for this video"). Down on the tabs closed the panel before the button had the focus. Down now goes to the button, Up goes back to the tab you came from, and OK on the Overview or Dolby Vision page opens the choice of output at once: O, then OK, changes how a Dolby Vision video goes out while it plays. After an idea of CroqueMr's build (its Quick Switch).
- **As SDR, for one video.** The output of the video that plays (the button of the player information, the video's on-screen controls and its video settings) now offers "As SDR (without HDR)" for any HDR video, Dolby Vision included, on a display that takes HDR: for a projector, or a TV whose SDR picture you prefer for that film. HLG videos get the choice too, with "HLG as Dolby Vision" and "Without Dolby Vision". The choice is remembered for the file. For a video that is not Dolby Vision, the list is called "HDR output (this video)". After ideas of YBLOD and CroqueMr's build.
- **The player information opens without a stall on the first press,** also when a video starts right after Kodi does or with a dialog left open: the windows that open over a video are now loaded as it starts, before its first picture. `kodi.log` says what each window opened over a video costs. In a virtual machine the first open went from 56 ms to 12 ms.
- **Kodi's original player information** (*System > Display*, Advanced, off): Kodi's own short panel on the first press, the pages of LibreELEC Hades on the second. After an idea of CroqueMr's build and YBLOD.
- **The player information says what the zoom to the active area does** (Video page, with the setting on): the area and how much larger the picture is drawn, or why it does nothing (no level 5 data, no black bars, another view mode, a disc menu and the like).
- **Opening the player information over a video no longer holds the picture for a moment on NVIDIA graphics** (about 100 ms on an RTX 4080 SUPER). The temperatures that it shows are read in the background, only the one that it shows, and ahead of the first open, so the bar has them at once: LibreELEC's command for the temperature of the graphics runs `nvidia-smi`, and Kodi waited for it on the thread that presents the video. Measured in a virtual machine with a command that takes 100 ms: 105 ms before, under 2 ms now.
- **The bar of the player information on a PC without a temperature sensor for its processor** shows the use of the processor alone. Kodi's "Not available" did not fit its box and was cut, in English and in Spanish.
- **The speakers of the Audio page no longer stay "not known".** When the sound output was set up again just after a video started, as it is when the display changes its mode for Dolby Vision, the layout of the sound was cleared and never given again, so the plan of the room showed no speaker for the whole video. It is given again now. Where the player has not said the layout, the plan shows the usual one for the track's number of channels, and says so.
- **The upscaler from the player information.** On the Video page (O), a button just above the one for the output of the video names the upscaler chosen for the videos like the one that plays (1080p and above, below 1080p, or animation) and changes it: OK on the button, or on the Video tab. The button for the output of the video stays in the bar, one press of Down below it, and both are wide enough for their longest texts. The choice is the setting of *Settings > Player > Videos*, for all those videos, and the list says so.

**Picture**

- **HDR is mapped on a display without HDR again, as the settings say.** A new profile, and every profile of 1.0 and earlier, had Kodi's tone mapping method "Off" instead of the documented default, "Reference (BT.2390)": on a display or projector without HDR, HDR10, HDR10+, HLG and Dolby Vision profiles 7 and 8.1 played unmapped and washed out, and so did *Play HDR videos as SDR*. A saved "Off" becomes "Reference (BT.2390)" once, also in the settings that Kodi keeps for each file; an "Off" chosen afterwards stays, and `kodi.log` says what it changed. Found by YBLOD.
- **Keep the wide colours of SDR videos (BT.2020)** (*System > Display*, Advanced, off). A few SDR videos (some Ultra HD Blu-rays and streams) are made in the wider colours of BT.2020, and Kodi converts them to the ordinary colours of BT.709, clipping what lies outside. With this on, on a display whose EDID says that it takes BT.2020, they go out as they are, and the menus and subtitles are converted to match so that they keep their usual colours. `kodi.log` says how each such video goes out. After an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- **SDR in BT.2020 converted to BT.709 keeps its greys and its black.** Without tone mapping, Kodi took such a picture into light with one gamma and back with another, so a mid grey of 128 came out as 122, and in limited range its black came out as 13 instead of 16. The bars around a picture composited with the menus were cleared to 0 instead of the black of the signal (16, or 64 at 10 bits). Each is fixed. Measured on a software renderer.
- **The menus, the on-screen controls and the subtitles over HDR keep the shadows they have in SDR.** Over HDR10, HLG and Dolby Vision, and with the menus in Dolby Vision, the interface was turned into HDR with the sRGB curve, whose straight part near black makes the darkest greys much lighter than a TV shows the same menus in SDR: at the default white, a grey of 2 % came out at 0.31 cd/m², where a TV in SDR shows 0.016. It now uses the curve of BT.1886, as ITU-R BT.2408 puts SDR into HDR. White, full colours and the video do not change. *GUI and subtitle gamma in HDR* (*System > Display*, Expert) also offers 2.2, or Kodi's sRGB curve as before; the choice of a curve is after an idea of YBLOD. Measured on a software renderer.
- **Limited colour range:** where the driver refuses to make the limited range itself, the picture is compressed once, not twice, and a change of the setting during a video takes effect when the video ends.
- **The dark squares of *Check this setup* in HDR** (issue #20) show the same light as those of the SDR card with a white of 203 cd/m² (0.017 to 0.47 cd/m²). In HDR10 they were half as bright or less, and the second one was hard to see.
- **Zoom to the active area of Dolby Vision videos, for 2.35:1 and 2.39:1 screens** (*Settings > Player > Videos*, Expert, off). Each scene of a Dolby Vision video is enlarged to the part of the picture that its Dolby Vision data mark as active (level 5), and the zoom follows the film when its shape changes, as in films with IMAX scenes. It changes at the cut where the film marks one, and a single odd picture or a fade moves nothing. Nothing of the picture is cut off: a scene taller than the screen fills its height, with bars at its sides. The screen is the one set in *Video calibration*, and the level 5 sent to a Dolby Vision TV is the one of what is drawn. It is not used for videos without that data, for 3D, while a disc menu is shown, or with a view mode you chose. With the setting off, everything is drawn as before (the same bytes, measured in a virtual machine). After an idea of CorentinB.
- **Upscalers** (*Settings > Player > Videos*, Expert, or the button of the Video page of the player information; off). When a video is drawn larger than it is, such as 1080p on a 4K screen or a DVD on a 1080p one, RAVU, FSRCNNX 8 or FSRCNNX 16 rebuild its fine detail instead of Kodi's scaling: one choice for videos of 1080p and above, one for smaller videos, and one for animation. Measured against Kodi's Lanczos 3 on film, animation and HDR10 pictures, they come closer to the original by 0.1 to 1.3 dB (RAVU), 0.3 to 3.4 dB (FSRCNNX 8) and 0.4 to 4.7 dB (FSRCNNX 16). They change the picture, so they are off unless you choose one, and they are never used for Dolby Vision. If the graphics cannot draw one in time, it turns itself off for that video and a message says so. After an idea of ashlar (neural upscalers in Kodi) and of CroqueMr's build.
- **HDR10 mapped scene by scene** (*System > Display*, Expert, off). Where Hades maps an HDR10 video itself (to SDR, or adapted to a projector), it can measure each scene as it plays and map it by its own brightest and average light, as it does for Dolby Vision, instead of by the one brightness that the studio wrote for the whole film. A dark scene keeps more of its highlights, and the mapping changes slowly within a scene, so the picture does not pump. HDR10+ videos keep their own scene data. After an idea of mephis.
- **Video at its own rate with VRR** (*System > Display*, Expert, Off by default). On a display with variable refresh rate (VRR), an SDR or HDR10 video can play at its own frame rate in the mode of the menus, each picture shown at its time: 23.976 fps goes out at 47.952 Hz on a TV whose VRR starts at 40 Hz, each picture twice, and at 71.928 Hz on a monitor whose VRR starts at 48 Hz, each picture three times. You choose where:
  - *Off*: every video as in LibreELEC, with a change of mode to its rate where the display has one.
  - *Where the display has no mode at the video's rate*: only where the display has no mode at the rate of the video, or at twice it, at any resolution. Some PC monitors have no mode of 24, 48 or 72 Hz, and a film of 23.976 fps went out at 120 or 360 Hz, one picture held a refresh longer every few seconds. Such a monitor takes only the modes that its EDID lists, so no mode at the rate of a film can be made for it; VRR is the way to that rate. A TV with modes of 24 Hz, as the LG C5, changes its mode as always.
  - *Always*: on any display with VRR, also where it has a mode at the rate of the video, so the screen does not go black when a video starts or stops.

  On NVIDIA GeForce GTX 16 and newer the display engine holds each picture until its time, on the RTX 50 series Media VRR keeps the rate, and on AMD graphics Kodi times each picture itself. Native Dolby Vision, HLG and 3D change the mode as always; HDR10+, and Dolby Vision sent as HDR10, HDR10+ or SDR, do too except on the RTX 40 and RTX 50 series (below); and so does any video that this cannot show exactly. With VRR on, the display's own information shows the rate that it gets (47 Hz on an LG C5, 72 Hz on the monitor), and so does the player information, measured. `kodi.log` says what was chosen for each video and how exact its pictures were. Needs VRR turned on in the display.
- **"Video at its own rate with VRR" checks that VRR really comes on.** In the first second of each video, Hades checks that the refresh of the output follows the video: on NVIDIA graphics the driver says so itself, and Hades also measures when each picture reached the output; on other graphics Hades measures the refreshes of the output. If it does not follow, the video plays as with the setting off, for the rest of that video. The player information (System page, VRR) says which it was, and "Not on: no mode at the video's rate" where the mode it plays in is no whole multiple of its rate. The display's own VRR setting must be on: displays do not report it. Videos that Kodi draws itself, and not on a layer of their own, are now paced too.
- **The player information gives the refresh rate that the display gets with VRR,** measured from the pictures as they reach the output: "2560x1440 @ 71.928 Hz (VRR)" for a film of 23.976 fps on a monitor whose VRR starts at 48 Hz. The VRR row says how many times each picture is shown ("On: each picture 3 times"), or why VRR is off: the setting, Dolby Vision that changes the mode, or no mode at the video's rate.
- **HDR10+ at its own rate with VRR on the RTX 40 and RTX 50 series.** Their driver sends the HDR10+ metadata of each picture next to the VRR signal, so *Video at its own rate with VRR* now takes HDR10+ videos too. Elsewhere HDR10+ changes the mode as always: on the GTX 16, RTX 20 and RTX 30 series the one place for HDR10+ is the one that VRR takes. The driver says which it is.
- **Dolby Vision sent as HDR10, HDR10+ or SDR at its own rate with VRR on the RTX 40 and RTX 50 series,** as HDR10+ goes, where it cannot go out as HLG. Dolby Vision sent as Dolby Vision changes the mode to the rate of the video as always: its signal has modes of its own of up to 60 Hz, and no display says that it takes it with VRR.
- **HDMI variable refresh rate (VRR) is off by default on NVIDIA** (*System > Display > HDMI variable refresh rate (VRR)*, Expert). NVIDIA's driver turned VRR on for an HDMI TV that declares it, in every mode from 50 Hz up, and some TVs switch to their game mode while it is on. Kodi now asks the driver to leave it off, as it is on AMD and Intel graphics, unless you turn it on. *Video at its own rate with VRR* leaves it to the driver in every change of mode at *Always*, and at *Where the display has no mode at the video's rate* in the modes of the menus of such a display. A change applies at once, with a change of mode of the menus (during a video, with the one at its end), so that the next video finds VRR as the settings say; the end of a native Dolby Vision video puts back what the settings say then, in the one change of mode that ends it.
- **A film at its own rate on a TV whose menus are in another resolution.** Without a whitelist of your own, Kodi took a mode at the rate of a video only at the size of the video or of the menus: an LG C5 with its menus at 2560x1440 played a 1080p film at 120 Hz, though it has 3840x2160 at 23.976 Hz. Where those sizes have no mode at the rate (or twice it), an SDR video now gets one at another size: the display's own preferred size first, then the nearest one above the menus. With a whitelist of your own, it decides as before; Dolby Vision and HDR keep their way. `kodi.log` says what was chosen.
- **HEVC videos that say their pictures may be fields, and are whole pictures, are shown as progressive.** Some HEVC streams (an HDR10+ film, for example) say in their parameters that the timing of each picture may make it a field, though every one is a whole picture: Kodi then never gave them a mode at their own rate (25 Hz for 25p), the whole-number multiples or VRR. Hades now reads the timing of their first pictures before the video starts, and where it says whole pictures the video is progressive from its first choice of a mode, with one change of mode. Interlaced video is treated as before, and native Dolby Vision keeps its cadence. The timing is read only where the answer can change the mode that the video gets: a film at 23.976 fps with *Video at its own rate with VRR* off, the default, starts without waiting for it.
- **Each display keeps the resolution of its menus.** Kodi kept one resolution for the menus, of whatever display it was set on, and took it to another display where that one had it: an LG C5 started in the 2560x1440 that a monitor had left, and its films lost their 3840x2160 at 23.976 Hz. The menus of each display now come back in the resolution you chose on it; a display new to Kodi starts in its own preferred mode.
- **Adjust display resolution** (*Player > Videos*, next to *Adjust display refresh rate*, off). A video goes out in a mode of its own size at the refresh rate Kodi chooses for it, and the TV scales it: a 1080p film at 1920x1080 at 23.976 Hz on a TV whose menus are at 3840x2160, where Kodi kept the size of the menus unless a whitelist of your own listed the 1080p modes. The cadence never changes, and where the display has no mode of that size at that rate the video plays as before. Native Dolby Vision keeps its output at 3840x2160, the only size LibreELEC Hades makes its Dolby Vision signal at; with a whitelist of your own, the whitelist decides. `kodi.log` says what was chosen.
  - With *Video at its own rate with VRR* on too, the size wins: the video goes in one change of mode to the mode of its size with the highest refresh rate in the display's VRR range (1920x1080 at 120 Hz on an LG C5), at its own rate with VRR in it, checked in its first second as always. Where the display has modes of that size and none with VRR, the change of mode is to the exact rate, as without VRR.
  - Where the menus need the fastest link of HDMI 2.1 (3840x2160 at 120 Hz on NVIDIA graphics), a 1080p film still goes out at its own size and rate, 1920x1080 at 23.976 Hz: its mode goes over the older TMDS link, as NVIDIA's driver chooses it, and the way back to the menus turns the output off first. Not tried on hardware.
- **A monitor that was slow to wake as the PC started gets its fastest modes without a restart.** NVIDIA graphics offer the modes that the link of an HDMI 2.1 display carries, and a display that was still waking up when the driver looked at it got only the modes of up to 600 MHz: a Samsung Odyssey G60SD started at 120 Hz, with no 360 Hz to choose until Kodi was restarted. When the display's own signal tells the driver to look again, Kodi now takes its new modes too, and the menus go back to the mode you chose.
- **The menus are no longer shown huge and cut for a few seconds at the start** on a display whose mode of the menus needs what other lit displays hold of the graphics card (2560x1440 at 360 Hz on a G60SD next to two other displays). The other displays, kept lit for 10 s so that their notice can be read, now go off as soon as they keep the menus from their mode.

**Dolby Vision**

- **HDMI variable refresh rate is left off during native Dolby Vision on every NVIDIA card of the open driver,** the RTX 40 and RTX 50 series included. 1.0 did it only where the driver had the property for HDR10+ (the GeForce GTX 16 and RTX 20 cards, and RTX 30 cards whose firmware leaves the slot of that packet free): an RTX 40 or 50 card sent Dolby Vision at 50 Hz and up with VRR on to a TV that declares it. On those cards a Dolby Vision video in the mode of the menus at 50 Hz or more now has one change of mode, to turn VRR off.
- **No active area that the video does not have.** Where the Dolby Vision metadata of a video have no active area (level 5) and the picture fills the screen, Hades no longer makes one of the whole picture: the video does not carry it, and a block that the video does not have can make the display apply adjustments that are not meant for it. The level 5 of the video, the one of black bars that the player adds and the one of Hades' own metadata go out as before, and the player information says "L5 sent: none". Found by fandangos.
- **The whole black bars of an HD Dolby Vision video with an RPU of its own.** ETSI GS CCM 001 counts the level 5 offsets (the active area) in the pixels of the picture, but an HD encode may also keep the RPU of its 4K master, and the metadata do not say which. Hades took the smaller bars of the two readings, so a 1080p film with an RPU of its own was sent half of its bars, and the zoom to the active area took away half of them. Now a few pictures of the video are measured: where the band between the two readings is black, with picture next to it, the display is sent the whole bars, and the zoom and the player information take them. One picture with picture in that band keeps the cautious reading for the rest of the video. 4K videos are not concerned: the bytes are the same.
- **The "Picture area" of the player information reads the level 5 block as the output does:** an HD encode that kept the RPU of its 4K master showed twice its bars. The check of the level 5 block against the picture also runs while the zoom to the active area is drawn, and offsets left aside are taken again when the film says so.
- **Low-Latency that cannot use what the display gives** (issue #25). Where the peak or the colours that a display gives for Low-Latency cannot be used, a display that also takes Standard now gets Standard, with the enhancement layer, and one that offers only Low-Latency gets the video rebuilt as HDR10, enhancement layer included; before, it got its HDR10 base picture without it. The log no longer says "the display offers no other" where it does. The help of the Low-Latency settings, of *Adapt Dolby Vision to my display (projectors)* and of *Show Dolby Vision as HDR10 when the display cannot* is written again, so that the difference between them is clear.
- **Low-Latency on displays whose colours reach past BT.2020** (laser projectors, as the one of issue #25, and TVs that round their blue past it). Each such primary is taken to the nearest point of the edge of BT.2020, the way that measured closest to the display's own colours, instead of refusing Low-Latency; a block that no display can have is still refused. The projector of issue #25 gets Low-Latency again, and an LG C5 is not affected. `kodi.log` says what the display declared and what is used.
- **Low-Latency mapped to the black that the display gives** (*Low-Latency: use the black level the display gives*, Expert, on). The darkest parts of the film are lifted to the black level that the Dolby Vision information of the display gives for Low-Latency, so that they do not sink into its black. Most OLED TVs give 0, and nothing changes; LCD TVs and projectors give about 0.005 to 0.27 cd/m². After an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- **Frames ahead for a graphics card at its limit** (issue #4). A card held to a low power limit missed a refresh now and then all through a film, too seldom for the rule of 1.0. Hades now also presents frames a frame ahead where 7 of the last 30 runs of 240 frames had a late frame and a picture that took more than a refresh period, and `kodi.log` says when. Replayed on the logs received: the film of issue #4 turns it on within about seven minutes, and the logs of an RTX 4080 SUPER, an RTX 4080 Laptop GPU and an RTX 5090 never do.
- **Dolby Vision through a USB-C hub or adapter.** A DisplayPort to HDMI converter is now found by asking the port itself, not only the tables of the board's firmware, so the converter of a USB-C hub or adapter is offered as the one on the board of a mini PC is, with *Dolby Vision through a display converter* (Expert) set to On. That setting is shown only where such a converter is behind the port, and the kernel log names the converter, so that one seen working can go on the list that Automatic uses. Based on the work of Felix Oghină (pull request #29).
- **No Dolby Vision through a passive DisplayPort adaptor that says DVI, on NVIDIA too.** A passive adaptor (DP++) that does not identify itself as HDMI, which NVIDIA's driver reports as a DVI one, no longer gets the Dolby Vision signal, as on Intel and AMD graphics; the video plays as on a display without native output, and `kodi.log` says why. Such an adaptor limits the picture to 1080p, so Dolby Vision, which goes out at 3840x2160, did not reach the TV through it before either. An HDMI port, an adaptor that says HDMI and the HDMI port of a laptop wired to a DisplayPort output are as before.
- **Radeon RX 400, RX 500 and Vega cards** (with *Dolby Vision on this Radeon (experimental)* on, issue #20).
