The Dolby Vision path no longer goes through the dynamic expansion of the card's formatter (`amdgpu.dvbridge_dce_expansion=1` on the kernel line brings back the way of 1.0, for a comparison), and the setting can be turned on for a DisplayPort port behind a passive adaptor that says HDMI. Where *Check the Dolby Vision output* finds that the card changes the pixels on an output, native Dolby Vision is not used there (the video plays as HDR10) until a new check after an update says otherwise. The expansion was found by CroqueMr.
- **Check the Dolby Vision output says where the bytes change:** which colour of the card's output breaks, and whether a change of one colour breaks the others too (a colour conversion) or only its own. Where it finds a card not exact, *HDR in 4:2:2 at 12 bits over HDMI 2.0* is not used on that output either, and HDR goes out as YCbCr 4:2:0 there, which that card showed right (issue #20).
- **Low-Latency with the interface over the picture costs the graphics less.** The interface is now composited by the packer of the signal as it packs, not into the full-precision picture of 3840x2160 first, which is then neither read and written under the interface nor cleared again around a picture that does not fill the screen. The same bytes go out, measured with the software bridge, where it took 18 to 23 ms instead of 33. After an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- **A Dolby Vision profile 8.1 video that also carries HDR10+, on a display with HDR10+ and without Dolby Vision,** goes out with its own HDR10+ metadata also where *Send it as HDR10+* is on; it was rebuilt with measured HDR10+ instead. Without that setting it already did.
- **An RPU without its terminator keeps its metadata.** A Dolby Vision RPU whose last byte (0x80) is missing, or is another byte, was refused whole, and the picture lost its Dolby Vision metadata, although nothing that the RPU says was lost. Where the checksum of the RPU holds at the place where its data end, it is now kept, with a warning in the log; one whose checksum does not hold is refused as before. Tested on hand-made RPUs. Reported against CroqueMr's build.
- **Dolby Vision profile 7 with an enhancement layer that starts late.** Where the first pictures of a video come without their enhancement layer, the layer no longer starts over every 64 pictures while it waits for the first picture it can start at. After an idea of CroqueMr's build.

**Sound**

- **Sound on a display that takes PCM of 16 or 20 bits only** (issue #23: a TV that played no sound at all). Decoded sound now goes out in 16 bits to such a display. Those that declare 24 bits, nearly all, are as before. *System > Audio > Limit PCM to what the display declares* (Expert, on; it was "Limit PCM channels to what the display declares") covers it.
- **An output kept on keeps its sound** (issue #24). Where the file `hades-keep-outputs` keeps an output on for an AV receiver, that receiver keeps the sound also with *Keep the display of the audio output on* off; it went to the display in use.
- **CEC through an output that carries no picture leaves the TV's input alone** (issue #24). Through a CEC adapter on an output that does not carry Kodi's picture (an AV receiver for the sound only, on another output of the graphics), Kodi no longer switches the TV's input, at its start, at the start of a video or when the receiver is switched to the PC; the remote and the rest work as before, and the settings of the adapter keep what you chose. Through the output of the picture, nothing changes. `kodi.log` says which it is.
- **CEC turned off takes the PC off the CEC bus at once.** libCEC kept the address of the PC until its adapter went away.
- **Kodi keeps looking for CEC adapters,** so the CEC adapter of a DisplayPort output that comes back after the TV or the converter is turned off and on, or a Pulse-Eight plugged in later, works without restarting Kodi. Found by CroqueMr.
- **Dolby Digital at 32 kHz is passed through.** Its frame size was taken a third too large, as in Kodi, so such a stream was never found. Found by CroqueMr.
- **Intel Alder Lake (the N97 and N100 too): HDMI sound no longer goes silent** after Dolby Vision ends while a passthrough track drains, as with Dolby TrueHD. The output pin of the sound was left off by the change of mode; Hades turns it on again, and the kernel log says when it did.
- **The TV stays in the list of outputs.** Changes of the sound devices that came in a burst, or while the audio engine was setting itself up, were dropped, and the list of outputs could stay without the TV's name; they are now acted on a moment later.
- **6.1 and 6.0 tracks keep their back centre** on a receiver that declares that speaker, when they are decoded to PCM with *Number of channels* at 7.0 or 7.1, instead of mixing it into the two back speakers. Other receivers and tracks are as before.
- **Surround sound through a USB-C adapter or hub** (*System > Audio > Surround sound through this USB-C adapter or hub*, Expert, off). Some hubs and DisplayPort to HDMI adapters tell the computer that the TV takes stereo only, even when the TV or the sound bar behind them takes more. With this on, the sound driver is told that what is behind the adapter takes PCM in 8 channels, Dolby Digital, Dolby Digital Plus and Dolby TrueHD; use it only if your TV or sound bar takes them, or a film plays without sound. It is shown only where such a converter is behind the port of Intel graphics, it acts only where the adapter declares stereo and nothing else, never for a DisplayPort monitor, and it takes effect from the next start: Hades writes it into the boot menus and asks whether to restart, and an update keeps it. After the finding of Felix Oghină (pull request #29).

**Streams, discs and files**

- **Blu-ray images that an add-on streams play as discs** when the address that the add-on gives does not end in `.iso`: Hades looks at where the address leads, at the type of the file or at its name. After an idea of mephis.
- **Seeking no longer stalls on a server that allows one connection per file** (61 s for each seek before, then the player gave up; now 1.5 to 1.8 s, measured), and an address whose token has run out is asked again of the add-on, so playback goes on. After an idea of mephis.
- **Disc images on the internet open faster:** their file system stays mounted a minute after the last read and is read in pieces of 128 KiB, and the folder beside them is no longer listed for extra audio or subtitles. Keeping the file system mounted, and reading it in pieces, are after an idea of mephis.
- **A change of subtitle track shows the new track at once,** from what was read, without reading the file again (PGS and text subtitles). Changing an embedded subtitle of a file also no longer interrupts the sound and the picture (Alexander Golovanov's change in Kodi), and the line on the screen is found at once, as after a seek. The idea of a change without a jump is mephis's.
- **Subtitles moved by a fixed step:** `SubtitleShiftUp(step)` and `SubtitleShiftDown(step)` move them a thirty-second of the screen's height at a time, for a remote or a keymap that sends single presses. After an idea of CorentinB.
- **Which variety of a language goes first** (*Settings > Player > Language > Variety of Spanish, Portuguese, French or Chinese*). When a video has both, Castilian or Latin American Spanish, European or Brazilian Portuguese, French from France or from Canada, and simplified or traditional Chinese, for the audio and the subtitles. Automatic follows the region of your language settings, and a track that you choose for a video still wins. After an idea of mephis.
- **A video no longer starts behind its clock.** At the start of a video and after a seek, the video waited for the clock while it asked the decoder for more pictures, which could make it take the start of the clock up to half a second late with hardware decoding. `kodi.log` says how long the video waited. After an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- **A seek in a file without sound, or without video, no longer waits a second.** Found by CroqueMr.
- **A Blu-ray menu page already shown takes the range of the disc's HDR playlist,** where the start of the disc said SDR: it stayed dim until the disc drew it again. Found by fandangos.
- **Blu-ray:** a menu button that links to a chapter no longer restarts the title, Java menus close without waiting for timers that a disc left running, and the service of the Java menus stops at once when it is told to (three fixes of libbluray after 1.5.1).
- **Kodi 22:** complex ASS subtitles are drawn within the texture limit of the graphics, without seams (vincent71711); picture subtitles are made again when the video output changes (Garrett Brown); the skin is loaded again when the screen changes to another shape (Marcin); channel up and down work again after a change of group in the guide, the channels window and the group manager (ksooo); a demuxer that has nothing to give no longer keeps a processor core busy, and the live channels of add-ons start sooner (Kel Modderman); two fixes of the information dialog (olympia); and the buttons of the game pad icons of Estuary the right way round (Chris).
- **Kodi 22, the latest:** a script that will not stop no longer keeps Kodi from exiting, and scripts started by their path are told to stop (Kel Modderman); stopping a video while the refresh rate changes no longer hangs Kodi (Kel Modderman; Hades does the same where it changes the mode itself for native Dolby Vision); a game in full screen is shown a frame sooner (Chris; Hades keeps it waiting for the graphics to finish each picture); the library keeps the label and order of a filter after playback, and list items are held while the thumbnail loader works on them (olympia); a plugin folder of the library is no longer taken for a disc (Kel Modderman); and the versions of a film in Estuary's lists (jjd-uk).
- **Add-ons of LibreELEC's repository get their settings again** (LibreELEC's fix): Kodi 22 saves them as version 4, and the start scripts of the add-ons that run a service read only versions 2 and 3, so they started with none of their settings.

**The start and the system**

- **One picture from the boot menu to the interface.** On a UEFI PC, the picture of the start (the eclipse over the name, on black) is on the screen from the moment an entry of the boot menu starts until Kodi shows its interface: no black box over the menu, no cursor, no line of text. The line of the version shows only with an error, also with the diagnostics on: it is in every folder of the diagnostics, and `hades.showversion` on the kernel line brings it back. Errors, an update and a check of a disk still show their text. On a BIOS PC only the line of the version goes. The new picture of the boot menu also comes with an update made from the system itself.
- **When Kodi cannot start, the screen says so.** After three stops of Kodi in a row, the screen shows a card in English and Spanish, over the picture of the start, with what to do for a report and the version. It never shows in a normal start. A kernel panic is drawn on the screen too, on AMD and Intel graphics.
- **The picture of the start stays until Kodi, on every display at its own size.** Where the graphics driver loads after the start (NVIDIA), the screen went black for some fifteen seconds before Kodi; the picture is now drawn again as soon as the driver has its frame buffer. With displays of different sizes (a TV and monitors), each one gets the picture at its own size, in its middle, in the mode it already has, and where the driver is there before the start (most AMD and Intel graphics), from the first frame; before, they all showed one picture of the size of the smallest, small in the upper left corner.
- **Kodi starts sooner where the clock of the PC is right.** With *Wait for network before starting Kodi* on, as it is by default, Kodi also waited for the time from the internet, 1 to 7 s after the network was up on a PC. Where an earlier start found the clock of the computer right (within 30 s), Kodi now starts as soon as the network is up, and the time comes a moment later. Where the clock was not found right, or may have changed since (a flat battery, a change of summer time or of the time zone, more than a month), Kodi waits for the time as before. The longest wait is the setting's either way.
- **The diagnostics time the network at the start.** With the diagnostic switch on, the journal has each step of DHCP, and the report gives the link, the address, the network and the time in seconds from the start of the kernel.
- **The notices of the start on every display at its size.** "LibreELEC Hades shows on one display" and the notice that Kodi could not start are cards, in English and Spanish, drawn on each display at its own size, and they stay until Kodi shows its interface.
- **The clock of the PC is read as it was measured to keep** (*Settings > LibreELEC > System > OS Configuration > Hardware clock*, Automatic by default, issue #22). Once the time has been taken from the internet, Hades compares it with the clock of the PC, and from the next start reads that clock as what it keeps: local time, as Windows keeps it, or UTC, as a PC without Windows does. It never writes the clock, so Windows keeps the right time. "Local time" and "UTC" still set it by hand, and Automatic never overrides them. A PC whose clock keeps UTC no longer shows the wrong time until the internet sets it.
- **NVIDIA: the return to 3840x2160 at 120 Hz after a video.** When the menus need the HDMI 2.1 link (FRL), videos now stay on that link instead of switching to the older TMDS link and back, which on an RTX 4080 SUPER with an LG C5 left the screen without a picture about once in nine returns. If the picture still does not come back, the menus move to 3840x2160 at 60 Hz until the next video, so that it comes back without a restart, and the resolution you chose is kept. A change from TMDS to FRL that is still made (at the start, or after the menus moved to 60 Hz) turns the output off first. Where the display engine does not finish a change of mode over FRL within a second, the link is trained again as NVIDIA's driver does for a link in use. Driver 620 also tells the firmware of the graphics that a display can stretch its refresh only where its mode has variable refresh rate, as driver 615 did.
- **More in the logs about the HDMI 2.1 changes of mode on NVIDIA:** what the display says after each change, its error counters, and why the driver refuses a change; and `kodi.log` and the journal are written to disk as soon as the picture stalls, so that a diagnostic report has them.
- **NVIDIA with two or three displays: shorter stalls at a change of mode.** With several displays connected, a change of mode could hold the picture for about nine seconds: the driver waited for each display in turn, three seconds each, and a display that was coming or going waited behind it. The displays of one change now share one wait of three seconds at most, and a display that comes or goes, or whose content protection changes, no longer waits in the queue of the driver's events. Also with driver 580, for the GTX 900 and 10 series (it has no events of content protection).
- **Intel graphics and AV receivers** (issue #28). An AV receiver that switches to another input and back now gets its scrambled HDMI link set up again once it is ready, instead of at once, so the picture comes back; and `i915.hdmi_empty_vsif=0` on the kernel line stops sending the HDMI vendor InfoFrame where it carries nothing, for a receiver that keeps showing the frame rate of an earlier video. Thanks to corgeone for the measurements.
- **A TV that does not lock onto its link after it is turned on again** (issue #24). Where a TV that is back after being turned off has the setup of a scrambled HDMI link from NVIDIA graphics but does not lock onto it, the output is set up again, once, with the limits of before: a TV that shows the picture is left alone, and nothing is done during native Dolby Vision. `kodi.log` says what the TV's SCDC said.
- **The clock no longer runs slow when the PC's HPET stops** (issue #22). When the HPET timer stops while it checks the processor's clock (the TSC), Linux marked the TSC unstable and moved the clock of the system onto that HPET: on an FX-8350 the clock then ran 13 % slow, the sound broke up and the PC hung. Now the HPET is the one set aside, the next timer checks the TSC, and the kernel log says so.
- **AMD graphics through a passive DisplayPort adaptor:** SDR of 8 bits goes out at 8 bits, as Windows and Intel graphics send it through such adaptors, instead of the 12 bits that the driver took at 1080p; and when a 3D display gets no 3D mode on a DisplayPort port, `kodi.log` says whether an active converter is the reason (issue #26).
- **Chromebooks with Intel graphics.** The drivers of the ChromeOS embedded controller and of the DisplayPort mode of USB-C are in, as modules that load only on Chromebooks and Framework laptops, so that a hub or an adapter on a Chromebook's USB-C port gets a picture. The device file of the controller and its other files for programs are left out: nothing in the system needs them, and they would let any program with full rights send it commands. Based on the work of Felix Oghină (pull request #29).
- **Wi-Fi and Bluetooth of more adapters:** the firmware of MediaTek MT7902, MT7915, MT7916, MT7615, MT7663 and MT7668, and of Qualcomm WCN785x and WCN6855 on every board, for drivers that the image already had. A MediaTek Bluetooth controller whose firmware does not exist is no longer reset over and over, and the *Installer* entry of the boot menu loads no Bluetooth driver.
- **The diagnostics of a start that stops** (issue #14). While the diagnostic switch is on, the boot menus start the kernel with a line in the journal for each driver that it starts, and the journal has room for the whole start, so a start that stops while drivers load leaves the driver it was in. Until the journal of a start is in the storage, the kernel's log of the start is copied there every second, so that a start that hangs in its first seconds leaves its log in `previous-boot/early-kernel-log.txt.gz` of the next start, with how far it got. `previous-boot/NONE.txt` says why a folder has no journal of the start before, `interrupts.txt` lists the interrupts of the devices, each snapshot says what the clock of the PC keeps, and the wait for the NVIDIA driver writes in the kernel log where that driver is. The Windows installer's "Save diagnostics there" sets this from the very first start. The report also lists the HDMI pins of each sound codec and the state of each stream that plays. The copy of the kernel log while a start runs is after an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- **Harder to attack:** OpenSSL 4 and 3 are built with the compiler's full protections, and Python with the light ones, where they cost nothing measurable (at most 1 % more instructions in TLS, and less in Python); the programs of Samba are position-independent. `i915.enable_dv_lab=0` on the kernel line no longer marks the kernel as tainted.
- **Restart into Windows:** on a PC that starts by UEFI and has Windows, the power menu has an entry that starts Windows at the next start, and only at that one.
- **Restart into LibreELEC Hades from Windows:** a button in the installer, next to a drive or disk that has Hades and on its last screen after installing or updating (and the link in its side bar), restarts the PC into Hades once, without the boot menu key; Windows asks for permission each time, and starts again at the start after that. A box next to the button also puts a shortcut that does the same on the desktop, only if you tick it.
- **Back to Hades where Windows comes first:** *Restart LibreELEC Hades* in the power menu, the first start of a new install, and the restart after an update, a reset or a restore, come back to Hades also where Windows comes first in the PC; before, they ended in Windows.
- Both restarts only set the next start of the PC (`BootNext`, once). They never add, remove or reorder the PC's boot entries.
- **The power menu names its restarts by what they do:** "Restart LibreELEC Hades" restarts the PC and comes back to LibreELEC Hades, and "Restart the interface" restarts only the interface; "Restart into Windows" is as before.
- **The installer removes Hades from a USB drive too,** and leaves it as an empty exFAT drive.
- **The published installer no longer carries its self-tests.** They, and what they use (a key made only for them, certificates and small disk images), are in a second program for developers, `LibreELEC-Hades-Installer-tests.exe`, built from the same sources. Installing, updating and removing are the same code in both.
- **Secure Boot, optional and off by default** (*Start with Secure Boot* in the installer for Windows). The installer makes a key for that PC alone and signs GRUB and the kernel with it; the private key stays in the Hades storage. The PC asks once, on a blue screen, to enroll it with a 4-digit code that the installer shows. Hades starts through shim and MokManager of Debian 13, signed by Microsoft with its 2011 and 2023 certificates, and the kernel locks itself down, so only signed drivers load. An update made inside Hades signs the new kernel and boot loader on the PC itself, or is not applied. It never writes the PC's Secure Boot databases (db, KEK, PK, dbx), its boot order or anything of Windows, and the installer says before anything what it writes and what it does not. The boot menu looks the same with Secure Boot.
- **Kernel lockdown:** ACPI tables from an initrd and `acpi_rsdp=` are refused again under `lockdown=integrity`; since Linux 6.12 they got through, because the check came before the lockdown module was ready.

**Up to date**

- **Linux 7.3.5.** It turns the protection against Branch History Injection back on for the Intel processors that need it (7.3.4 left it off in a kernel built without KVM, as this one is), keeps the refresh rate of AMD graphics exact while variable refresh is off, and fixes Bluetooth L2CAP, the NVMe and SATA drivers and the memory management. The two fixes for Intel Wildcat Lake graphics and one of the NTFS fixes that Hades carried are part of it now.
- **Kodi 22:** the `Piers` branch up to `51b30cf19be9`, with the changes named above.
- **FFmpeg 9.0.2 with its stable branch up to `8c7ef49002ff`:** a CAF, AIFF, MOV or MP4 file whose header claims millions of sound channels no longer makes it take and clear up to 2 GB of memory before it reads them (Marvin Scholz).
- **FFmpeg: 73 fixes from its development branch that `release/9.0` does not have yet** (`ffmpeg-9970`, with their authors): among them wrong pixels in some H.264 pictures decoded in software, an HTTP connection used again for another host, an HLG flag of a HEVC stream that stayed on after an SDR part, HE-AAC sound after a seek, VAAPI decoding of HEVC with many tiles, the default colours of a Dolby Vision RPU without display management, and crashes, leaks and hangs in the MP4, MPEG-TS, DASH, HLS, HTTP, HEVC, AV1, VP9, AAC, FLAC and PGS code. The Dolby Vision and HDR10+ metadata that FFmpeg gives the player are the same, byte for byte, in every Dolby Vision and HDR10+ video of the regression inputs (measured).
- **LibreELEC `master` up to `3a98afddff5b`:** dbus 1.16.4 (two denial-of-service fixes of the system bus, CVE-2026-120749), Intel's media driver 26.4.2 (hardware decoding of H.264 from Tiger Lake on refuses an output smaller than the picture instead of writing past it), eSpeak NG with about thirty memory fixes, and the add-on repository 12.80.8 (every one of its 238 add-ons loads in the image, measured).
- **Security fixes:** glibc up to `c90470a5a096` of its 2.44 branch (CVE-2026-100290 and CVE-2026-115819, and two fixes of `malloc`); OpenSSL 4.1 and 3.5 with the fixes of the advisory of 17 November 2026 (CVE-2026-35190, CVE-2026-107493 and CVE-2026-124709) and of their stable branches; BusyBox, with two fixes in the probe of disks at start (CVE-2026-124743 and CVE-2026-124746: a crafted partition on a disk that is connected no longer holds the start up or makes it read out of bounds) and one in `tar` (CVE-2026-124745: an update file can no longer make a hard link through a symbolic link that the same file made); dav1d 1.5.5, with a fix of an out-of-bounds read in AV1 decoding at 10 and 12 bits on processors with AVX-512.
- **libplacebo `f2e04cb1840e`** (the picture of the Dolby Vision bridge is the same, byte for byte, measured; what changed upstream is motion estimation, which Hades does not use) and **flex 2.6.16**.

**For skins, add-ons and tests**

- **Tests in the tree.** `tools/regress` now carries the checks of the Dolby Vision bridge (what it sends, measures and draws, byte for byte on a software graphics chip), of the player information and its language files, of Kodi's output logic, of the enhancement layer of profile 7, of the FFmpeg patches on FATE and of the boot menus that the installer writes, each proven by a mutant that must make it fail; one script runs them all.
- The strings of Hades moved to a block of their own, 20000 above their numbers in 1.0, away from those of Kodi, after an idea of YBLOD. A skin that names a string of Hades by its number needs the new one.

### Details

**The player information**

- Kodi's skin: the pages are made from Estuary's in `system/hades` for every skin, as before; one frame of 820 lines for every page (the height of the longest, the Dolby Vision page in the longest language), with the bar at the bottom; Down from a tab goes to the button of its page (`5562`, the upscaler's, just above the one of the bar) or of the bar (`5561`) where it is shown and closes the panel where it is not, Up from the button goes back to the tab it came from, and OK on Overview and Dolby Vision asks for the output. The System page reads `NV_OUTPUT_LINK` and `NV_OUTPUT_BPC` of nvidia-drm (FRL with its lanes and rate, or TMDS with its character clock and whether it is scrambled), `VRR_ENABLED`, `vrr_capable` and `NV_HDMI_VRR_OFF`, the version of the graphics driver (`/sys/module/nvidia/version`, or the DRM interface of amdgpu, i915 and xe) and of the kernel, twice a second and only while the panel is open; amdgpu and i915 say no link, and the page says "not said by the driver". The generator of the pages stops if a middle dot, a multiplication sign or an arrow is left in them.
- The output of a video: `OutputChoice::SDR` for Dolby Vision, HDR10, HDR10+ and HLG where the output would send HDR, mapped as on a display without HDR with the tone mapping of the video for the display of *HDR to SDR: the display it is made for*; Dolby Vision is rebuilt and mapped where it can be (profile 5 always, 7 and 8.1 with "Reference (BT.2390)"), otherwise its HDR10 picture is mapped. The choice is kept for the file (value 8).
- The speakers (`hades.audio.spk`): `ActiveAE::Configure()` clears what the player told Kodi's data cache of its stream (`CDataCacheCore::ResetAudioCache()`) whenever the engine's own format changes; `CVideoPlayerAudio::KeepStreamInfo()` gives the layout, rate, bits, decoder and presentation again from its own copies, and a stream that the player synchronised before it reported its start gives them with its first frame played. `hades.audio.spkfrom` marks the usual layout of the stream's channel count (the standard layouts of `CAEUtil::GuessChLayout()`). The upscaler from the player information: `hades.video.upscaler` and `hades.video.upscalerscope`, and `PlayerControl(HadesUpscaler)` for the list.

**Picture**

- Tone mapping: `CMediaSettings` starts its defaults with "Reference (BT.2390)"; a one-time migration (`hadestonemap` 2) turns the saved "Off" of 1.0 into it, and a one-time pass (`hadestonemapfiles` 1) does the same in the settings of each file in the video database, with the count in `kodi.log`. A method out of range is no longer cut to `VS_TONEMAPMETHOD_MAX`. Rules in `xbmc/settings/ToneMapDefault.h`.
- SDR in BT.2020 (`videoscreen.hadessdrbt2020`): the composition of the interface has a mode into SDR of BT.2020 primaries, after ITU-R BT.2087 case #1 (the BT.1886 EOTF, the BT.709 to BT.2020 matrix in linear light, and back), mixed by coverage as Kodi mixes in SDR. It is set up only where the setting is on, the picture is SDR with BT.2020 primaries, the output is Kodi's ordinary one, the EDID declares `BT2020_RGB` and the connector offers it in `Colorspace`, and neither native Dolby Vision nor 3D has the output; during a video it goes back to BT.709, with a warning, if any of that changes. Off, not a pixel changes. Without tone mapping, the conversion of primaries now goes back through the gamma it came in by (2.4 for BT.2020), the YUV matrix gives full range and the shader applies 16-235 after the conversion (`KODI_RANGE_AFTER_CONVERSION`), and a composited frame is cleared to the black of limited range (`GuiCompositeClearBlack()`).
- A refused BT.2020 tag: where the driver refuses a request that changes the colour tag of the output to BT.2020 (`Colorspace` `BT2020_RGB`, for HDR or for SDR in BT.2020), `kodi.log` says so, and whether the same request with the tag that the output has now is taken, that is, whether the tag was the cause (`CDRMAtomic::TellBt2020Refused()`, a test on a copy, once until a colour tag goes out); the request before it goes out in its place, as before. After an idea of CroqueMr's build.
- Upscalers (`videoplayer.hadesupscalerhd`, `hadesupscalersd`, `hadesupscaleranime`): mpv user shaders, RAVU lite r4 by Bin Jin and FSRCNNX x2 8-0-4-1 and 16-0-4-1 by igv (LGPL-3.0-or-later, unchanged, in `system/hades/upscalers` with their licence texts), run by libplacebo in the bridge (`pl_mpv_user_shader_parse`), because Kodi's GLES renderer converts and scales in one pass and cannot run a shader on the luma plane. Used only where the video is drawn more than 1.3 times its size in width and height (1.41 for RAVU); anything else keeps Kodi's path, byte for byte. FSR 1, Anime4K, NNEDI3, the EWA filters and ArtCNN were measured and left out.
- HDR10 scene by scene (`videoscreen.hadeshdr10scenes`): the GPU measurement of HDR10 sent as Dolby Vision (cuts, and the level 1 of each scene, two pictures ahead) gives libplacebo the scene's brightest and average light (`max_pq_y`, `avg_pq_y`) in place of the film's static metadata; within a scene the values follow a change only past 32 of 4095 PQ codes, at 4 codes a picture (`dvbridge_scene_map.c`).
- Video at its own rate with VRR (`videoscreen.hadesvrrvideomode`: 0 off, the default; 1 where the display has no mode at the video's rate; 2 always; `VRR_VIDEO::Mode`): "always" is asked before Kodi's choice of a mode (`TakesBeforeKodi()`), "where no mode" after it (`Automatic()`). The boolean of the first builds of 1.1 (`videoscreen.hadesvrrvideo`) stays hidden for them, and is taken over when the settings load: on is "always", off is off (`settings/SettingTakeOver.h`, the rule of the two Dolby Vision settings that took over booleans, now one list in `CSettings`). Only SDR and HDR10 known to be progressive, not 3D; the rate is the smallest whole multiple of the frame rate that is 5 % above the display's VRR minimum. NVIDIA holds each flip until its time in the display engine (`NV_FLIP_TIME_RAW_NS`, the layer time stamp of NVKMS converted with the GPU-CPU time correlation of the driver, `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9076`), the RTX 50 series uses Media VRR (`NV_VRR_MEDIA`, `NV_VRR_MEDIA_TARGET`), and amdgpu waits for each time with `clock_nanosleep()`. Kodi's clock follows the times of the flips; any change of mode, native Dolby Vision or the end of the video turns VRR off again. Where the driver does not hold flips, Kodi sees it within 48 intervals and times them itself. Measured on a loaded host, the software timer was not exact to 1 ms (99th percentile 4.2 to 5.0 ms); the held flip and Media VRR can be measured only on hardware.
- VRR checked: `VRR_VIDEO::CStartCheck` reads `VRR_ENABLED` of the CRTC after each flip and, where nvidia-drm has it, `NV_VRR_ACTIVE` (`nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9079`: bit 0 whether NVKMS has VRR active on the head, bits 8 to 15 the type of VRR of its timings): the first flip is left out, a 0 after it turns VRR off at once and three flips with 1 keep it, where the signal confirms it: the out-fence of each paced flip, read with the next one, against the time asked for it (`CStartCheck::FenceInterval()`); where more than a quarter of the intervals are off by over 1 ms, as a fixed refresh puts them, it is `OUTPUT_FIXED`, and where the mode's rate is nearly a whole multiple of the video's (71.928 Hz in 359.999 Hz) the fences cannot tell and the driver's word holds. Elsewhere it counts the vertical blanks of the CRTC between flips in the first second of a video, and with neither it does not keep VRR. Where the refresh does not follow the flips, the mode changes as without VRR (`TriggerUpdateResolution()`), and `hades.out.vrr` says `checking`, `follows`, `samerate`, `fixed` or `nomode` (the mode is no whole multiple of the video's rate, within 200 ppm). `VRR_VIDEO::Paced()` paces wherever a video is on its way to the screen (`IsRenderingVideo()`), not only a video on a layer of its own.
- HDMI VRR (`videoscreen.hadeshdmivrr`, off by default): `NV_HDMI_VRR_OFF` on every mode set that Kodi makes while it is off (`CHdmiVrrOff::Reason()`: the setting first, then HDR10+), shown only where the connector has the property; `CHdmiVrrOff::Allowed()` leaves it to the driver where *Video at its own rate with VRR* takes the display (`VRR_VIDEO::VrrInModeSets()`: every mode set with "always", those of a display without a mode at the rate of films with "where no mode", none with it off), and Media VRR in the mode sets of the menus follows the same rule. A change of the two settings while the menus show goes out at once, in a mode set of its own (`CHdmiVrrOff::ModesetForSetting()`, `CWinSystemGbm::StageHdmiVrrOff()`); during a video, with the mode set at its end. The end of native Dolby Vision puts `NV_HDMI_VRR_OFF` back as the settings ask then (`CHdmiVrrOff::AtHandBack()`, an intent of `CWinSystemGbm::HandBackIntents()`), not as it found it, so a setting changed during the video costs no second mode set right after the one that ends it. A mode of the menus set with VRR off is not used for a video at its own rate until the next change of mode, and the log says so.
- Where no mode: `VRR_VIDEO::Automatic()` takes a video at its own rate with VRR where Kodi's own choice (`CResolutionUtils::ChooseBestResolution()`) stays in the mode of the menus, that mode is no whole multiple of the video's rate, and no mode of the display at any resolution is at the video's rate or twice it (`DisplayHasModeFor()`, within 200 ppm); on NVIDIA the mode sets of the menus leave HDMI VRR to the driver where the display declares VRR and has no mode at 23.976 Hz or twice it (`VRR_VIDEO::AutoForDisplay()`). HDR10+: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9082` gives the connector `NV_HDR10PLUS_WITH_VRR`, 1 where the HDMI route of HDR10+ does not need VRR off (shared generic slot 2 next to the EMP in the generic one on AD10x, shared slot 9 on Blackwell: `nvHadesHdr10PlusHdmiSlot()`); there `VRR_VIDEO::Decide()` takes HDR10+ and `CHdmiVrrOff::Wanted()` no longer asks for VRR off in the mode set of an HDR10+ video.
- Dolby Vision that does not go out natively (rebuilt, or sent as HDR10, HDR10+ or SDR) is taken by `VRR_VIDEO::Decide()` as HDR10+ is, where `NV_HDR10PLUS_WITH_VRR` is 1 and it cannot go out as HLG (`DVBRIDGE::StreamOutputMayBeHlg()`: an HLG stream or base layer, profiles 8.4 and 10.4). Native Dolby Vision is refused with its reason (`NativeDolbyVisionWhy()`: a transport of 3840x2160 at a CTA rate of up to 60 Hz, and no display that declares Dolby Vision with VRR) and not asked again after Kodi's choice (`AskedAfterKodi()`). A video that *Always* refused only because the mode of the menus was set with HDMI VRR off keeps the mode it got until it ends (`RefusedForModeSetOnly()`).
- The rate shown with VRR: `VRR_VIDEO::CFlipRate`, the median of the last 96 intervals between the out-fences of flips asked one slot apart (the vblanks without an out-fence are left out), and `ShownRefresh()`: with VRR confirmed and measured, the rate asked for where the measure agrees with it within 2 %, the measure where it does not, and the mode's until it is measured. `KODI::UTILS::VrrModeText()` writes the mode row with "(VRR)"; the System page takes `hades.out.modeplain`, as its VRR row is just above it; the VRR row is `RowValue()` (`hades.out.vrrtext`, Estuary #54307 to #54309). `kodi.log` gives the VRR minimum of the display at the start of each video and the measured rate at its end.
- A mode at the video's rate at another size (`windowing/ExactRateElsewhere.h`, `CResolutionUtils::FindExactRateElsewhere()`): without a whitelist of your own, after Kodi's tiers of the exact rate and of twice it at the size of the video and at the width of the menus, and before 3:2 pulldown at the width of the menus, a progressive 2D mode of the kind of the menus at the rate (then twice it, within Kodi's 0.01 Hz) not smaller than the video (the margins of `MatchesMode()`): the size of `RES_DESKTOP` first, then the smallest at or above the menus, then the largest under them. Only SDR in 2D; native Dolby Vision (`FindDVTransportResolution()`, the rule of cadence), HDR10, HDR10+, HLG and a video whose kind is not known are not looked at, and a video not known to be progressive takes no mode of 30 Hz or less but 24 Hz.
- HEVC progressive by its picture timing: `PROGRESSIVE_VIDEO::Check()` says `byPictureTiming` where `frame_field_info_present_flag` is all that bars a HEVC stream; `CDVDDemuxFFmpeg::PrefetchForPictureTiming()` reads the prefix SEIs of the base layer of its first access units before the streams go to the player (1.5 s, 4800 packets of all the streams and 48 MiB at most, `PROGRESSIVE_VIDEO::PictureTimingRead`, which says in `kodi.log` the limit that stopped the read; 1200 stopped a profile 7 film with TrueHD at 19 of its 24 access units; the packets are given back in order by `ReadFrame()`), and `CPictureTimingProof` makes it progressive (`CDemuxStreamVideo::progressiveByTiming`) where 24 access units with a `pic_timing` say `pic_struct` 0, 7 or 8 with a `source_scan_type` other than 0, and none says otherwise. A plain input from the start of a file only, not with the two tracks of Dolby Vision joined; native Dolby Vision output does not take it (`CVideoPlayer::OpenVideoStream()`). It is read only where its verdict can change the choice of a mode (`PROGRESSIVE_VIDEO::PictureTimingNotNeeded()`): with *Adjust display refresh rate* on, and *Video at its own rate with VRR* not off, or whole-number multiples with a whitelist of your own, or, without a whitelist, where the frame rate, twice it or 2.5 times it is a refresh rate of 30 Hz or less but 24 Hz; elsewhere no packet is read for it, and `kodi.log` says why. Where the settings or the frame rate are not known, it is read.
- The mode of the menus of each display (`windowing/gbm/DisplayModeMemory.h`): kept by the make and model of its EDID in `special://profile/hades-display-modes.txt`, noted whenever `videoscreen.screenmode` is set with that display in use, and taken by `CDisplaySettings::GetDisplayResolution()` (a start, a move of Kodi to another output, a change of the display's modes); a display new to Kodi gets `DESKTOP`, its preferred mode. With one display, as before.
- *Adjust display resolution* (`videoplayer.hadesadjustresolution`, `windowing/VideoSizeMode.h`): after Kodi's whole choice in `CResolutionUtils::ChooseBestResolution()` (and after the two places where VRR may take the video, which end it in the mode of the menus), the allowed resolution of the kind of the one chosen (`D3DPRESENTFLAG_MODEMASK`) at its refresh rate within 0.01 Hz whose size is the video's by Kodi's rule (`MatchesMode()`, which now calls `VIDEO_SIZE::OfVideoSize()`), the nearest size and then the nearest rate. Not with a whitelist of your own, nor for native Dolby Vision: the HDMI policy of the tunnel takes CTA 1080p (`drm_dvbridge.h`), but the engine of the bridge and the window of native output are made for a raster of 3840x2160 (`DVPlaybackPolicy.h`, `dvbridge_source.c`, `dvbridge_render.c`). Core #62145 and #62146, Estuary #54145 and #54146.
- With *Video at its own rate with VRR*: `VIDEO_SIZE::ChooseForVrr()` before the two places where VRR may take the video, with `CWinSystemBase::VideoModeLink()` (the display's VRR range, NVKMS's 50 Hz, 720 lines and 110 % of its minimum, over FRL kept for the menus no mode under `FRL_KEEP::MIN_KEPT_KHZ`, which would go over TMDS, and, over TMDS, a ceiling of 600 MHz: a video at its own rate never changes the link). The mode of the video's size with VRR, nearest in size and then of the highest rate, goes to `TakeVideoAtOwnRate()` as the mode of the video (`sized`), whose mode set allows HDMI VRR; where the display has modes of that size and none with VRR, VRR is not asked (`ForgetVideoAtOwnRate()`), unless no mode of that size is at the rate either, when it goes in the mode of the menus as before; without a mode of that size at all, the menus as before.
- HDMI FRL kept only from 296 MHz: `FRL_KEEP::ForMode()` leaves `NV_HDMI_FRL_KEEP` at 0 in the mode set of a mode under `MIN_KEPT_KHZ` (296 MHz, just under the 296.7 MHz of 3840x2160 at 23.976 Hz), which then goes over TMDS as NVKMS chooses; the way back to the menus over FRL turns the output off first (`FRL_MODE_CHANGE::OffFirst()`) and, where its flips do not come back, goes to a TMDS mode of the size of the menus (`Fallback()`). A change of mode from TMDS back to FRL that does not finish stops the flips, which Kodi sees and undoes; a mode kept over FRL gives the source no such sign, since NVKMS, the display engine and the display's SCDC report it the same way whatever the display shows. Modes of 296 MHz and more (3840x2160 at 23.976 to 30 Hz, 1920x1080 at 100 and 120 Hz) stay on FRL as before. `kodi.log`: `HDMI FRL: ... is not kept on FRL`.
- The logs of a change of mode: the line of each one says how many ms after the one before it it went, where that was under 10 s, and the hot-plug of the display that follows a change of mode writes `kodi.log` and the journal to the disk, as a stall of the flips does, so the end of both logs is on the disk even if the PC is switched off right after.
- The modes of the display read again after a hot-plug event (`ModeListRefresh.h`): the same EDID with other modes is taken as other modes (`MODELIST::Of()`), as nvidia-drm offers the modes of the HDMI FRL link it assessed (`nvHdmiFrlAssessLink()`), which a hot-plug event assesses again.
- The outputs that Kodi does not use (`CDRMAtomic::DisableOtherOutputs()`): a change of mode refused while they wait for the end of the notice on them turns them off with the next commit, which carries the mode; a commit that is due (a refused change of mode asked for again, those outputs turned off) gets a frame where the interface draws none (`CDRMAtomic::CommitDue()`, `CWinSystemGbm::AskFrameForCommit()`); the wait for the audio engine to name the outputs of its sound is looked at each second, 30 s at most as before.
- The interface over HDR (`videoscreen.hadesguicurve`: BT.1886 by default, 2.2, or sRGB as Kodi): the curve that takes the interface into light, in the exact composition, in its tables and in the packer's `GUI_COMPOSITE`; the primaries (ITU-R BT.2087) and the white (203 cd/m² times *GUI white*) are as before. `kodi.log` says what goes out and with which curve.
- Limited range: after two refusals of "Limited 16:235" Kodi asks the driver for "Full" and compresses once; who compresses follows what the kernel has, so the interface is not compressed twice when the setting changes during a video.
- The windows that open over a video (`DialogPlayerProcessInfo`, the OSD) are loaded also while a video starts and draws nothing yet, and each open over a video says in `kodi.log` what it cost the thread that presents the pictures, where it took 12 ms or more or read its file then. `videoscreen.hadeskodiplayerinfo` opens `DialogPlayerProcessInfoKodi.xml`, Kodi 22's panel unchanged, before Hades' pages.
- The temperatures: `CBackgroundReading` (`xbmc/utils/BackgroundReading.h`) gives the last reading without waiting and starts one on the job manager where it is due, one at a time; `CSystemGUIInfo` reads only the temperature asked for, the thread of the GUI never waits for it, and other threads (add-ons, JSON-RPC) wait as before. The panel asks for the temperatures that it names when its XML is loaded ahead, and the first publication of its values after it opens is timed in `kodi.log`. `HadesBarCpuVar` leaves out Kodi's "Not available" (string 10005), and `tools/regress/playerinfo` measures it.

**Dolby Vision**

- `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9074` gives `NV_HDMI_VRR_OFF` to every connector with a TMDS output, on every GPU of the open driver (it existed only where HDR10+ needed it: Turing, and Ampere without the firmware flag `HDMI21_SW_ACR_BUG_3275257`). Its default, 0, changes nothing until a client asks.
- Level 5: `dvbridge_dv_made_area()` makes no block where the four margins of the picture are 0, deciding on the place of the picture and not on what is drawn over the bars; Hades' own metadata write their own level 5 (`dvbridge_profile81_metadata()`), so what those displays are sent is byte for byte as before. `DVBRIDGE_SENT_NO_L5` tells the player information.
- Level 5 of an HD picture: where the offsets fit both the picture and a 3840x2160 master, `dvbridge_dv_margins()` takes the smaller bars until the measurement of native output (`dvbridge_l5check.h`) finds the band between the two readings black on every side, with picture from edge to edge next to it, in 4 pictures over 3 s or more and none against; then it reads them as offsets of the picture (`dvbridge_dv::l5_picture`, a decision of the video, kept by `dvbridge_dv_restart()`), and one picture against undoes it for the rest of the video. One picture a second is measured in the first minute, then one at each cut (at most one a second) and one every 5 s, only in videos with such a band; a 3840x2160 picture does not take that path. The serializer, the zoom (`RenderManager::ActiveAreaOf()`) and the "Picture area" of the player information (`dvbridge_l5_offsets_area()`) take the same decision through `CDVBridgeGLES::ReadL5OfPicture()`. `DVCheckL5` reads the offsets and their bars from the metadata of each frame, in samples of the picture, so it measures under the zoom too. Test: section 11 of `serializer_test.c` in `tools/regress/dvbridge-meta`, whose 255 payloads of before stay byte for byte the same.
- Low-Latency: the target is judged before the signal is chosen (`DVLowLatencyTargetUsable()`, `DV_DISPLAY::LowLatencyWayOf()`). Primaries past BT.2020 (`dvbridge_ll_gamut_of()`): each is taken to the nearest point of the edge of BT.2020; measured in delta E ITP with the software bridge on 24 cases of real Dolby Vision blocks, against the triangle of largest area inside the intersection of the display and BT.2020, it gave a mean of 13.38 against 13.58, a 99th percentile of 54.48 against 54.85, and 4.11 against 4.56 on what the display can show (1.0 against 2.7 for the projector of issue #25). A block is refused where a primary is more than 0.075 past BT.2020 or 0.025 past the spectral locus, where the triangle is not valid, or where its intersection with BT.2020 is less than 0.40 of its area: of the 220 blocks with primaries in the corpus of EDIDs, 157 are taken as before, 63 at the edge and none refused. The black (`videoscreen.hadesdvllblackedid`) is the black level lift of BT.2390, with the black of the Dolby Vision block, 0.5 cd/m² at most; of 177 blocks of version 2, 40 give 0, the median is 0.035 and the largest 0.267.
- Frames ahead (`CDVAhead`): a run of 240 frames presented the way that waits counts where it had a late frame and a frame drawn without the interface in more than a refresh period of GPU time (`TimedWithoutGui()`); 7 of the last 30 turn frames ahead on. The rules of 1.0 come first, unchanged.
- The converter: `x86/linux-9890` takes as a candidate every DisplayPort port that is not eDP or LSPCON, the route still read from the DPCD; the list of boards counts only on a port that the VBT calls HDMI, and a new list of converters by their DPCD identity (OUI, device id, hardware and firmware revisions), empty for now. Kodi shows *Dolby Vision through a display converter* only where `DVBRIDGE_DV_CONVERTER` is not none.
- DP++ on NVIDIA: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9073` and `nvidia-legacy-open-9077` give the connector `NV_DUAL_MODE_ADAPTOR` (0 none reported, 1 or 2 an HDMI adaptor of type 1 or 2, 3 one that does not say HDMI); Kodi does not try native output where it says 3, and NVKMS refuses the Dolby vendor InfoFrame there.
- Radeon DCE: `x86/linux-9859` turns the dynamic expansion of the formatter off while the tunnel is on and judges it in the read back of the pipe, which read it before without judging it (`amdgpu.dvbridge_dce_expansion`, 0 by default); `x86/linux-9860` gives `DVBRIDGE_DUAL_MODE` to every DisplayPort connector of amdgpu and offers `amdgpu.dvbridge_dce` on the DisplayPort connectors of DCE 11.2 to 12. Kodi keeps the verdict of the last check of the output for each output, kernel and set of driver parameters (`OutputCheckVerdict.h`) and offers neither native output nor HDR over the tunnel where it was not exact.
- The check of the output (`hades-output-check` of hades-diag): for each value of the card's CRC, named by the colours whose bits change it, whether the measure broke there, and what breaks when one colour of the test picture changes at a time; the report names the kernel and the driver parameters that decide the Dolby Vision path.
- RPU: `ffmpeg-9935` keeps an RPU whose 0x80 terminator is not where its data end, nor after the padding byte of `ffmpeg-9910`, where the CRC32 computed at that place holds; it warns once for each decoder (`av_log_once`). An RPU with its terminator changes nothing. Tested in `tools/regress/rpu` with 26 hand-made RPUs under ASan and UBSan: those kept give the same `AVDOVIMetadata`, byte for byte, as with the terminator, and a wrong CRC is still refused.
- The enhancement layer of profile 7 (`tools/dvbridge/dvbridge_fel.c`): while it waits by picture order for a picture to start at, with no place being counted, a packet without enhancement units now empties the map of base pictures as one with them did, so 64 such packets in a row no longer fail the layer ("too many base pictures wait for their enhancement pictures") and make Kodi start it again. On the bench of the layer, with the enhancement units taken out of the first 70, 200 and 500 packets of a stream: 1, 3 and 7 restarts before, none now, with the same pictures paired; every other run of the bench gives the same pictures, byte for byte.
- Low-Latency and the interface (`<video><dvguiinplace>`, on, now also for Low-Latency): the packer of the converted transport composites the interface with Kodi's exact arithmetic, in the form with quadruples; a packet that fails gives a warning and the next frame is composited as before. `dvpasstimes` times it.
- Active area zoom (`videoplayer.hadesactiveareazoom`): `dvbridge_l5_area()` reads the level 5 with the serializer's own `dvbridge_dv_margins()`; no zoom without the video's own metadata, with no or two level 5 blocks, with offsets that fit no reading, with an area under 40 % of a side, or where the native measurement left that level 5 aside. A change at a cut that the RPU marks is taken at once (marks less than 0.5 s apart do not count as cuts), a larger area when 2 pictures in 4 bring it, a smaller one after 1 s, nothing under 1/256 of a side. The source rectangle stays the whole picture and the destination grows, as with Kodi's own zoom, so the ordinary, native and converted paths take it unchanged; it starts again on `Configure`, `Flush` and `DiscardBuffer`. The player information reads what is drawn and why from the renderer (`ACTIVEAREA::Shown`, kept where `kodi.log` is written, at a change), at most twice a second and only with the panel open. Test `TestActiveAreaZoom` (38 checks).
- Two streams of one video: `DualVerdictOf()` gives `HDR10PLUS_DISPLAY` for a profile 8.1 with its own HDR10+ on a display with HDR10+ and without Dolby Vision, where it would have been rebuilt, with the mechanism of `doviHdr10PlusWins`; with a choice made for the video, or the picture mapped to the display, it is as before. Test `dualhdr` in `tools/regress/kodi-host`.
- *Check this setup*: the HDR10 and HLG cards define their dark squares by the light of the SDR card at 203 cd/m², and the Dolby Vision cards carry the level 5 block that Hades writes for its own metadata.

**Sound**

- PCM bits: the most bits of LPCM that the ELD declares, read as the HDA codec reads them (`AE::PCM::MaxBitsOfSads()`), kept with the other audio data of the display; under 24, ActiveAE asks the sink for 16 bits, does not fall back to more, and lists no format of more than 16 bits.
- Kept outputs: the output that `hades-keep-outputs` keeps lit keeps its sound (`ActiveAEKeptOutputs.h`), matched with the sound card by PCI and with the display by its ELD and EDID.
- CEC: Kodi asks the kernel which connector a CEC adapter is on (`CEC_ADAP_G_CONNECTOR_INFO`) and compares it with the output of the picture (`PeripheralCecPicture.h`); for an adapter of another output, libCEC is set up without activating the source and without the TV among the devices to wake, Kodi's own requests are not sent, and `libcec-9015` adds `CECSetTakesNoTvInput()`, so that a routing change to the PC does not take the TV's input through that adapter only (an addition: nothing compiled against libCEC 8.1.7 changes). `libcec-9014`: closing the Linux adapter clears the logical addresses that it set. In Generic, Kodi no longer applies the patch that stops polling for adapters.
- AC-3 at 32 kHz: 3 words per kb/s, as A/52 table 5.18 gives (`AEStreamInfo.cpp`, `AudioPresentation.h`). The flush of the player waits only for the stream players that are open (`FlushBuffers()`: 1000 ms to 5 ms, measured).
- 6.1 and 6.0: the back centre goes to the back centre speaker that the ELD declares (RC), with a line in the log when the choice changes.
- Alder Lake: `x86/linux-9893`, only for the codec `0x8086281c`, with the PCM of the pin in use, a valid ELD and the pin's device selected: `PIN_OUT` set again in `i915_pin_cvt_fixup()`, keeping EPT and HBR, with a line in the kernel log.
- Device changes: a fourth event within a second is deferred instead of dropped (Kodi dropped it), and those that come while ActiveAE reconfigures are sent again when it is done.
- `audiooutput.hadespconaudio` writes `i915.pcon_hd_audio` into the boot menus, where `scripts/mkimage` writes `i915.pcon_hd_audio=0` on Run, internal and Live and the Windows installer carries it across an update; `x86/linux-9892` names the parameter on a DisplayPort connector (`HADES_BOOT_OPTION`) only while a DisplayPort to HDMI converter is behind it, so the setting shows only there. `x86/linux-9891` then declares, only after a converter found in the DPCD whose ELD declares stereo PCM and nothing else, PCM in 8 channels at the rates and sizes that the display data give, Dolby Digital, Dolby Digital Plus and Dolby TrueHD (CTA-861 codes 2, 10 and 12), the 7.1 speaker allocation, and an ELD length as the specification counts it. DTS is not declared.

**Streams, discs and files**

- `CHttpSourceMemory` remembers per server a redirect, one connection and the type of a file; `CCurlFile` closes a second connection that the server refuses (403, 429 or 503) and goes on with one, and asks the address of the add-on again where the one of the CDN runs out (401, 403, 404 or 410). An image is told by the extension of where an address leads, by its type (`application/x-iso9660-image` and the like) or by the name of `Content-Disposition`. The UDF volume of an image on the network is kept 60 s after its last reader, read in pieces of 128 KiB with the last 8 kept.
- `CSubtitleTrackMemory` keeps what was read of each subtitle track, 60 s back, 4 MiB a track and 24 MiB in all, for a change of track without a read. `locale.languagevariety` (`CLanguageVariety`).
- Measured in a virtual machine against a server made to behave as a CDN: a seek on a server of one connection, 61 s to 1.5 to 1.8 s; an expired token at a seek, an error to 1.8 s; an image without extension, or told by its type, nothing in 180 s to 2.3 to 2.9 s; eight changes of subtitle, eight reads to none.
- The Blu-ray page: on a change of the range or matrix of the disc's graphics, what is already shown is drawn again as a copy (BD-J: its range; HDMV: its palette, from the original palette that `CDVDOverlayImage::paletteSource` now keeps), once, with a line in the log.
- `libbluray-9100` to `9102`: the HDMV button command after a link, the JVM started with `-Xrs`, and the `java.util.Timer` threads that a disc leaves cancelled when the service stops.
- FFmpeg: `ffmpeg-9951-33` is `release/9.0` up to `8c7ef49002ff` (`ff_mov_read_chan()` and `ff_mov_read_chnl()` grow the map of the channels as they read it; a CAF file of 116 bytes that declares 50,000,000 channels took 1.5 GB and a second before its error, and now 4 MB). `ffmpeg-9962` is the libswscale part of master's `72f8c3376be3` (Dom Cobley): the unscaled C converters from YUV420P, YUV422P and YUVA420P to RGB write the last pixel of a row of odd width, which kept what the buffer had; on x86 Kodi's own conversions take the SSSE3 ones, with room in their buffers, so what Kodi shows does not change. Tested on the host at seven odd widths into 22 formats, with and without the patch.
- FFmpeg `ffmpeg-9970-01` to `74`: 73 fixes of `master` after `release/9.0` split from it (`47e9d68e203f`), up to `01ca75d35067`, and the refactoring that one of them needs (`1586fd2dc206`), each with its author, its message and the commit it was picked from; 69 are the commits as they are and 5 are adapted to 9.0.2, with a note in their message. They were chosen among the commits of `master` that `release/9.0` and the tree do not have, that touch a file the image builds and that say they fix something; 115 were read whole, and each one taken fixes code that 9.0.2 and its patches have. FATE on what they touch (1759 tests) gives the same results with and without them but for `seek-hls`, which is what `9970-01` fixes, and `checkasm` of H.264 fails 20 of 238 functions without `9970-16`. The 13 videos with Dolby Vision or HDR10+ metadata among the inputs of `tools/regress` (Dolby Vision profiles 7 and 8, HDR10+ in HEVC and VP9), their main video track decoded by FFmpeg with and without them, give the same pictures, Dolby Vision metadata, RPUs, HDR10+ metadata and HDR10+ payloads, byte for byte. `9970-32` (`5986d3031439`) changes only the colour metadata that FFmpeg gives an RPU without display management: the two chroma coefficients of the green row become negative and the offsets 1/16 and 1/2, as BT.709's YCbCr to RGB has them and as every RPU codes them (16777216 and 134217728 in 28 fractional bits); native output refuses such an RPU as before (its `signal_eotf` is not that of the tunnel, and it has no display management blocks), and where the bridge rebuilds it through libplacebo (`dvbridge_map_color()`, `pl_map_avdovi_metadata()`) the colours are now right. `9970-12` (`2c2f6e96e317`): `av_dynamic_hdr_plus_to_t35()` writes `color_saturation_mapping_flag` for each window whatever its `tone_mapping_flag`, as ST 2094-40 and FFmpeg's own parser have it; Kodi uses it only for `Player.Process(video.sidedata)`, and the HDR10+ InfoFrame is written from the metadata themselves (`HDR10PLUS::MakeVsif()`).
- Subtitles by a step: `SubtitleShiftUp(step)` and `SubtitleShiftDown(step)` move 1/32 of the height (33 pixels at 1080 lines, 67 at 2160); `save` works as before, in any order.
- A seamless join of a Blu-ray that opens the streams again says in `kodi.log` which stream differs; the decision is as before (`KeepStreamsAtJoin()`, the same verdict in 400,000 cases).
- The Kodi commits of `Piers` up to `51b30cf19be9` are taken with their authors. Low latency for games keeps the fence of the graphics with the commit (`fenced` and `async` apart, with a line in the log at its start and end), stopping during a change of refresh rate also covers the second change of resolution that Hades makes from the player's thread for native Dolby Vision, and `m_bIsLoading` of the background loader is atomic (ThreadSanitizer found three races in each run of its test). Of the earlier ones, 22 are taken; the change of an embedded subtitle without a new search keeps Hades' search of the line on the screen after a change (`865a607`). Those for Android, Windows, macOS and WebAssembly, and the build of git dependencies, change nothing in the image. Garrett Brown's sampling of the GUI's lookup tables at the centres of their texels goes into the branch of `gles_gui_composite.frag` without highp, where the tables stay; with highp (every OpenGL ES 3 GPU) Hades makes the GUI PQ or HLG without tables, and that shader is the same after the preprocessor. Dom Cobley's fix of the FFmpeg of `tools/depends`, which the image does not build, goes into the image's FFmpeg as `ffmpeg-9962`.

**The start and the system**

- `grub-9001`: an entry started by the end of the wait clears the terminal as one started by Enter does, and prints no "Booting"; the theme of GRUB has a full-screen terminal over `boot.png`, the picture of the start as the init draws it on a screen of that size. The kernel takes the screen only when it has text to show (`FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE_DEFERRED_TAKEOVER`, `CONSOLE_LOGLEVEL_QUIET=3`), `plymouth-lite-9001` shows the frame buffer that it drew on, `show-version.service` writes the version line only with the diagnostics on or after an error, and `kodi-display-notice` clears its notice from the frame buffer too. `CONFIG_DRM_PANIC` draws a panic on the screen where the driver supports it (not NVIDIA's, nor the firmware's frame buffer). `kodi-failed-notice` shows its notice after three stops of Kodi within a minute. `update.sh` writes the new theme of the boot menu in an update in place, at once and with `theme.cfg` last; the theme travels in the system (`usr/share/grub/hades`, about 13 MB).
- `hades-picture` (new, GPL-2.0-or-later, libdrm and libpng only): each active CRTC gets a dumb buffer of its mode in an atomic commit without `ALLOW_MODESET`, held until Kodi replaces it (`hades-picture release` before Kodi); the init draws with it from the initramfs, and `hades-boot-picture.service` once the driver has its frame buffer; the notices are cards over the splash screens (`brand/notice`), and the version on the card of a start that fails is written with an atlas of the typeface. `show-version.service` runs only with `hades.showversion`. Kodi puts back the frame buffer of its output at its end only while it is the same.
- The clock: `clock-measure` and `hades-clock-measure.path` keep the verdict in `/storage/.cache/clock-measured`. A measurement counts only with the time from the internet, and only where the clock is within 15 minutes of one of the two and not of the other; a clock in local time is compared with every offset that the time zone had in the year before. `connman-9002` and `systemd-9000` never write a clock that is read as UTC without being written. `LibreELEC-settings-9042` makes Automatic the default; a choice of local time of an earlier version, which wrote nothing, is Automatic now.
- The wait for the network: `kodi-waitonnetwork.service` runs `network-wait` (in the package wait-time-sync). Where `/storage/.cache/clock-trusted` says that a start read the clock within 30 s of the time from the internet (`clock-measure` writes it, again only when it changes or once a week, and takes it away where a start read the clock further off), the wait ends at `connmand-wait-online` (a service ready or online) or at the time, whichever is first. It is not used where the clock is before that start, more than 31 days after it, read another way (`/etc/adjtime`), or, read as local time, in a time zone with another offset. The journal says which ended the wait (`hades-network-wait`), and `results.txt` of the diagnostics whether the clock was trusted.
- With the diagnostic switch on, `hades_diag_dhcp` in the init makes `/run/hades/connman-diag.conf` with `CONNMAN_DHCP_DEBUG=1`, which `connman.service` reads: connman then logs each step of its DHCP client at the level info. `results.txt` has the link, connman's states, those steps and the end of the wait for the network, in seconds since the kernel started.
- NVIDIA: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9072` (`hades_frl_stall_retrain`, on): a second after a mode set over HDMI that the display engine has not finished, the kernel log has the raster, the SCDC of the display and the VRR of each head, and the FRL link is trained again in flush mode instead of hanging. `9075` (`nvidia-modeset.hades_vrr_rm_by_timings`, on): NVKMS tells RM of a head that can extend its vertical blank only where its timings have VRR (or it is G-SYNC), the rule of 615; driver 620 told it by the capability of the display, also at 23.976 Hz. Kodi writes each mode set that nvidia-drm takes, how long its commit took and the link before and after it (`CModesetReport`).
- HDMI FRL kept: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9078` gives the connectors with a TMDS output `NV_HDMI_FRL_KEEP`; with it, NVKMS keeps a head that runs over FRL on FRL, at the rate of its link, for a mode that TMDS would carry, where that mode is not 3D or YCbCr 4:2:0 (`nvHadesFrlKeepDecide()`; `nvidia-modeset.hades_frl_keep`, on), and the kernel log says whether it kept it. Kodi asks for it while the resolution of the menus needs more than 600 MHz (`FrlKeep.h`), never during native Dolby Vision, and commits again without it if the driver refuses. A change from TMDS to FRL that is left turns the output off in a commit of its own first (`CDRMAtomic::CommitOutputOffFirst()`), and where the flips do not come back after it the menus go to the best TMDS mode (`FRL_MODE_CHANGE::Fallback()`), at most three times in a session. `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9077` writes the raster and the SCDC of every FRL mode set that finishes, with the display's error counters (CED and RS), and names the states of NVKMS where it does not make a mode set that it confirmed.
- NVIDIA with several displays: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9080` and `nvidia-legacy-open-9078`: the flips of a commit that waits share one wait of 3 s, where nvidia-drm waited 3 s for the flip event of each CRTC in turn. `9081` and `nvidia-legacy-open-9079`: a change of display that NVKMS reports no longer takes `mode_config.mutex` in the queue of its events; it is marked, and the work of hot-plug sends it, as NVIDIA's driver does for hot-plug. `9083`: a change of content protection, and of its topology, no longer takes `mode_config.mutex` in that queue either; it is marked and applied from the work of content protection (driver 580 has no such events). The kernel log names an event of NVKMS that took more than 100 ms.
- Intel: `x86/linux-9880` (`i915.hdmi_empty_vsif`, 1) and `x86/linux-9881` (`i915.scdc_settle_ms`, 500): the link of a display whose hot-plug signal came back is set up again once the signal has been back that long, where no mode change of Hades explains the drop. `x86/linux-9858`: `enable_dv_lab` is a plain parameter. `x86/linux-9861`: the kernel log says what goes through a passive DP++ adaptor of amdgpu.
- Hot-plug: `HotplugRelink.h` also judges by `Status_Flags_0` of the display's SCDC. On a link that the driver does not report as unscrambled, a display with the setup but without the clock, or with a channel not locked, is `NOT_LOCKED`, treated as a lost setup: the output off and on once, with 10 s for the display's answer, 30 s between two and nothing during native Dolby Vision. Where the flags cannot be read, and for FRL, it is as before. Test `relink_test` in `tools/regress/kodi-host` (29 checks).
- The clocksource watchdog: `x86/linux-9896`. `clocksource_delta()` gives 0 both for a counter that did not move and for one that went back, so a watchdog that stopped made 7.3.5 mark the clocksource that it watched unstable and choose the watchdog. Where the watchdog has not advanced at all while the clocksource advanced 250 ms or more (`WD_FREQ_WATCHDOG_STOPPED`), the watchdog is marked unstable with a rating of 0 and the next one watches; the clocksource keeps its rating. Seven KUnit tests (`CLOCKSOURCE_WATCHDOG_KUNIT_TEST`, not built in the image).
- Chromebooks: `CHROME_PLATFORMS`, and as modules `CROS_EC`, `CROS_EC_PROTO`, `CROS_EC_LPC`, `MFD_CROS_EC_DEV`, `CROS_USBPD_NOTIFY`, `CROS_EC_TYPEC` with its alternate modes, `CROS_TYPEC_SWITCH`, `USB_ROLE_SWITCH` and `TYPEC_DP_ALTMODE`; `CROS_EC_CHARDEV`, `CROS_EC_SYSFS`, `CROS_EC_DEBUGFS`, the light bar, the sensor hub, the chargers and the LEDs are left out. They load only with Google's ACPI or on Framework laptops; 322 KiB of modules, and the kernel itself the same. `newt` comes from GitLab, where it moved from pagure.io: the same file.
- The diagnostics: `initcall_debug=1 log_buf_len=1M` on every entry of the boot menus while the switch is on (`internal-disks option initcall_debug`, `hades-diag-kernel-line.service` for a file renamed by hand), and `RuntimeMaxUse=16M` for the journal from the start's init; `LibreELEC-settings-9041` says so in the help of the switch. The early copy of the kernel log: `hades_diag_early_log` in the init, from `mount_storage`, writes it to `/storage/.cache/hades-diag/early-log/<boot ID>.txt` once a second where it changed (written whole, synced and renamed), and `hades-diag-early.service`, which only the init has systemd start, goes on until `/run/systemd/journal/flushed` and then deletes it; 300 s at most in the init and 120 s in systemd.
- Hardening: `openssl` and `openssl3` with `PKG_BUILD_FLAGS="+hardening -bindnow"`, Python 3 with `_FORTIFY_SOURCE=2`, `-Werror=format-security` and the stack protector of functions with character buffers; their exported symbols and sonames are the same, and the 25 add-ons that ask for `libssl.so.3` load as before. Samba with `--with-pie`: its 11 programs are position-independent, and its 109 libraries have the same machine code.
- Linux 7.3.5: `linux-9823`, `9848` and `9849` are part of it and go. The patches of Hades for the kernel made after their numbers ran out go in `projects/Generic/patches/linux/x86/`, applied after the others, with the free numbers of `packages/linux/patches/default/`. The patches of Kodi of this version are in `projects/Generic/patches/kodi/x86-2/`, applied after `x86/`, from `kodi-9001`.
- BusyBox: `busybox-9903` and `9904` (`volume_id`: an HFS+ block size must be a power of two of at least 512, and a buffer whose offset and length wrap around is refused; a disk with such a partition made 1.0 wait at its start), and `busybox-9905` (`tar`: a hard link whose target passes through a symbolic link of the same archive is refused). glibc `9069` to `9071`; `openssl-9949` to `9964` and `openssl3-9940` to `9947`.
- The two restarts: `hades-bootnext` (`status`, `windows`, `again --keep-chosen`) writes only `BootNext`, on entries that the firmware already has, and nothing without `BootCurrent` or on a start by BIOS. Kodi's *Restart* asks it first to come back to this system, and leaves a choice of Windows made in this start as it is; `init`, `fs-resize`, the reset and the restore set `BootNext` to `BootCurrent` before their restart. Each step leaves a line in `kodi.log` or the journal. The installer's *Restart into LibreELEC Hades* (`FirmwareBoot.cs`) looks only at the drives that it would offer, never at the Windows disk or a protected one. Its button next to a chosen drive that has Hades and on the last screen asks the same question as the link; the box for the shortcut on the desktop is off until it is ticked, the question starts as the window's box is, and the shortcut is made only when the PC is restarted with it ticked. A USB drive that Hades is taken off is left in MBR with exFAT; one of more than 2 TiB and an internal disk, in GPT.
- Firmware: 54 files of `kernel-firmware` 20261019 (the same version and hash) for drivers that were already built: MT7902, MT7915, MT7916, MT7615, MT7663, MT7668 and MT7610E, the NVM files of each board for QCA WCN785x and WCN6855, and Rome; 8.0 MB, about 4.4 MB in the system image. `linux-9862`: btmtk does not reset the controller for a firmware that does not exist (in 7.3.5 the reset probed it again with a new `hci_dev`, the mark of the retry went back to zero, and it went on without end). The *Installer* entry blacklists the 10 Bluetooth drivers that load by alias, as it did with NVIDIA's.
- Secure Boot: `shim-signed` and `shim-helpers-signed` are the `.deb` of Debian 13 (`shim-signed 1.51~1+deb13u1+16.1-2~deb13u1`, `shim-helpers-amd64-signed 1+16.1+2~deb13u1`), each with its `PKG_SHA256` and the SHA-256 of `shimx64.efi.signed` and `mmx64.efi.signed` checked at install. The two Microsoft signatures were verified against the 2011 and 2023 CAs, and shim was rebuilt in a chroot with the 177 packages of its `.buildinfo`, without network, to the same bytes (the signed files are the build plus their signature table). GRUB has an `.sbat` section (`grub,6`, `grub.hades,1`), checked at build. `hades-sb-sign` (Python with `openssl`) and the installer's own Authenticode code give the same bytes as `sbsign` 0.9.4. The installer writes only `MokNew`, `MokAuth` and `MokTimeout` under shim's GUID (an allow list in the code, with a test); "Remove LibreELEC Hades" offers to delete a pending request. `linux-9894` fixes the order of the lockdown check for ACPI tables, and `linux-9895` sets `CONFIG_LOCK_DOWN_IN_EFI_SECURE_BOOT`. The image keeps a key slot in the storage, mode 0600, and a `/secureboot` folder in the boot partition; `update.sh` signs before it touches anything, and refuses an update that cannot start with Secure Boot. Under Secure Boot GRUB refuses a font from the disk (since the fixes for CVE-2022-2601 and CVE-2022-3775), so the fonts of the boot menu are also in a memdisk inside GRUB, which is part of the signed program and which GRUB reads without its verifiers; `theme.cfg` takes them from there only when GRUB is locked down, and the pictures of the theme, which the verifier lets through, from the disk. Without Secure Boot the menu is drawn from the same files as before. No check of GRUB is changed. The licence of shim is in `licenses/` (BSD-2-Clause-Patent), and Debian's copyright files and shim's own notice in `/usr/share/licenses/shim-signed` and `shim-helpers-signed`.
- The installer: `build.ps1` builds `LibreELEC-Hades-Installer.exe` without the files of its self-tests and `LibreELEC-Hades-Installer-tests.exe` with them (`/define:HADES_TESTS`); in the first one, the words of the self-tests (`--selftest`, `--capture`, `--vhd-test`, the `--simulate-*` commands, `--make-test-image`) say where the self-tests are and do nothing, and the build refuses it if it holds a piece of a value of `TestVectors.cs`, a PEM key or a class of the self-tests. `--list` and `--boot-entries` stay in both.

### Credits

- Taken with their authors: the Kodi commits of the `Piers` branch named above, among them Alexander Golovanov's change of an embedded subtitle without interrupting playback, vincent71711's ASS subtitles, Garrett Brown's, Marcin's, ksooo's, Kel Modderman's, olympia's, Chris's, jjd-uk's and Dom Cobley's; and the fixes of libbluray, glibc, OpenSSL, FFmpeg and the Linux kernel.
- The upscalers are mpv user shaders by Bin Jin (RAVU) and igv (FSRCNNX), under LGPL-3.0-or-later, shipped unchanged.
- The work for Chromebooks and USB-C hubs follows pull request #29 by Felix Oghină, written again here.
- The fixes that the items above say CroqueMr, fandangos and YBLOD found, and the ideas that they credit to CroqueMr's build (among them the upscalers and the switch of a video's output in the player information), ashlar (neural upscalers in Kodi), YBLOD, CorentinB and mephis, are written again here. corgeone measured what an AV receiver shows with the empty HDMI vendor InfoFrame.
- Thank you to everyone whose reports and tests shaped this version, in the issues of this repository and on the LibreELEC and Kodi forums, among them wright96d, harment, NTM-3D, SeaMonkey82, SunnySnacks, Khazar321, corgeone, sat4all1-dev, ht2tweak, astrotrain, louyeh, MrMagic, testmest and Felix Oghină.

## 1.0

The first version that is not a release candidate. It makes Hades easier to set up and to understand: a check of your setup that plays test pictures and sounds and says in plain words what reached the TV, a diagnostic report made with one button, and a player information page that says in one sentence whether a video plays as intended. It fixes what was reported after 1.0 RC8, keeps picture and sound in step, sends Dolby Vision as the composer makes it by default, maps HDR to displays more faithfully, plays DVD films as films, keeps a stream going through a network drop, and brings every part up to date. Every change was tested on the host, with the code that the image runs, or in a virtual machine, and each item says how. The final build was tried on the author's PC, a GeForce RTX 4080 SUPER with an LG C5 TV, a TCL Q85H Pro sound bar and a Samsung Odyssey OLED G6 monitor: native Dolby Vision of profiles 5, 7 with its full enhancement layer and 8.1, with Dolby TrueHD and Atmos passed through; HDR10 sent as Dolby Vision; three films that had dropped out of Dolby Vision before; HDR10 with DTS-HD Master Audio passed through; the new player information page; moving between the TV and the monitor from the settings, with the display not in use turned off; and, on the monitor, which takes HDR10+ and not Dolby Vision, Dolby Vision videos with their own HDR10+ and HDR10+ videos sent as HDR10+ ([What has been tested](docs/tested.md)). The new things that could behave differently on your hardware, and that nobody has seen yet, are listed under [Not tried on hardware yet](docs/tested.md#not-tried-on-hardware-yet).

The documentation is rewritten for readers: the README, the [extras](docs/features.md), the [settings guide](docs/settings.md) and [how it works](docs/how-it-works.md). It is in English only. The Spanish README and NOTICE are retired; the Windows installer and the menus of Hades keep their Spanish.

### What's new for you

**Easier to use**

- **Check this setup** (*Settings > LibreELEC > System > Check this setup*). It plays short test clips that the build of Hades makes itself: a test picture in SDR, HDR10, HLG and Dolby Vision (profiles 8.1 and 5), a soft noise from each speaker in turn, and a chime in each sound format that your receiver or TV may take as it is (Dolby Digital, Dolby Digital Plus and Dolby TrueHD). After each one it says what went out and how (the display mode, the HDR signal, whether Dolby Vision went out natively and why not, the sound format), asks what you saw or heard, and says what to do if something was wrong. It switches on only the sound formats that you heard; nothing else is changed. DTS cannot be checked this way and is left as it is. No material of anyone else is in the clips. Tried in a virtual machine.
- **A diagnostic report in one file** (*Settings > LibreELEC > System > Submit Log > Create a diagnostic report*).
