- VC-1: `BlurayVc1Rate.h` takes the rate that a playlist declares, and for a `.m2ts` or `.ts` file the rate of its sequence header after the second opening of the demuxer. A seek step left out while the video of the step before it gets in sync is kept (`SeekLeftOut.h`) and made once it is.
- Subtitles: `OverlayFrameMatch.h` gives a frame the picture subtitles that start nearer its time than the next frame's; `ffmpeg-9960` makes `pgssubdec` ignore an end segment that follows no presentation segment (after matthane's patch for CoreELEC); `subtitles.hadesbitmapsaturation` scales the chroma around the luma in linear light (100 % computes nothing); `subtitles.hadesbitmaprange` (`PgsRange.h`) takes a PGS stream as SDR or HDR, and Automatic draws as SDR, in a PQ video only, a mostly opaque palette entry with Y' of 202 or more. The PGS of profile 5 (and of profile 10 with no other base layer) are taken as PQ in BT.2020 (`IptPQVideo()`).
- `ffmpeg-9950`: a Matroska seek starts at the earliest key frame of the video tracks. h264_mvc fix `9009`: the dependent view is finished before an IDR empties the views.

**Boot and system**

- The boot menus: `scripts/mkimage` writes a wait of 5 s in a form whose length does not depend on its value (`TIMEOUT 050`, `set timeout="05"`) and says `boot-choices` in their first line; `update.sh` (`wait_update`) and the Windows installer bring a menu without that word that waits 10 s to 5 s, once, and keep every other wait. `LibreELEC-settings-9060` offers 10, 5, 3 and 1 s. The installer carries the wait and the two kernel options of the display across an update.
- `update.sh` of the new system gives each boot menu, for that one start, a block between `# hades-once-begin` and `# hades-once-end` that starts the system at once (GRUB: a hidden menu with a timeout of one second, Escape shows it; syslinux: `MENU SHIFTKEY` alone, which starts at once unless Shift is held, and then the menu waits as usual). `hades-menu-once.service` takes exactly those lines out at the next start, so the menu is, byte for byte, the one that was there. A menu that is not one of Hades, or has more or fewer than one line that sets its wait, is left as it is, and that is said.
- DMA translation: `scripts/mkimage` writes `iommu.passthrough=0 intel_iommu=on,igfx_off` in Run and Live; the installer keeps the words of the medium, and adds them to older menus as it adds `lockdown=integrity`; `update.sh` (`dma_update`) gives them once to the menus of a system updated in place. The init writes one line, `hades-iommu:`, read from sysfs, and each snapshot of the diagnostics has a section "DMA translation (IOMMU)". `LibreELEC-settings-9036` says in the help of the internal disks that the entry that shows them starts without it.
- `LibreELEC-settings-9040` and `hades-diag` (`report.sh`, `switch.sh`): the report collects at once what a snapshot of a start has (`now/`, with `boot-time.txt` and `results.txt`), the journal of this start and of the one before, the newest crash log of Kodi, the folders that the diagnostics saved (16 MB at most, the newest first), and `report.txt`, which says what is in it and what was left out and why; a file that the filter fails on is left out, and without the filter nothing is collected. The switch renames `DIAG-OFF.TXT` and `DIAG-ON.TXT` on the boot partition, which is writable only for that moment. The share Logfiles runs the filtered `createlog` as its `root preexec` (`filtered-log-<date>-<time>.zip`; a note while one takes more than 10 s to make).
- `LibreELEC-settings-9050` and the package `hades-check`: the clips are made at build time from patterns of their own, coded with `x265:host` and checked with `ffmpeg:host` (only the build host runs them; no encoder goes into the image), and installed in `/usr/share/hades/check` with a `SHA256SUMS` that the install step checks. While a clip plays, Kodi publishes what its player information would show (`hades.check.running`), and the check uses Estuary's own words of that page.
- `LibreELEC-settings-9031`: the window that shows the address, the user and the generated password of Samba. `samba-config` writes a share with `printf`, keeps a name free of `%`, backslashes and brackets, and links a path that holds them under `/run/samba/media`.
- `LibreELEC-settings-9035`: the nameservers of a connection with any IPv4 method, IPv4 or IPv6, a row "In use now", and the lists cleared when a connection is opened. `LibreELEC-settings-9033`, `9037` to `9039`: the entries of Updates without an update server.
- `50-hades-usb3-hub-autosuspend.rules`: SuperSpeed hubs with a delay of 2 s. A udev rule sets `manage_shutdown` on a disk behind a USB bridge, not of removable media.
- `hades-diag-installer.service` (`installer.sh` of hades-diag): in a start of the installer with the switch on, `hades-diag/start-N/installer/journal.txt` and `install.txt`, appended every 2 s from `journalctl --after-cursor`, under the lock of the writers of the boot partition, which the installer's "Save log" takes too.
- The IR decoders: the host LLVM gets the BPF target, `v4l-utils` builds its nine `bpf_protocols` from source with that clang (byte-identical sources since 1.22.0), and `v4l-utils-9000` inlines `emitBit()` of `manchester`, which clang 23 made a subprogram that `ir-keytable` does not load. The package `ir-bpf-decoders` goes. systemd keeps `bpf-framework` and `libarchive` off, as the builds have had them. Each decoder was checked with `rc-loopback` under `lockdown=integrity`: 33 of 33 frames, as with the objects of 2021.
- `libcec-9006` to `9011`: six faults of libCEC 8.1.7 that freed memory while its threads used it, found with AddressSanitizer and ThreadSanitizer on the host with an emulated Pulse-Eight adapter. Kodi's CEC adapter asks an amplifier only what volume control needs, and its flag of readiness is an atomic. `libcec-9013`: `CCECClient::CheckKeypressTimeout()`, which the processor thread calls on every pass of its loop, read the held key before it took the client's lock, while `ResetKeypressState()` wrote it under that lock when `CLibCEC::Close()` unregistered the client (a data race; the same lines are in libCEC's `master`); it is read under the lock. A test that polls for key timeouts as the processor thread does while another thread unregisters the client saw the race under ThreadSanitizer in 11 runs of 11 before and in none of 11 after.
- `CONFIG_AMD_PMC=m`, `CONFIG_SND_ALOOP=m`, `btproxy` installed, and `linux-9857` (mainline `ac22c8c723c6`, by Mark Anthony Agarro with Devin Wittmayer). The USB/IP client is not built (it was in no published version).
- Reproducible builds: `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is the time of the commit; the kernel takes it with `KBUILD_BUILD_TIMESTAMP` and the user and host `builder@hades`, the release tar is sorted and owned by root, `populatefs-9001` clamps times as `mke2fs -d` does, the salt of the image's password follows from the commit, NSPR, keyutils (`keyutils-9001`), libCEC (`libcec-9012`) and the nvidia id of the modules take no time or host of the build machine, and git looks for no repository above the build folder. Two assemblies of the image gave the same system, kernel and release tar byte for byte.
- From LibreELEC `master` up to 23b6180439a3: Intel's processor microcode 20261110 (fixes of functional issues for 13th generation Core mobile processors, Core and Core Ultra Series 3 and Xeon, and a first microcode for the Xeon 6900E+; its release notes name no security advisory; the archive holds the files of its tag, each the same), and exfatprogs 1.4.4 (`fsck.exfat`, `exfat2img` and `defrag.exfat` reject a boot sector with a malformed geometry instead of overflowing or reading out of bounds, `fsck.exfat` stops at an I/O error instead of taking it for an inconsistency, and a full format of `mkfs.exfat` is faster), with a valid GnuPG signature of its maintainer.
- The `Piers` branch of Kodi up to `169296d6bd84`: the eleven commits of the information dialog and the Manage menu (olympia's pull request 30308) were in the series already, from the pull request, with the same changes; the four others are `kodi-9954` to `kodi-9957`, with their authors. A floating-point framebuffer is read as floats for screenshots and captures (Garrett Brown), in place of the read that Hades had for the half-float output of nvidia-drm and the picture of native Dolby Vision; Hades keeps from its own the read in bands of 64 rows, so that a 3840x2160 picture does not take 133 MB at once. A game port whose saved controller it no longer accepts keeps its default one (Kel Modderman). The depth and colour of the output reported for captures on Android, and the processor passed to the dependencies of Windows Store builds (Garrett Brown), change nothing in the image.
- `lz4` 1.11.1 (1.11.0 can write out of bounds in `LZ4_resetStreamHC()` at levels 10 and up; the library is used by libarchive and OpenVPN) and `libfmt` 12.2.1, with `automake` 1.20 and `ccache` 4.14.3 from LibreELEC `master`.
- OpenSSL 4.1 and OpenSSL 3.5 (`openssl3`, kept beside it for the add-ons that ask for `libssl.so.3`) carry the fixes of the five advisories that OpenSSL published on 2026-11-10: CVE-2026-88823, CVE-2026-107496, CVE-2026-88822, CVE-2026-95691 and CVE-2026-95692, all of low severity. They are taken from the `openssl-4.1` and `openssl-3.5` branches, with their authors (`openssl-9921` to `9948`, `openssl3-9921` to `9939`).
- glibc: the 38 commits of its stable branch `release/2.44/master` up to `47e2b570410f` (`glibc-9030` to `9067`) and the fix of CVE-2026-84243 (`glibc-9068`, from `master`): `gettext` skipped the entries of `LANGUAGE` that contain a `/` only in setuid and setgid programs, so a value that crossed a trust boundary could make any other program load a message catalog from any path; now they are skipped in every program.
- FFmpeg 9.0.2 with the stable branch `release/9.0` up to `d838f9f9aeb0` (`ffmpeg-9951`; the LCEVC code of the last eight commits is not compiled in the image) and `ffmpeg-9952`, the fix of `master` that makes the `hqdn3d` filter forget its configured width when its buffers are freed.
- The patches of Hades are numbered again in the order in which they apply: those of Kodi from `kodi-9001`, and those of the kernel from `linux-9800` in `packages/linux/patches/default/` and from `linux-9900` in `projects/Generic/patches/linux/`. Numbers in this section are those of the tree at the commit of this version.

### Credits

- The format and the name of `Player.Process(video.sidedata)` are Team CoreELEC's, read from its Kodi branch `aml-5.15.196-22.0` as documentation of the format; the code is written here.
- The ideas that the items above credit to YBLOD, CroqueMr's build, CorentinB, cinemaONE, SamuriHL, pannal, avdvplus, matthane, malard, damnms and Pro-me3us are written again here. The settings for the colour format and the depth of the link follow Kodi pull requests 30099 and 30192, by smp79, as CroqueMr's fork carries them.
- Taken with their authors: tanio99's fixes of DTS-HD passthrough (Kodi pull request 30440, sent by samnazarko for OSMC) and heit88's of TrueHD (pull request 30326); Kodi's JSON-RPC server change by Martin Ellis, with Garrett Brown's test change; matthane's two Blu-ray fixes of Kodi's `master`; the fixes to `fs/ntfs`, `mt7921`, `mt7925` (Mark Anthony Agarro and Devin Wittmayer), Wildcat Lake and DCN 4.2B (Gabe Teeger, AMD) from Linux; and Ville Syrjälä's GMBUS fixes for Panther Lake and Wildcat Lake, from drm-intel-next.
- The trims are applied as SMPTE ST 2094-10 and ETSI TS 103 572 publish them. The tone mapping was measured against an open test film under CC BY 4.0; nothing of it is in the image or in this repository.
- Thank you to everyone whose reports, requests and tests shaped this version, in the issues of this repository and on the LibreELEC and Kodi forums, among them roronoazoro-blip, RaphS8, SunnySnacks, Levi741i, wright96d, sat4all1-dev, Khazar321, testmest, ht2tweak, lfemperor, nissel, doppingkoala and vertox.

## 1.0 RC8

An update that fixes faults found in 1.0 RC7 and adds a few smaller things. Each change was tested on the host, with the code that the image runs; each item says how.

### What's new for you

- **HDR10 upright with NVIDIA's driver 580** (GeForce GTX 900 and GTX 10 cards, TITAN V; issue #17). In 1.0 RC7, HDR video drawn into the 10-bit buffers that Kodi allocates itself on these cards went out upside down, menus and subtitles too. The plane that shows them now flips them back, in the same commit as their first frame. A plane that cannot flip them never gets them: the video then goes out upright at 8 bits.
- **No GRUB error after an update** (issue #17). An update with the `.tar` now also replaces the two GRUB programs of the boot partition with those of the new system. A drive installed before 1.0 RC6 kept its older GRUB, which lacks the command `[` that the boot menu of 1.0 RC7 uses: each start said "can't find command `['" and waited for a key.
- **Seeking and the arrows during a Blu-ray played with its menus** (issue #1). A Blu-ray with Java menus keeps its menu graphics open, and fully transparent, through the whole film, and Kodi took every key for the disc: nothing could seek, the arrows did not move through the film and the player information did not open. Kodi now takes the disc to be in its menu only while its menu plane shows something; subtitles alone do not count.
- **The thin text of Blu-ray menus is no longer faint** (issue #1). The graphics of a Blu-ray are drawn at 1920x1080, and at 3840x2160 each of their pixels was mixed with its neighbours, which takes a quarter of the contrast off a line one pixel wide and about half off a dot. At a whole scale they now keep their pixels, as the disc drew them.
- **How long the boot menu waits** (issue #17). *LibreELEC Settings > System > Boot menu* sets how long the menu shown at start waits before it starts the system by itself: 10 seconds (the default), 3 or 1, on UEFI and BIOS computers alike.
- **HDR at 10 bits over HDMI on GeForce GTX 10 cards and the TITAN V, as an option** (issue #17). Over HDMI, the display code of driver 580 sends RGB at 8 bits at most on these cards, whatever Kodi asks and the display takes, so 1.0 RC7 was wrong to say that HDR10 goes out at 10 bits there. They do drive YCbCr 4:2:2 at 10 bits, at the speed of RGB at 8 bits: with *System > Display > HDR at more than 8 bits where the card sends RGB at 8 (NVIDIA)* on, HDR goes out that way where the display takes it, from the next video. The same setting turns on the 12 bits of the GeForce GTX 16 and RTX 20 graphics, which needed a kernel parameter until now. It is off until it is confirmed on those cards. The GeForce GTX 900 cards stay at 8 bits over HDMI, and DisplayPort is not under this limit.
- **Up to date:** LibreELEC (NSS 3.133, LLVM 23.1.5, HarfBuzz 15.1.0, lz4 1.11.0, rsync 3.5.2) and the `Piers` branch of Kodi, whose one new commit keeps the clock of the display running through a change of mode: a video that switches the output to HDR as it starts no longer falls back to the system clock until it ends.

### Details

**Display**

- `kodi-9963`: the plane that scans out Kodi's own buffers of more than 8 bits reflects them from top to bottom (DRM `rotation`, `reflect-y`). OpenGL draws into a framebuffer object with the bottom row of the picture first, and a plane scans a buffer out from its first row. The reflection is asked in the test commit that checks the buffers before use and in the commit of their first frame. Once asked, every frame states the rotation of its plane, so the window of EGL goes out upright after them. The `nvidia-drm` of driver 580 offers `reflect-y` and turns it into a vertical flip of the layer (read in its source). Checked: every source file that the commit changes, and the files that include a header it changes, compiled with the commands of the build (31 of 31). Not yet seen on a card with driver 580.
- The limit of 8 bits over HDMI is in `nvkms` of driver 580: RGB deeper than 8 bits over HDMI needs `supportsHDMI10BPC`, which is false for the display engines of these cards (`nvEvo97`, `nvEvoC3`), and that driver has no 12-bit output at all. Read in its source.
- `nvidia-legacy-open-9071`: in a mode set that signals HDR (BT.2100), where the client fixed neither the depth nor the colour format and NVKMS chose RGB at 8 bpc, on an HDMI display, a GPU without 10 bpc in RGB over HDMI and with the BT.2020 matrix, and a display for which NVKMS offers 4:2:2 at 10 bpc, the mode is validated in YCbCr 4:2:2 at 10 bpc, limited range, first, and as before if it is refused. The parameter is in the kernel tree of driver 580 too (`nvidia-legacy-9000`). The colour conversion, the range and the AVI InfoFrame are those of NVKMS for that format. Dolby Vision (8 bpc through "max bpc"), SDR and DisplayPort are not touched. The kernel log says it at each mode set. Checked: the file compiled with the command and the compiler of the build, and the decision in 15 cases on the host, each condition that must keep it off among them. Not yet seen on one of these cards.

**Blu-ray**

- `kodi-9964`: `IsInMenu()` goes by whether the IG plane (the menus of HDMV, the graphics of BD-J) has an image with a palette or an ARGB pixel that is not fully transparent, or by the disc itself (`BD_EVENT_MENU`); PG alone is subtitles. What the start of a disc with no playlist goes by is as it was. `kodi.log` says when the menu plane starts or stops showing something. Checked: compiled with the commands of the build (31 of 31). Not yet seen with a disc.
- `kodi-9965`: the setting of HDR at more than 8 bits where the graphics send RGB at 8 bits at most writes the parameter of nvidia-modeset that the driver in use has (`hades_hdr_ycbcr422` of driver 580, `hades_turing_12bpc` of driver 615) before the mode set of each HDR video; it is shown where one of them can be written (`nvidia-legacy-open-9071` and `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9060` make them writable by root). `kodi.log` says each change. Checked: compiled with the commands of the build.
- `kodi-9966`: where what a Blu-ray draws is scaled by a whole number in both directions, it is drawn without mixing its pixels; any other scale, and every other overlay, is filtered as before. Checked: the rule in 12 cases on the host, and compiled with the commands of the build. Not yet seen with a disc.
- `kodi-9967`: the one new commit of the `Piers` branch (Chris): the thread of the vblank clock waits up to two seconds for vblank to come back after a mode set, in place of stopping. Compiled with the commands of the build.

**Boot and system**

- `update.sh` replaces `EFI/BOOT/bootx64.efi` and `bootia32.efi` with those of the new system where they differ, before it looks at the menu. Each is written as `<name>.new`, written through to the device and read back, and then renamed over the old one, so a power cut leaves the old one or the new one. A program that is not on the partition stays away. LibreELEC does not update them in place. Checked with the `busybox` of the image on a made-up boot partition: replaced, a second run changes nothing, and a failed copy leaves the old program and nothing else.
- The boot menu setting (`LibreELEC-settings-9030`) is kept in the menus and nowhere else, as the setting of the internal disks is. Its script reads it (`internal-disks wait`) and changes it (`internal-disks wait N`) only in the menus that this system writes. It uses the same checks, lock and written-through copy, and changes both menus or neither. GRUB says it in seconds and syslinux in tenths of a second; 0 is never written, since in syslinux it means "wait for ever". Where the two menus wait differently, or are not ones that this system writes, the setting is hidden. An update made with the installer for Windows writes the menus anew, at 10 seconds. Checked with the `busybox` of the image on made-up boot partitions, in 102 checks:
  - the menus for a USB drive, an internal disk, BIOS only and UEFI only;
  - both menus or neither;
  - a menu changed by hand;
  - a partition that cannot be made writable, or read-only again.

  The test of the setting of the internal disks gives the same result with the script of 1.0 RC7 and with this one. The settings add-on applies its 31 patches with no fuzz. The window of the setting in Kodi has not been seen yet.

## 1.0 RC7

An update that fixes faults found since 1.0 RC6 and adds a few smaller things. Each change was tested on the host, with the code that the image runs; each item says how.

### What's new for you

- **A film no longer ends when its drive stops answering for a while** (issue #16). When a USB drive stopped answering and the kernel reset it after 30 s, the wait used up the time that the player gives each packet, and the film ended just as the drive came back. The time that a read of a local file waits for its drive no longer counts against that time, and a read that fails with an I/O error after the reset is tried again at the same byte for up to 15 s: the picture stands still while the drive is away, and the film goes on when it answers. This covers Blu-ray and DVD images and folders on a local drive too, and Stop during such a wait ends it within a fraction of a second. A drive that is unplugged ends the film at once, as before, and files over the network are not changed. Tested on the host.
- **Limited colour range on NVIDIA is right from the start** (issue #17). With *Use limited colour range (16-235)* on, a GTX 1060 with driver 580 sent full range after each start of Kodi while the AVI InfoFrame said limited, which crushed the blacks until a video changed the mode. Where the range changes at the same mode, NVIDIA's driver now turns the output off and on, so that the new range reaches the cable: the TV loses its signal for a moment at Kodi's start with the setting on, when the setting changes, and when Dolby Vision starts or ends at the same refresh rate. A flip that changes the HDR state of the output no longer puts the range back to full. With the setting off nothing changes. Both driver series, 615 and 580.
- **Outputs that carry the sound stay on.** Ten seconds after it starts, Hades turns off the outputs that Kodi does not use, and a receiver or sound bar on another output lost its sound. Hades now never turns off the output whose sound Kodi uses (the audio and passthrough outputs of *System > Audio*, or the ones used in their place): it finds it by the graphics that its sound card belongs to and by the display that its audio data (ELD) name, and where that is not certain, every output that can carry sound and has a display stays on. When Kodi moves its picture to another output, the one it leaves stays on, black, if its sound is in use. An output kept on for its sound is turned off again once that sound is no longer in use, as in 1.0 RC6. An output turned off before its sound came into use comes back by itself on other graphics, and on Kodi's own graphics with the next video that changes the display mode, which Kodi says on screen, or when Kodi starts again. The file `/storage/.kodi/userdata/hades-keep-outputs` names more outputs to keep on, one per line (`DP-1`, `card0-HDMI-A-2` or `*`): it is harment's patch, with his authorship (issue #11). `kodi.log` says which output is kept for which sound, and why.
- **"Dolby Vision as HDR10+" for one video** (issue #16). *Dolby Vision output (this video)* now offers "Dolby Vision as HDR10+" where the display takes HDR10+, and "Dolby Vision as HDR10" sends plain HDR10, whatever *Send it as HDR10+ where the display takes it* says, so that the two can be compared from the player. "Dolby Vision as HDR10" kept for a file by 1.0 RC6 still does what it did, and the list shows it under the name of what it gives. On Intel at 3840x2160 up to 30 Hz and 4096x2160 at 24 Hz, HDR10+ chosen while a video plays now goes out, with one change of mode. The help line of the button in the video OSD no longer says "RadioText Plus info". Tested on the host.
- **The output depth during native Dolby Vision** (issue #16). The overview of the player information said "8 bits (fixed)" on NVIDIA and "up to N bits" elsewhere; on every driver it now says "8 bits: Dolby Vision carries its 12 bits in them", which is how Dolby Vision goes over HDMI.
- **HDR10 at 10 bits with NVIDIA's driver 580** (GeForce GTX 900 and GTX 10 cards, TITAN V). That driver offers Kodi no window of more than 8 bits, so HDR10, HLG and Dolby Vision shown as HDR10 went out at 8 bits. Kodi now draws such video into 10-bit buffers that it allocates itself, after checking that the driver takes them, also after a change of resolution during the video; where a step fails, the video goes out at 8 bits as before, and `kodi.log` says which step. `<video><hdrownbuffers>false</hdrownbuffers></video>` turns it off. Driver 615 is not changed. Tested on the host.
- **A Blu-ray played with its menus keeps its seamless joins.** From the disc's menus, the player closed and opened its streams at every new play item, so a film made of shared and specific clips (several cuts on one disc) paused at each join. Where the playlist joins two play items seamlessly and their streams are the same, playback now goes on, on one timeline, as it has without menus since 1.0 RC6; anything else is as before, and `kodi.log` says which, and why, for each join. A key pressed while the disc changes its title, playlist or play item no longer makes that change get lost. Tested on the host with discs made for the test.
- **Dolby Vision decoded by the processor goes out natively.** Where the graphics cannot decode a stream (AV1 on GeForce RTX 20 and GTX 16, AMD Renoir and Cezanne, Intel before Tiger Lake) or VAAPI is off, single-layer Dolby Vision (profiles 5, 8 and 10) played as its base layer, and profile 10.0 with wrong colours. Its pictures now go to native Dolby Vision output, to Dolby Vision shown as HDR10 and to the mapping to SDR, from the planes that Kodi already uploads, with the same bytes as those of a hardware decoder (measured with the software bridge). Profile 7 is as before. Idea from CroqueMr's build. No AV1 file with Dolby Vision has been tried.
- **Level 5 offsets that do not fit the picture are left aside.** The level 5 metadata of Dolby Vision say where the bars of a film are. Offsets that fit no reading of the picture made every picture be refused, and the video left native output; offsets that say bars where the picture has content (an open-matte film with the metadata of its letterboxed master) turned those rows black. The first are now left aside for the rest of the video. For the second, Hades measures a picture now and then and leaves the offsets aside too once at least ten pictures have been measured under that level 5 value, more than half of them with picture across the width of the bars, three of them 2 s or more apart; the whole picture shows after about ten seconds. The decision lasts until the level 5 offsets change, and is undone if the measured pictures stop contradicting them. A letterboxed film is never changed. Idea from CroqueMr's build. Measured with the software bridge.
- **Each UEFI boot entry starts the kernel of its own drive** (issue #14). The boot menu started the first file named KERNEL on any drive, so a second drive with another build could start its kernel with this system: a black screen. Each entry now finds its system partition by its identifier, first on the drive that the menu was read from, so that two drives written from the same image each start their own; where no partition has it, the kernel of the menu's own partition, and only then the first on any drive, as before. An update with the `.tar` in the `Update` folder changes the menu the same way and keeps the one of before as `EFI/BOOT/grub.cfg.old`; a menu that is not one of Hades as it was written is left as it is. Tested in a virtual machine, and the update on the host.
- **An empty `/opt` in the system,** where Entware can be mounted (issue #11).
- **The diagnostics name the USB controllers and every disk** (issue #16). Each snapshot lists the USB controllers, with USB4 and Thunderbolt (who sets up their connections, and at what security level), and the power state of every USB device; the timeline saved at shutdown has every other disk, in the seconds when it read, wrote or had a request waiting ([diagnostics](docs/diagnostics.md)).
- **Up to date:** the `Piers` branch of Kodi up to 42848fff43c5 (among its fixes, HEIF and AVIF photos stored as a grid of tiles, as most iPhone photos are, shown whole and turned as the file says), and LibreELEC at its latest commit, which does not change the image.

### Details

**Dolby Vision**

- "Dolby Vision as HDR10+" is offered where the display declares HDR10+ and its connector can send it, the picture can be rebuilt as HDR10, and no setting maps it to the display; where it is not offered, no choice sends HDR10+. What a rebuilt picture goes out as is decided once a video, and again when the choice changes during the video (it stayed the decision of the start). A file kept with "Dolby Vision as HDR10" by 1.0 RC6 follows *Send it as HDR10+ where the display takes it*, as it did, and is listed as "Dolby Vision as HDR10+" where that gives HDR10+, otherwise as "Dolby Vision as HDR10"; back on 1.0 RC6, the new choices kept for files are dropped and the others stay. `kodi.log` says what a rebuilt picture goes out as, and why, also with "As the settings say".
- HDR10+ started while a video plays: amdgpu starts and ends it with a flip; on i915 and xe, at the modes that DRM names by their HDMI VIC, the next commit sets the mode again, as at the start of a video, and the first picture waits for its own measurement in it; nvidia-drm puts the CTA VIC in the AVI InfoFrame with the flip that starts it. Once HDR10+ has gone out at a mode, the AVI InfoFrame keeps the CTA VIC until the next change of mode, so HDR10+ chosen again goes out with a flip. A refused commit is tried again without HDR10+, and the video goes on as HDR10. Tested on the host.
- Dolby Vision decoded by the processor: the bridge takes the three planes of 10-bit 4:2:0 that Kodi's renderer uploads, without a copy, and the composer of ETSI GS CCM 001 reads each sample as its code, so that they give the codes of the P010 surface of a hardware decoder. Not for profile 7, whose enhancement layer needs VAAPI, nor for a picture made by a filter or of another format, of an odd size, larger than 3840x2160 or rotated. Single-layer HEVC decoded by the processor (VAAPI off, or graphics without HEVC Main10) goes the same way. When the processor cannot keep up, the AV1 decoder drops pictures that are not references. The player information no longer gives "software" as the reason for such a stream.
- Level 5 offsets fit when they describe the picture read at its own size or as a 3840x2160 master. The measurement runs only for native output, on VAAPI pictures (P010): a picture a second in the first 10 s, then one at a scene cut at most every 10 s and one every 30 s without a cut. A picture contradicts the offsets where more than half of the rows of a bar (columns, for a side bar) have content across its width, in at least 12 of the 16 strips of the measurement (6 of 8 for a side bar): a dirty edge, a dark scene, a logo, a line of subtitles or bright rows at the edge do not. It is the measurement of HDR10 measured as Dolby Vision, collected a frame later without waiting. Pictures decoded by the processor are not measured, and in profile 5 very dark content counts as black, so that it can only decide less often. A decision holds while the level 5 offsets keep their value, also when the renderer is made anew; other offsets start their own count. The measurement goes on at a low rate, and the offsets come back if half or fewer of the measured pictures contradict them.

**Display and HDMI**

- NVIDIA and limited range (`nvidia-legacy-open-9069` and `9070`, `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9058` and `9059`): where the RGB range of a head changes and its clock, raster and protocol do not, NVKMS now shuts the head down in an update of its own before it sets it again, as it already does for DisplayPort and FRL; set again in place, the head kept its old range. A flip that changes the HDR state of a head (a plane turned off, new HDR metadata of a plane and, with driver 615 on RTX 30 cards whose connectors are all HDMI, the start and end of HDR10) keeps the range that the mode set asked for. The kernel log says when a head is shut down first for its range, and when its range compression goes on or off. No report shows the fault with driver 615; it is done the same way there, so that both series behave alike.
- Outputs kept on for their sound: a sound card belongs to graphics when it is on the same PCI slot (NVIDIA, AMD) or when the Intel graphics are its supplier, and the output kept is the one whose EDID names the display that the ELD names (maker, product and name). Two displays of the same model are both kept. Where that is not certain (the audio engine has said nothing yet, the card belongs to no graphics that can be told, the display is not known or is on no output), every HDMI, DisplayPort or DVI output with a display stays on, of the graphics of the card if they are known, otherwise of all. An analogue, S/PDIF or USB output keeps nothing on. The turning off waits up to 30 s for the audio engine.
- On Kodi's graphics, an output kept on stays black with nothing on it, or as it was where the driver needs a plane (amdgpu); the driver is asked in a test commit first, and where it takes neither, the output goes off, so that the others still do, with a warning. A refused commit that turned off the outputs that Kodi does not use is asked for again 10 s later, three times at most for a mode; before, a refusal left them on, also without the file. An output of Kodi's graphics comes back only in a commit that changes a mode anyway, never with native Dolby Vision or a move, black or, on amdgpu, with a black picture of its own. Each entry of `hades-keep-outputs` that names no output is said once, with the names there are.
- HDR10 at 10 bits with driver 580: three buffers of AB30 (or XB30, then half floats where the half-float output is allowed), with the modifiers that the plane takes. Before use, four pixels are written with values that 8 bits cannot hold and read back from each, and the driver takes one in a test commit. A failure keeps 8 bits, and that display, mode and format are not tried again; a commit refused for the buffer puts the window back for the rest of the video (after three videos in a row, for the next seven). A frame-packed 3D mode stays at 8 bits, and native Dolby Vision at its 8. While the buffers are in use, the interface is drawn whole at each frame, and the 8-bit window stays allocated (about 100 MB at 3840x2160). `<video><hdrownbuffersalways>true</hdrownbuffersalways></video>` uses them with any driver. Tested on the host with a software renderer; the import of a real buffer and what the driver sends are not tried.

**Files and streams**

- A read of a local file that takes 1 s or more gives its time back to the deadline of the demuxer, 20 s for each packet; what the demuxer itself takes still counts, and a stop is never undone. A read of a played file that fails with EIO is tried again at the same byte after 0.1, 0.25, 0.5 and 1 s, then every 2 s, up to 15 s from the first error; not for a file that is not on a drive of this machine (network, FUSE, memory: said once), for another error, or for a drive that is gone (it left `/sys` or is offline), which is checked before each wait. Only reads for playback retry, also those of a Blu-ray or DVD image or folder on a local drive, which also count as local for the deadline; thumbnails, file details and other reads that are not playback fail at once, as before. A damaged sector now holds the picture for up to about 14 s before the video ends. Stop pressed during a retry wait ends it within a fraction of a second; Stop pressed while the kernel still waits for the drive keeps the interface still until the read returns, as before.

**Blu-ray**

- A join is kept when it is the next play item, reached by reading to the end of the one before; its bytes start where the playlist puts it; its connection condition is 5 or 6 (read with Kodi's own playlist parser); the play item before does not end in a still; it is not the second view of a 3D disc; and the streams that the playlist and the two clips present are the same (PIDs, codings, formats, rates, languages, dynamic range and the others). The kept play item goes on the timeline of the one that the demuxer opened with, and the times of the disc's menus are not moved.
- The events of the disc are taken by one thread at a time. A key could empty the event queue of libbluray while the player held an event (a title, a playlist, a play item, an angle, a seek or a still) and lose it; such an event is now left to the thread that reads. An event that comes with a key press, most often with BD-J, now opens the streams again as when it comes with a read; before, it was processed without.

**Boot and system**

- GRUB: `search --fs-uuid` for the identifier in the entry's `boot=`, with the partition that the menu was read from as hint. The installer of the image and the setting of the internal disks know the new lines, and the Windows installer writes the new menus with an update, as it always did. The update in place (`bootloader/update.sh`, run by the system that is updated, and only then) changes a menu only when it is, line by line, one of Hades as written, with the identifier of this start in its four entries; anything else is left as it is, with a line on the console of the update. The new menu is written to `grub.cfg.new`, flushed, read back and renamed, so that a power cut leaves one menu or the other. `syslinux.cfg` is not touched: in BIOS the kernel always came from the menu's own partition.
- `/opt` is an empty folder of the system, made with the other folders of its base layout.

**Diagnostics**

- `results.txt` of each snapshot: the power control and runtime state of each USB device; every USB controller (UHCI, OHCI, EHCI, xHCI and USB4) and every device of the `thunderbolt` driver, with their root hubs; the autosuspend of usbcore; and the USB4 lines of the kernel, who runs its connection manager, the security level of each domain and, of each router, its name, generation, authorisation, lanes and speed. No identifier of a router is read.
- The storage timeline of the shutdown (`io-timeline.txt.gz`): after the columns of `/storage`, each other disk with its reads, writes, times and requests in flight, on each line where one of them changed or a request is waiting, and a line whenever the disks, or those mounted under `/var/media`, change.

**Updates**

- LibreELEC `master` up to `ffcc494ea24a`: fixes to emmctool, and six unused Allwinner kernel patches dropped; neither is in the Generic image, which does not change with it.
- The `Piers` branch of Kodi up to `42848fff43c5`, 12 commits with their authors (the thirteenth, the clock of the data of audio visualisations, was in 1.0 RC6 already): HEIF and AVIF pictures stored as a grid of tiles composited whole and turned as the file says, the main picture of a HEIF file decoded, the border clamp of RetroPlayer's shaders on GLES, and changes to the builds of other systems and to a test, which the image does not build.

## 1.0 RC6

### What's new for you

- **Dolby Vision output (this video) applies while the video plays**, also for HDR10 videos ("Dolby Vision measured from the HDR10 picture") and HDR10+ videos ("Dolby Vision made from its HDR10+ metadata"), and is kept for each file, by a fingerprint of its path. Only the video stream is opened again, from the key frame before where it is, and the sound plays on; a button of the video OSD and any key with `PlayerControl(HadesDVOutput)` open the same choice. Where that cannot be done (a disc with its menus, a live stream, a paused video, among others), the whole player goes back to where it is, as when another video stream is chosen, and `kodi.log` says which of the two was done ([settings](docs/settings.md#in-the-player-video-settings)). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **Dolby Vision and HDR10+ in one video, as a choice** (*With Dolby Vision and HDR10+ in one video*, *System > Display*; Dolby Vision by default): for a video that carries both (profile 7 of a disc, and 8.1) on a display that takes both, "HDR10+" sends its base layer, the HDR10 picture, with its own HDR10+ metadata, picture by picture, and leaves its Dolby Vision aside; *Dolby Vision output (this video)* offers both for one video. `kodi.log` says what such a video is sent with, and why, also with the default. Idea from p3i (CoreELEC). *Not tried on a display yet.*
- **The content mapping v4.0 of Dolby Vision, as a setting** (*Dolby Vision content mapping v4.0*, Expert): "As in the video" (the default), "Add v4.0 where the video has none", two automatic choices made once per video (for a video mastered brighter than the TV, or for one whose metadata carry no trims of the colourist), and "Version 2.9 only", for a TV that shows a black or wrong picture with v4.0. The player information says what is sent. Ideas from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i), avdvplus and SamuriHL. *Not tried on a display yet.*
- **For projectors and displays that map HDR their own way** (options, off by default): Dolby Vision Low-Latency also to a TV that takes Dolby Vision Standard, and Low-Latency mapped to a peak, colours and a black of the display set by hand, with the black lift of ITU-R BT.2390 (measured with the software bridge); *HDR10: limit the luminance told to the display*, which changes what the HDR10 InfoFrame says and not the picture; and *HDR10 and HDR10+ adapted to the display*, which maps HDR10 and HDR10+ to the peak of the display in the player, always or only for a video brighter than the display, and gives its GPU time in `kodi.log` every 10 s ([settings](docs/settings.md#system--display--hdr)). Low-Latency as a choice and the luminance limit follow ideas from the CoreELEC builds of avdvplus and SamuriHL. *Not tried on a display yet.*
- **HDR10 through the HDMI port of Intel graphics of display version 9 (HD and UHD 6xx) goes out as Dolby Vision** where the TV takes Dolby Vision: that port cannot send HDR10 (the driver gives it no HDR metadata), and such a video was mapped to SDR. It now goes out as native Dolby Vision, with metadata measured from the picture as *HDR10 as Dolby Vision, measured* makes them, unless another output is chosen for the video. *Not tried on such graphics yet.*
- **HDR10+ sent as Dolby Vision measures what its HDR10+ metadata do not say.** A scene without statistics of its own (credits that come without HDR10+, 12 pictures in a row without it inside a scene) and a video whose HDR10+ metadata never carry any now get metadata measured from the picture, instead of those of the scene before and then HDR10. Measured on the host; *not tried on a display yet.*
- **Dolby Vision sent as HDR10+, version 1, carries DistributionY99 and DistributionY100nit** in its places of 5 % and 10 %, as the definitions quoted by quietvoid give them, measured in luminance on the GPU; the thresholds at which they change within a scene were measured on two films. Thanks to quietvoid for the definitions and to cinemaONE for measurements of a licensed player. *Not tried on a display yet.*
- **Menus over Dolby Vision cost less GPU time on weaker graphics** (issue #12). On graphics that present a frame ahead, the packer of the transport composites the GUI from its texture in YCbCr, without the full-precision picture under it; the rows of 4:2:2 chroma come from a compute pass that reads each source row once for a tile of rows; and on AMD graphics whose driver has power profiles, the GPU runs its COMPUTE profile while Dolby Vision draws, with its clock in `kodi.log`. The same bytes, measured with the software bridge. Ideas from YBLOD (dangerouslaser) and big-fella. *Not measured on hardware yet.*
- **Dolby Vision after a long *Delay after change of refresh rate*.** The Dolby Vision signal set up with the change of mode of a video now waits for the video 10 s from the moment the display is back, and no longer from the change itself, which a long delay used up; a wait that runs out while the video still opens is no longer counted as a refusal. Tested on the host; *not tried on a display yet.*
- **Dolby Vision in two tracks whose times are a constant apart** (a file with its enhancement layer in a second track) is now paired, with the distance learnt at a picture where both tracks can start, also at a later one. Tested on the host; no real file with such a distance is known here.
- **The IPT tunnel, which carries profiles 7 and 8 by default since 1.0 RC5, is more exact:** computed without cancellation in 32-bit floats, it is at most one code from a computation in 64-bit floats in at most 0.04 % of the samples, where up to 3.6 % were. Measured with the software bridge.
- **Picture subtitles can be resized, moved and dimmed** (PGS of Blu-ray and VobSub of DVD; *Player > Subtitles > Image based subtitles*): a zoom from 10 to 200 % and the position of text subtitles applied to them, for example into the picture of a 2.39:1 film whose subtitles were made for 16:9, with keymap actions for both, from the pull request 29060 of cinemaONE to Kodi; and a brightness from 10 to 100 % that scales their light, the same on SDR, HDR10 and Dolby Vision. With the defaults nothing changes, and disc menus are never changed ([settings](docs/settings.md#player--subtitles--image-based-subtitles)). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **Text subtitles after a seek.** The line that is on screen at the target of a seek is shown there (SRT, ASS, WebVTT and the other text subtitles of a file), instead of being lost until the next line: a line read before the seek comes back, and in a Matroska file a line that started before the place the demuxer reads from is found where the cues of the file point or, where the file has no cues for that track, by reading the clusters just before it once playback has resumed. That is done on a thread of its own at the lowest priority, a few hundred bytes for each line, over HTTP by byte ranges, and never holds the picture or the sound back. Measured on files made for the test, with 203 lines on screen at the targets of the seeks: 103 of them were lost before; with the cues every one is found, in 1 to 4 reads of 4 KiB. Idea from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **Editions of Matroska files**, as an option (off by default): with *Ask which edition when a file has several* (*Player > Videos*), a file that holds several editions of a film, such as the theatrical cut and the extended cut, asks which one to play. An ordered edition plays only its parts, the chapters are those of the edition, and resume points and bookmarks keep their edition. To find the editions, a file on this device, on the local network or over HTTP or HTTPS is read before playback, and over HTTP only the parts that describe them, by byte ranges, with a busy dialog that can be cancelled. Ideas from Sebastiii and from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **A picture with a damaged timestamp no longer stops playback.** One picture timed far ahead held every picture queued behind it until the clock reached its time; it is now left out once the pictures after it are due, and `kodi.log` says so. Idea from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **A TV that is switched off and on gets its picture back** in an HDMI mode above 340 MHz, such as 3840x2160 at 50 or 60 Hz, or one that goes by FRL (issue #11). On NVIDIA, after such a hot-plug event Kodi reads the SCDC registers of the display through the DDC port of that connector and, where the display lost the setup of its link, turns the output off and on once, so that the driver sets it up again; `<video><hotplugrelink>false</hotplugrelink></video>` turns this off. On AMD the kernel now does the same where it did not see the TV leave. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **Intel and AMD: a TV that drops its hot-plug signal for a moment** (some TVs do when Dolby Vision starts or stops) is not reported as unplugged for up to 3 s, and on Intel the SCDC of the TV is read again for 4 s after it comes back; Intel graphics tell the TV that the source is a player ("Digital STB") and not a PC; and on AMD Ryzen with Vega graphics (DCN 1), where the output is YCbCr (HDR at 3840x2160 above 30 Hz), the background where nothing covers the screen, such as the bars of a film, is black and not green. A kernel parameter turns each of them off ([settings](docs/settings.md#outside-the-settings-screens)). From the kernel patches of big-fella in YBLOD 0.3.3. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **Limited colour range is compressed once, by the driver, on Intel, AMD and NVIDIA.** With *Use limited colour range (16-235)* on, Kodi compressed its own picture and the Intel driver compressed it again at every TV mode (raised blacks), as the AMD driver did for a TV that cannot select the RGB range, and both in YCbCr. Kodi now asks the driver for limited range through its "Broadcast RGB" property, which nvidia-drm now has too, and draws full range; the AVI InfoFrame says limited range where the TV can be told. Native Dolby Vision is not affected. Turning the setting on or off sets the picture of the TV up again (a moment of black), and `kodi.log` says who compresses ([settings](docs/settings.md#kodis-own-settings-that-matter-here)). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **NVIDIA: Dolby Vision in the modes that the driver names by their HDMI VIC** (3840x2160 at 24, 25 and 30 Hz and 4096x2160 at 24 Hz, where the display does not declare them as UHD VICs): the AVI InfoFrame now names the mode by its CTA VIC or, where the display does not take that, the vendor InfoFrame of the HDMI VIC goes in a free slot beside the Dolby one. With driver 580 (GeForce GTX 900 and GTX 10 cards and the TITAN V), Dolby Vision is no longer refused in those modes. The kernel log says each InfoFrame that a head puts on the cable (issue #4). *Not tried on such a display yet.*
- **NVIDIA: the window of Kodi has three buffers** (two where the driver refuses three), so that native Dolby Vision can be presented a frame ahead there too; on nvidia-drm that is done only where frames shown the usual way miss their refreshes again and again, as measured. The window takes one more picture of its size of graphics memory (33 MB at 4K), and `kodi.log` says how many buffers it lets Kodi hold. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **An HDMI port that NVIDIA drives through a DisplayPort output** (issue #4, HP ZBook 17 G6): a change of resolution, such as the menus at 1920x1080 at 120 Hz to a video at 3840x2160, now goes out with the output turned off and on too, so that the TV takes the new mode whole. *Not tried on that laptop yet.*
- **A change of mode that the driver refused is asked for again.** The display no longer stays in the mode before while Kodi draws for the new one until the next change: the new mode is asked for again with the next frames and then every 2 s, six times in all, and `kodi.log` says each step. Idea from CroqueMr. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **HDR ends after a video even when the driver refuses it with a change of resolution.** If the driver refused the commit that carried the end of HDR with the change of mode, the interface kept the HDR signal, its colorimetry and HDR10+ until the next video; the end is now asked for again on its own, once the mode is out, and `kodi.log` says so. Looked at after a fix in CroqueMr's build (R1.0.0 RC3 Preview). *Not tried on a display yet.*
- **Whole-number multiples of the frame rate**, as an option under the whitelist (off by default): where the whitelist has no mode at the exact frame rate of a video, the display stays in a whitelisted mode at a whole-number multiple of it (23.976 fps at 119.88 Hz, 24 fps at 120 Hz, 25 fps at 100 Hz), without a change of mode and without judder; for SDR, and for HDR10 only where the graphics driver has said that the link carries 10 bits in that mode. Never for Dolby Vision, HDR10+, HLG or interlaced video. Idea from CoreELEC. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **An EDID with an extension block that is not valid:** that block is skipped and copies of the Dolby Vision block count as one, the same in Kodi and in the kernel; on Intel, HDR10+ is read from the whole EDID (after an idea of CroqueMr); and `kodi.log` gives the shape of the EDID where Dolby Vision is not taken for it. Tested on 614 EDIDs, whose verdicts do not change, and 2,830,720 mutated ones; *not tried on such a display yet.*
- **The player information says more of the sound**: the bed and the objects of Dolby Atmos (TrueHD, E-AC-3) and the dialogue normalisation (AC-3, E-AC-3, DTS) on the overview page, the layout of a stream that is passed through instead of "RAW", and why "Sync playback to display" decodes the sound to PCM. The Dolby Vision page says only what the overview does not, and skins get labels with the names of the CoreELEC builds (`Player.Process(video.dovi.profile)` and others), so that skins made for them show their values. Ideas from avdvplus.
- **`kodi.log` says whether the sound of a stream passes through, and why not**: the setting that is off, "Sync playback to display", a live stream, or what the display or receiver takes. A report of "no Atmos" can be answered from the log. Idea from CroqueMr.
- **Dolby Digital: a seek no longer turns off its dynamic range compression** until the next video (a fix of FFmpeg by Jun Zhao), and the decoder of AC-3 and E-AC-3 no longer spoils a block of 256 samples of some channels once in a stream (measured with the samples of FATE).
- **Dialogue normalisation at 0 dB in passthrough**, as an option (off by default): AC-3, E-AC-3 and DTS that are passed through tell the receiver not to lower the sound, so it plays as mastered and the volume is set on the receiver. Nothing else of the sound changes, and every frame rewritten is checked again; Dolby Atmos in E-AC-3, TrueHD and a few forms of DTS are left as they are, and `kodi.log` says why. Ideas from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i) and of SamuriHL. *Not tried with a receiver yet.*
- **Dynamic range compression of Dolby Digital in *System > Audio*** (100 % by default, as Kodi), applied in the decoder that plays, from its next frame, with no sound lost; and **the downmix settings that Kodi lacks**: the level of the centre channel (the dialogue) in steps of 0.5 dB, whether the centre level that the track asks for is used, and "Include LFE when downmixing" up to 300 %. With the defaults the mix is that of Kodi, bit for bit, and sound that is passed through or plays on as many speakers as it has does not change. Ideas from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **Audio settings changed during playback apply at once**: passthrough and each of its formats, the DTS-HD core, stereo upmix and the LFE in a downmix. Idea from the CoreELEC builds of pannal (p3i). *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **The debug information of the player (ctrl+shift+o) no longer moves the subtitles back** after they were moved by hand, and the player information names the dialogue normalisation by what it is: "Dialogue level" in dBFS for AC-3 and E-AC-3, "Dialogue gain" in dB for DTS. The first after a fix of pannal in p3i.
- **The interface:** a dialog that does not finish closing no longer leaves Kodi ignoring the remote and the keyboard (issue #11): 3 s after the first action it drops, it is closed, and `kodi.log` says so; while a video plays, the settings window no longer saves the mode of the video as the resolution of the interface; only the items that change are drawn again when the focus moves in a list (Markus Härer, from the master of Kodi); and making a file a version or an extra of a video reads it with the busy dialog, without holding the interface. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **The library tells FEL from MEL by the rule that draws them**, read from the RPUs of a profile 7 file when it is scanned, and keeps it where Kodi keeps it, so that a library shared over MySQL with other Kodi 22 systems is not changed. Skins made for the CoreELEC of p3i show the Dolby Vision profile (`ListItem.DvProfile`, `ListItem.HdrTypeAlt`), and the video information of Estuary says, for example, "Dolby Vision P7.6 FEL / HDR10+". Names from p3i (pannal, vlix).
- **CEC with a Pulse-Eight adapter.** Hades no longer takes the input of the TV back after you switch to another device; an AV receiver that powers down with the TV no longer wakes it; a TV that refused *Image View On* once is woken with *Power On Function*; and switching the TV to the AV receiver that Hades is connected through no longer pauses or stops the video. Three fixes from the CoreELEC of pannal (p3i), by CoreELEC, pannal and Stuart Marks, and one of Hades. *Not tried with an adapter yet.*
- **Blu-ray playlists played directly keep one timeline across their seamless joins.** A film whose playlist joins clips timed apart (as releases with several cuts do) stepped its timestamps by a fraction of a second at each join: the picture froze and passthrough audio, TrueHD included, paused for the step, or pictures were dropped and the sound skipped. Every packet now goes on the timeline of the playlist, from the play item it was read in. After a design of matthane for CoreELEC; the joins of a disc measured by SamuriHL. Measured on the host with discs made for the test; *not tried with a disc yet.*
- **Blu-ray menus are kept apart from subtitles** in the player, and across its flushes, so that changing the subtitles or a flush no longer takes a menu away. From 78andyp, in the master of Kodi. *Not tried with a disc yet.*
- **Simulate a 3D display**, as an option (off by default), for Blu-ray 3D discs that stop on a black screen on a display without 3D: the disc is told that the player and the display show 3D, and the film plays in 2D, its base view ([settings](docs/settings.md#player--discs)). Idea from SamuriHL. *Not tried with a disc yet.*
- **3D subtitles: a stereoscopic subtitle stream keeps its depth**, and the menus of a 3D disc are no longer moved by the depth of the subtitles (they keep the depth setting, as before 1.0 RC5); `kodi.log` says which depth sequence the subtitles follow, or why none. Idea from p3i (pannal). *Not seen on a 3D display yet.*
- **Movies on discs in the library** (Blu-ray and DVD folders and images): a movie is no longer deleted when its disc is scanned again, the discs of a movie are one movie, and a refresh keeps what the movie had (from the pull request 30199 of 78andyp to Kodi); and the information dialog updates only the movie it changed, without a race of the thumbnail loader (from the pull requests of olympia). *Not tried with a library of discs yet.*
- **Blu-ray Java (BD-J) menus with nothing to install, in a service of their own.** The Java menus of a disc now run in the BD-J service of the system, never in Kodi: a process for each disc, started by the system under a user of its own, without privileges, with a filter of system calls and no network, which reads the disc only through Kodi and ends with it. The Java runtime comes with the system (OpenJDK 27.0.1.0.3, built for the image, 28 MB of the image), and no Java of a disc can start in any process that the system does not isolate. The add-on "JRE for BD-J menus" is no longer used, and what discs saved before is not carried over. Where the menus cannot run, the main title plays and a notification says why, and `kodi.log` now has the lines of the BD-J service that say why the Java of a disc stopped or waited ([settings](docs/settings.md#player--discs)). Tested on the host with discs made for the tests; *not tried with a commercial disc yet.* The idea of Java menus with nothing to install comes from CroqueMr's build (R1.0.0 RC3 Preview).
- **BD-J menus that play as their discs expect**, with fixes in libbluray: discs with BD+ that stayed black, and a JAR whose signature cannot be verified, which is now used as unsigned (both from Daniel Oberlin, in libbluray); the size of 2160p video, the list of each kind of stream of a play item, and a language choice that returns once the stream is selected (from SamuriHL, the first with skull-01); the HAVi background plane, a BD-J screen that plays no playlist, and timing that the disc is told when the picture gets to the screen (from SamuriHL, with the parts that Kodi and the BD-J service needed); and the height of the video area and the size of picture-in-picture. *Not tried with a commercial disc yet.*
- **BD-Live, as an option (off by default).** *BD-Live: Internet for disc menus*, in *Player > Discs*, lets the Java menus of a disc reach the Internet, as BD-Live discs do for trailers and extras, and only the Internet: IPv4 addresses, never this system, your home network, the other networks this system is on or any other local address, with the names that the disc asks for resolved by Kodi. The system, the filter of system calls of the service and the Java part of libbluray each hold that rule, and `kodi.log` lists each name and connection allowed or refused. Off, the disc has no network at all. Tested on the host in networks made for the tests; *not tried with a BD-Live disc yet.*
- **A new boot menu.** Instead of the text menu, which had the installer first and a wait of 5 s, the boot menu is drawn for LibreELEC Hades: the eclipse of its mark large on the right, on black, and in a column on the left the four entries, each with a line that says what it does: *Run* (recommended: the normal start, with the other NVMe and SATA disks of the computer hidden), *Run (internal disks read-only)*, *Live* (settings and add-ons kept in memory only, gone at power off) and *Installer* (installs on a disk you choose, erasing it, after asking twice). *Run* is first and starts by itself after 10 s, with a countdown. In UEFI it is drawn in the mode that the screen is already in, with sizes from 1024x768 to 3840x2160, and the kernel starts in the same mode as before; in BIOS it has the same look at 1024x768. The MBR code is built from its source, byte for byte the one of the release. Seen in a virtual machine at several resolutions; *not tried on hardware yet.*
- **The start screen leaves room for a notice.** The mark and the name stand a little higher on the screens shown while the system starts and Kodi loads; where several displays are connected, the notice that Kodi uses one of them is now a picture on the start screen, in English and in Spanish, instead of text on a black screen. *Not tried on hardware yet.*
- **The diagnostics filter more of what is personal.** The names of drives, network shares, folders and files in the paths that the logs write (`/var/media/...`, `smb://...`, `bluray://...`, mount tables, labels) are now markers, the same name always the same marker, and the structure of a disc (`BDMV/STREAM/00001.m2ts`) is kept. With the diagnostic switch on, the journal of the start before is kept too (`previous-boot`, after an idea of CroqueMr). The EDID files of earlier starts are no longer damaged when they are filtered again ([diagnostics](docs/diagnostics.md)).
- **The firewall is no longer left open by a configuration it does not understand.** A value of `RULES` in `iptables.conf` other than none, public, home or custom (an empty or cut file, a line end of Windows) loaded no rules at all; it now gets the rules of a home network, and the journal says so. The network page of the LibreELEC settings opens with such a file, and the diagnostics keep it.
- **The Dolby Vision module of Kodi is GPL-2.0-or-later,** all 42 of its files (`tools/dvbridge`): its parts that came from other code are written again from the public specifications, with the same bytes and decisions, measured on the host. Two files of Kodi outside it, `DVBridgeState.cpp` and `DVBridgeState.h`, stay GPL-3.0-or-later; README and NOTICE name the licence of each file.
- **Up to date and safer:** Linux 7.3.1, with 47 fixes queued for 7.3.2 (io_uring, 14 known security issues among them, and a panic of the MediaTek MT7925 Wi-Fi at restart); LibreELEC at its latest commit; the `Piers` branch of Kodi up to 846211c81d20 (among its fixes, accurate seeks, the commercial breaks of EDL files skipped by the latency of the display, and embedded subtitles shown without a seek back) and 15 commits of its `master`; libplacebo 29a56f9290ba; FFmpeg with the fixes of its 9.0 branch, fixes for five known security issues in five of its decoders and one in its IMF demuxer; expat 2.10.0, libarchive 3.8.11 and nghttp2 1.71.0; and fixes from the branches of OpenSSL, curl, OpenSSH, BlueZ, glibc, Python, iptables and libdrm after their releases.

### Details

**Dolby Vision picture and metadata**

- Content mapping v4.0 (`videoscreen.hadesdvcm40`): "Add v4.0 where the video has none" adds the neutral part that dovi_tool writes by default, as `<video><dvcm40default>` does. "Automatic" adds it where the first RPU of a video has no v4.0 part and its `source_max_pq` is above the peak in the Dolby Vision block of the display (never where that peak is not known); "Automatic 2: no trims in the video" where that RPU carries no level 2 block, the condition under which no trim of the colourist is set aside. The decision is made with the first RPU of a video and kept for that video, also when the native output starts again; the menus never decide. A later frame that brings what the decision was made without ends the addition for the rest of the stream, once, and `kodi.log` says so. Measured on the host: the test of the serializer from 1.0 RC4 gives the same 255 payloads as before with the defaults.
- The automatic choice of the content mapping v4.0 is made by the video that a picture belongs to: a picture of the video before, drawn after the next one was opened, no longer decides for it. Measured on the host.
- Low-Latency: "Low-Latency also where the TV takes Standard" sends it where the Dolby Vision block of the display offers both; "peak and colours set here" maps it to the values set (40 to 4000 cd/m²; BT.2020, DCI-P3 or BT.709), and the black of the display (0 to 0.5 cd/m²) is lifted as in ITU-R BT.2390 on R'G'B' in PQ after the tone mapping, inside the picture only. With a black of 0 the bytes are those of 1.0 RC5, measured with the software bridge on four profile 7 FEL pictures. Whether BT.2390 takes the lift on each component of R'G'B' rather than on the intensity is not verified.
- HDR10 limited (`videoscreen.hadeshdr10limiton`, `videoscreen.hadeshdr10limit`): the peak of the mastering display, MaxCLL and MaxFALL of the HDR10 InfoFrame at most the value set, from 100 to 4000 cd/m²; the picture and the dynamic metadata of HDR10+ do not change.
- HDR10 and HDR10+ adapted to the display (`videoscreen.hadeshdr10adapt`): the video goes through the Dolby Vision engine with the mapping that it already makes for HDR10 on a display without HDR (BT.2390 guided by the static metadata, perceptual gamut mapping), toward an HDR10 target with the peak of the display; "Automatic" only where the curve has something to compress, decided once per video, and published only once it is written. Where it cannot map (no VAAPI decoding, no mapping, an output without HDR10, or 48 pictures in a row not mapped) the video goes out as HDR10, as mastered. Tested on the host (19 checks and 10 mutations, also with ThreadSanitizer); the drawing with an HDR10 target is not tried on hardware yet.
- HDR10 as Dolby Vision where the output cannot send HDR10 (its connector has no `HDR_OUTPUT_METADATA`, as the HDMI of display version 9 in Linux 7.3, or the display declares no HDR10): only with no other output chosen for the video. `<video><dvfromhdr10whenneeded>false</dvfromhdr10whenneeded></video>` turns it off for a comparison.
- HDR10+ sent as Dolby Vision: 12 pictures in a row without HDR10+ in a scene of HDR10+ go to measured metadata once, with a scene refresh, measured from the first of them; a stretch of eleven, or two of six with HDR10+ between them, keep the metadata of the scene. A stream whose HDR10+ gives no statistics in any of the 48 access units that the probe reads goes out as Dolby Vision 8.1 measured from the picture. Where the measurement cannot be made on the GPU, nothing is filled and it goes out as before. `<video><dvhdr10plusfill>false</dvhdr10plusfill></video>` turns the filling off. Measured on the host (37 checks).
- Dolby Vision sent as HDR10+, version 1: the GPU gives each sample its maxRGB and its luminance; a scene keeps the highest 99.99th percentile of its pictures (DistributionY99, in units of 16 cd/m²) and the average share of samples at 100 cd/m² or less (DistributionY100nit). Version 0 keeps its nine percentiles and its decisions: 3000 pictures in 40 scenes give the same lines as before. The thresholds of change of DistributionY100nit within a scene (2 and 10 points) were measured on an open test film and a profile 7 FEL test film against the ideal of each scene. A first picture of a scene that comes while the output changes mode is counted and told as such in `kodi.log`.
- The IPT tunnel is computed without cancellation in 32-bit floats: against 64-bit floats, one code apart at most, in at most 0.04 % of the samples, where up to 3.6 % were. YCbCr and profile 5 give the same bytes. Measured with the software bridge.
- The Dolby Vision signal set up with the change of mode of a video waits for the video 10 s from the moment the display is back, and no longer from the commit (a long "Delay after change of refresh rate" made it run out); a time that runs out while the video still opens no longer counts as a refusal. Tested on the host.

**Performance**

- The GUI composited by the packer (`<video><dvguiinplace>`, on): for a frame presented a frame ahead whose transport is YCbCr (profiles 7 and 8 as composed, also at the last level of the fidelity step-down), the packer reads the GUI texture where the composite of Kodi reads it and does its arithmetic, so that the full-precision picture of 3840x2160 under the GUI is neither written nor read. Only where the GUI composite of the graphics converts exactly. Measured with the software bridge, with the composite of Kodi itself and a GUI like that of the player information: 0 bytes apart, also under the GUI, and 3 of 3 mutations caught. Idea from YBLOD (dangerouslaser).
- The rows of 4:2:2 chroma by a compute pass with a tile shared by its rows (`<video><dvcomputerows>`, on, OpenGL ES 3.1): 28 of 28 files the same bytes as the fragment pass, about 16 to 22 million texture reads fewer for a 4K picture. Where the compute pass fails on the graphics, the fragment pass takes over from that frame on, and `kodi.log` says it once. Idea from YBLOD (dangerouslaser).
- The COMPUTE power profile of amdgpu while the Dolby Vision engine draws (`<video><dvgpuprofile>`, on), where the driver has profiles and nobody else chose one, undone 3 s after the last frame, also after a Kodi that ended without undoing it; and, where the driver has none (Rembrandt, Raphael, Phoenix, Strix), the performance level "high" as an option (`<video><dvgpuforcehigh>`, off). The 10-second lines of `kodi.log` give the average clock of the GPU. Tested on the host (304 checks). Idea and measurement from big-fella, taken up by YBLOD.
- Studied and not taken: Vulkan for the Dolby Vision engine (the shader compilers are the same as with OpenGL ES, and what makes a Dolby Vision engine faster does not depend on the API and is in this version), and QMS on Intel (`linux-9956` only says in the kernel log whether a display announces it).

**Display and HDMI**

- After a hot-plug event on nvidia-drm, the output is set up again only where the link needs it, read from `NV_OUTPUT_LINK` (new): TMDS with scrambling (SCDC `TMDS_Config`), FRL (`FRL_Rate`); TMDS without scrambling and DisplayPort are left alone. The SCDC is read on a thread of its own, through the adapter of the DDC port of that connector (`NV_DDC_I2C_PORT`, new), checked against its EDID, and no other bus. Not during native Dolby Vision, nor twice in 30 s. `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9057` and `nvidia-legacy-open-9068` give the two properties. Tested on the host.
- AMD (`linux-9964`): after a hot-plug event with the same display on an HDMI link with scrambling, amdgpu reads `SCDC_TMDS_CONFIG` and, where it is not what it wrote, sets the link up again as for a display plugged in, at most once every 30 s for a connector; never on FRL or DisplayPort. The Dolby Vision tunnel comes back as it was.
- `Broadcast RGB` in nvidia-drm (`nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9056`, `nvidia-legacy-open-9067`): "Limited 16:235" makes the head compress RGB and the AVI InfoFrame say limited range (with QS) or the default (without); never with the Dolby Vision tunnel or the HDMI 1.4b signalling; a change of it is a mode set. Kodi asks for it only in a commit that allows a mode set.
- NVIDIA and the HDMI VIC: `nvidia-open-kernel-modules-9053`, `nvidia-legacy-open-9064` (the CTA VIC in the AVI InfoFrame with the Dolby vendor InfoFrame, where the display takes it: HDMI 2.0 with SCDC and the VIC listed), `9055` and `9066` (otherwise the vendor InfoFrame of the HDMI VIC in a free slot: the generic one, or slot 9 on RTX 50; without a free slot, VIC 0 as before, and the kernel log says what holds the slot), `9054` and `9065` (each InfoFrame that a head puts on the cable, bounded). Tested on the host with the code of the driver (498,402 checks for 615, 101,729 for 580).
- egl-gbm (`nvidia_egl-gbm-9001`): the stream of each window with a FIFO of three, and two where the driver refuses it.
- The EDID: an extension block that is not valid is skipped, copies of the Dolby Vision block count as one, and one that is seen and not taken makes the verdict "invalid", in Kodi and in the kernel (`linux-9984`, `9987`, `9991`, `9994`); the atomic check of i915 reads HDR10+ from the whole EDID (`linux-9999`, after an idea of CroqueMr). `kodi.log` gives the shape of the EDID where Dolby Vision is not taken for it, and a connector whose EDID was read in part is probed once more. Tested on 614 EDIDs, whose verdicts do not change, and 2,830,720 mutated ones.
- A framebuffer for a buffer made without modifiers is given none, in the view of native Dolby Vision, of a DRM PRIME picture and of the GUI: the kernel refused it. After a fix of YBLOD.
- A change of resolution on a dual-mode DisplayPort connector of nvidia-drm is decided by the mode that is on the output, not the one selected, and `kodi.log` says how long the output was off and the pixel clocks before and after.
- On such a connector, leaving native Dolby Vision toward menus of another size restores the output with the first frame of the new window, with the head turned off and on once, not twice. Tested on the host.
- `kodi.log` and the kernel log: `linux-9956` (whether an HDMI display announces QMS, Intel), `linux-9970` (what the HPD debounce of amdgpu held, without debugging), and the HPD lines of `linux-9962`.

**Files and streams**

- Dolby Vision in two tracks: two tracks whose decoding times are a constant apart are paired, with the offset learnt at a random-access picture of both, also at a later one; and the enhancement packets dropped right after a seek are counted apart from the others. Tested on the host; no real file with such an offset is known here.
- Matroska editions: `ffmpeg-9940` exports the editions and their chapters in an option that only reads; what is demuxed does not change. Over HTTP, the first request is a range of 64 KiB, and only what the SeekHead points to follows, 1 MiB at most, with the session of curl that the player then takes over; a server that answers with the whole file is cut after its headers.
- Matroska editions: nothing is looked up by DNS on the thread of the interface before the question, the messages of the playback before wait while it is asked, and a cut of an `.edl` file that is the same as one of the edition is no longer lost when a saved position is placed with another edition.
- IMF (`ffmpeg-9934`): a package keeps only the assets whose path is inside its folder (CVE-2026-120210); one that points elsewhere no longer opens.

**Sound**

- Dialogue normalisation at 0 dB: AC-3 (dialnorm and dialnorm2, with crc1 computed again), E-AC-3 (every substream, with crc2), and the DTS core with encoder version 7 (no CRC covers that field; nothing of the extension substream of DTS-HD is touched). Tested on the host with the samples of FATE through the stream parser of Kodi: the CRC checks of FFmpeg say the same of the rewritten streams as of the originals, and the decoded sound is identical bit for bit. Not verified with a receiver.
- `ffmpeg-9945` (from the `master` of FFmpeg, by Jun Zhao): a flush, at every seek, no longer resets the dynamic range compression of AC-3 and E-AC-3. `ffmpeg-9946`: the decoder of AC-3 and E-AC-3 no longer spoils one block of 256 samples at its first block switch, in some channels only; one frame changes in the stereo and 4.0 samples of FATE and everything else is identical bit for bit, and the patch moves the three FATE references that were made with the defect.
- Audio settings during playback: no player is a callback of the settings any more, so a setting changed on another thread as a video ends cannot reach a player that is gone (one listener for the process counts the changes; tested on the host with AddressSanitizer and ThreadSanitizer), and a setting changed between two files of a list no longer opens the decoder of the next one once more.
- ALSA: where HDMI gives a map of two channels without names, stereo takes the front left and right (`kodi.log` says so).

**Blu-ray and BD-J**

- The BD-J service: `libbluray-9050` to `9053` (no JVM in a process that is not isolated: another user, no capabilities, `no_new_privs`, a filter of system calls, no network but loopback; from Java 24, without a security manager only there, and no native access for the disc), `9060` to `9063` (its protocol, the client in Kodi and the host), `9070` to `9074`, the package `hades-bdj` and `openjdk`. Kodi gives the service the disc through paths that it checks, and a link that leads out of a disc folder on this system is not followed.
- The XML of a disc (the manifest of an update, the index of its fonts) is read with no external entity, DTD or XInclude (`libbluray-9083`); an index of fonts that names another DTD is now read.
- BD-Live (`libbluray-9080` to `9082`, `hades-bdj`): the service with a network to the Internet only, the names resolved by Kodi, at most 4 at a time and 20 a second, the Java sockets checked after the name is resolved.
- What a disc writes to `kodi.log` has a cap for each disc, with a count of what was left out, and the Java check of a connection reads the networks this system is on at each connection (`libbluray-9084`, `9085`).
- The player waits on the BD-J service for a limited time only: 5 s for each message it sends, five times its time for a call, 30 s for the greeting; when one runs out the service is ended, the disc goes on as when BD-J does not start, and `kodi.log` says so once (`libbluray-9086`).
- The BD-J service tells the system that it is ready only after its greeting, and none outlives its player: one that does not greet within 30 s is killed by the system, and one whose player is gone ends within 5 s, with everything that its disc started (`libbluray-9087`).
